      NotImplementedError when reached (decision 4's sentinel unwinding is
      still not built); `include?`/`member?`/`key?`/`has_key?` of a `T?` is
      false for nil; an overload chosen by argument class (decision 12)
      sees `T?` as `T`; and `x << a << b` on a local rebinds it twice.
    - **Compiler, for all code:** `class X < Struct.new(:a)` (a hidden
      value class as the superclass; program code types the members with
      `#: [A]` on the class line); `@x &&= v`; a bare `raise` in a rescue
      clause re-raises; `raise C, msg, backtrace`; `raise e.class, msg` is
      `raise e.exception(msg)`, a copy of `e` (MRI calls `e.class.new`, so a
      subclass's own initialize is not rerun); a `cause:` keyword is dropped
      and the lexical cause (decision 103) stands; raising a constant that
      aliases a class (`ParamsTooDeepError = QueryLimitError`); `klass.new`
      on a `singleton(HashSubclass)` (decision 165); `deprecate_constant`
      is a no-op, as MRI's warning is off by default. Dynamic calls route an
      argument count to its `__<name>_<n>` overload (`rack.input.read(n)`)
      and `call` on a Proc held untyped to a type switch over the program's
      Proc types (`rbDynProcCall`; procs taking `self` or a rest parameter
      are not covered).
    - **Prelude:** `String#index(s, start)`, `String#[re]`/`[re, n]`,
      `String#split(re, limit)`, `URI.decode_www_form_component(s, enc)`,
      `Tempfile.new([prefix, suffix])`, `Exception#exception`,
      `Errno::EMFILE`, `Encoding::ISO_2022_JP` (a dummy encoding, left out
      of `Encoding.list`).
    - **Sigs:** `Rack::QueryParser`'s parsers and `@params_class`,
      `Rack::MediaType`, three `Multipart::Parser` constants and helpers;
      `Cuba#req` is a `Rack::Request`, so routes call it statically.
    - **Generated code:** `if a; elsif b; end` with no else is Go's
      `else if` (gocritic's elseif; a longer chain stays nested, which its
      ifElseChain would flag), and `!x || y` no longer negates twice.
    - **Not yet:** multipart/form-data bodies compile but are untested; the
      rackup handler is still the prelude copy (#93; done in decision 176).
    - **Proof:** example 116 equal to MRI; `TestBootAutoload`
      (`demo/gemish.rb`'s lenient cases, `demo/config.rb`'s load-time
      state, `demo/unnamed.rb` never compiled); minitest
      `StringRackTest`, `RackStdlibTest`, `ControlReraiseTest`,
      `EncodingDummyTest`, `ObjectStructSuperTest`, `HashSubclassNewTest`,
      `DynamicOverloadAndProcTest`; `strings.txtar`'s `index_offset` and
      `objects.txtar`'s `class_lt_struct_new` are gone, and `control.txtar`'s
      bare-raise and three-argument raise cases now
      cover a raise outside a rescue and four arguments.
176. The real rackup gem (#93; supersedes 174's prelude copy). `require
    "rackup"` compiles rackup 2.3's own unmodified files through the gem
    path (decisions 169, 172, 173): `prelude/rackup.rb` and
    `prelude/go/rackup.go` are deleted and `rackup` left `libs.go`.
    Nothing of rackup is kept in the prelude; WEBrick still is (decision
    27's server over `net/http`: the gem sits on sockets and threads).
    The Ruby-visible API is 174's (`run` options, the yielded server,
    `server.config[:Port]`, `shutdown`); the server is now the gem's own
    `Rackup::Handler::WEBrick::Server`, a `WEBrick::HTTPServer` subclass.
    - **WEBrick surface the handler reaches.** `HTTPServer` is a struct
      class over a Go `Handle__` (listener, mux) instead of a `@go_type`,
      so it can be subclassed; every request runs `service(req, res)`,
      whose default looks the path up in the mount table (NotFound when
      nothing is mounted), so the gem's `Server#service` bypasses it as on
      MRI. `mount`'s servlet comes from `AbstractServlet.get_instance`.
      `HTTPRequest#meta_vars` (WEBrick's keys, `GATEWAY_INTERFACE`,
      `REMOTE_HOST` and an absolute `REQUEST_URI` included; `HTTP_*`
      sorted; no `SERVER_SOFTWARE`), `#body { |chunk| }` (one chunk:
      net/http has read it), `#unparsed_uri`, `#request_uri`.
      `HTTPResponse#body=` takes untyped and raises NotImplementedError for
      anything but a String (IO and streaming bodies, 174's "not yet"),
      `#cookies` is `Array[untyped]` (Cookies or Strings, each `to_s`'d),
      `#upgrade!` sets the headers.
    - **Pruning (169), sharper.** A constant resolves lexically in the
      walk's scope, so `Server` in `Handler::WEBrick.run` is that Server,
      not `Rackup::Server`, whose CLI code would otherwise be reached; a
      body's receiverless call to its class's own singleton method makes
      the class live (`register :webrick, WEBrick`); a computed
      `const_get` makes the receiver's nested classes live (every class
      off `self`) instead of stopping pruning (`Handler.[]`); an
      effect-free gem constant initializer (a literal, lambda, Array of
      them, `.freeze`, a regexp interpolating constants) is walked only
      once its name is reached and is dropped otherwise (Rack::Lint's
      `HOST_PATTERN`, which RE2 cannot express); a pruned class's
      `def_delegators` go with it; and a call on a parameter the method's
      signature types as a prelude class reaches only that class's method
      (`res.cookies` in the handler's `service` is WEBrick's, so
      `Rack::Request#cookies`, which needs `Hash#fetch` with a block,
      stays pruned in example 116).
    - **Gem leniency (172, 175), wider.** `include Enumerable` on a class
      whose each was pruned takes untyped; `x.replace(y)` and
      `x.clear` on a local String rebind it; `each` with a block on an
      untyped receiver (a Rack app's headers and body) is `rbEachAny`: an
      Array's elements, a Hash's pairs, or a class's own each, matched by
      its Go shape; a regexp RE2 cannot match (`\g<name>`, lookaround) is
      built and raises RegexpError when used (`rbRegexpLenient`), as
      Rack::Lint's URI patterns are built at load and never used.
    - **General gaps the gem hit** (beyond 175's Proc `call`, Struct
      superclass and lenient Structs/attrs, which rackup needs too):
      `$VERBOSE` read and write (nil silences `warn`); `Kernel#Array`; the
      `require_relative`s in a top-level `begin` (rackup.rb's `rescue
      LoadError` guard) load where they stand; regexp `(?#...)` comments;
      `ERB.new(<<-'T'.gsub(/re/, ""))` folds at compile time
      (Rack::ShowExceptions' template); `def_delegators :@x, *NAMES` of a
      constant Array literal; `Enumerable#include?` takes untyped (an
      untyped nil argument is false, not TypeError); a parameter narrowed
      by `||=` and reassigned stays visible and narrowed (`buffer ||= "";
      buffer = buffer + x` said "undefined local"); `URI::RFC2396_Parser`
      and `RFC3986_Parser#make_regexp` (MRI's pattern, lenient).
    - **Signatures.** `sig/gems/rackup/rackup.rbs` types what no call in
      the program reaches: `run`/`shutdown`, `service` (the Go server
      calls it), `Server#initialize`, `Input` and `Stream::Reader`'s read
      family. `sig/rackup.rbs` stays for `rbs validate`.
    - **Harness.** `TestRun` and `TestMinitest` compile through
      `CompileWithGems` too (175 did `TestExamples`), so `net_test.rb`'s
      `require "rackup"` compiles the gem (boot runs the file under MRI at
      compile time, decision 161); a file without a gem never starts Ruby.
    - **Differences from MRI**, beyond 174's: `rack.input` is the gem's
      Fiber-backed `Input` over a body net/http has already read;
      `SERVER_SOFTWARE` is absent.
    - **Proof:** examples 115 and 116 and `net_test.rb`'s `RackTest`,
      unchanged and equal to MRI, now running the compiled gem;
      `ControlVerboseTest`, `StringRegexpCommentTest`.
177. `ENV["TZ"]=` retargets local time, as MRI's `ruby_reset_timezone`
    (mspec's `with_timezone`, #49 phase A). Every ENV mutator goes
    through `ENV#[]=`, which on `"TZ"` calls `rbResetLocalZone`
    (`prelude/go/time.go`): a zoneinfo name loads as one (`CET`,
    `America/New_York`, `EST5EDT`); otherwise a POSIX
    `NAME[+-]h[:mm[:ss]]` becomes a fixed zone with the sign flipped
    (`JST-9` is +09:00); nil restores the zone the process started in;
    `""` and anything unparsable are UTC, as libc's `tzset`. Matches MRI
    on the zones ruby/spec uses.
    - **Known differences.** A POSIX DST rule (`XXX5YYY,M3.2.0,M11.1.0`
      with no zoneinfo file) stays on standard time. `time.Local` is
      written without a lock (decision 128's no-global-locks rule), so a
      thread reading local time while another assigns TZ races, as
      libc's `tzset` does under concurrent `localtime`.
    - **Also:** a `bot` call or `raise` in a value position (`f(raise
      "x")`, `f(die)`) is coerced as `func() T { ...; panic(...) }()`:
      Go accepts a function whose body ends in `panic` without a return,
      so this works for any `T`. It emitted an unparsable argument list
      before. `x = raise "e"` assigns the same way, typed as x's declared
      type or `untyped` (it declared a Go var with no type).
    - **Harness (amends 127).** A chained `before`/`after` hook
      (`__rbspec_<hook>_N`) whose body does not compile now has that body
      replaced by `skip "rb2go: <error>"`, not cut, so each example under
      it reports the real reason instead of `undefined method
      __rbspec_before_2`. `ruby_exe`/`ruby_cmd` are `bot` helpers that
      skip with a fixed reason: running Ruby source in a subprocess
      needs eval, so these never compile to anything else. `argf` is left
      undefined (ARGF lacks `seek`/`pos`/`readpartial`/... that its
      specs call next).
    - **Proof:** `stdlib_test.rb` `test_env_tz`, `control_test.rb`
      `test_noreturn_argument`; `core/time` 157 → 185 passing.
178. `RB2GO_BACKTRACE=1` prints an uncaught exception as MRI does (#49):
    `file:line:in 'label': msg (Class)` and a `\tfrom` line per frame,
    captured in `rbTopRecover`, where the panicking frames are still on
    the stack (an uncaught exception records none otherwise; decision
    106 captures only in a binding rescue). Unset, the report stays `msg
    (Class)`: decision 106's default is unchanged, and switching it is
    still its own decision. MRI 4.0's error_highlight snippet is not
    printed. `cmd/rubyspec` sets it, so a fixture's top-level statement
    that raises while the program loads (`::Module.prepend X` in
    `core/kernel`'s fixtures, a dynamic call on a method no class has)
    is located and cut like any crash, instead of failing the whole
    directory (`loadFrame`, used when no `_spec.rb` frame is found).
    - **Also:** `rbSourceMod`, the `-trimpath` prefix trimmed from frame
      files, cut at `prelude/go/backtrace.go` exactly; a pruned program
      can leave its helper under no `//line`, so frames read
      `gen/main.rb`. It now cuts at `prelude/go/`, else main.go's
      directory.
    - **Also:** union helpers (`rbUnion_<hash>`, decision 150) are
      emitted in every `emitNext` round once the tables are out, each
      once (`unionsOut`): a dispatcher emitted after the tables could
      name a union no table had, which failed `go build` (`core/proc`).
    - **Proof:** `cmd/rb2go` `TestUncaughtFullReport`, `cmd/rubyspec`
      `TestCrashFrames`; `core/kernel` runs (1,896 examples, 335 pass,
      was FATAL) and `core/proc` runs (182 examples, 18 pass, was FATAL).
179. ruby/spec phase B (#49): compile-time answers to what MRI decides
    at run time.
    - **Implicit conversion.** Where a String, Integer, Array or Hash is
      expected (a parameter, or a union member) and the argument's static
      class is a struct class defining `to_str`, `to_int`, `to_ary` or
      `to_hash`, the compiler calls it there (`implicitConv`), as MRI's
      `rb_convert_type` does at run time. Only struct classes: a core
      value type keeps its own rules (a Float is still not an Integer,
      decision 12). Untyped arguments are unchanged (checked by `rbAs`).
    - **Mocks.** `cmd/rubyspec` rewrites `x = mock(..)` (and `@x`) whose
      every later `should_receive`/`stub!` in the same statement list
      answers a literal (`and_return(lit)`, `and_raise(Const[, "msg"])`,
      with `with`/`once`/`at_least`/... ignored) into a class of its own
      in a generated `rbspec_mocks.rb`; `mock_int(lit)` and
      `mock_to_path(lit)` likewise. `should_not_receive` defines nothing.
      Call counts are not checked (ponytail in `mock.go`).
    - **Struct/Data members untyped when unannotated** (was an error),
      with a warning, as gem code's (decision 172). An anonymous
      `Struct.new(:a)`/`Data.define(:a)` (anything but a constant
      assignment) is a hidden class per literal, as decision 145's
      `Class.new`; `klass.new(a: 1)` on one maps keywords by member.
      Zero members are allowed. Structs gain `[]`/`[]=` (index, Symbol or
      String, MRI's IndexError/NameError), `values_at`, `dig` (3 deep: no
      splat into a dynamic call), `select`/`filter`; a member of the same
      name wins.
    - **Class-body guards.** `Const.respond_to?(:m)` folds like
      `defined?(Const.m)` (decision 167); `Data.define`/`Struct.new` count
      as present. The runner cuts a failing statement inside a folded
      `if`/`unless` branch, not the whole `if`.
    - **Lambdas.** A lambda parameter the body never reads is `untyped`
      when no call types it (it loses nothing); one that is read still
      needs a call or an annotation (decision 146).
    - **Overloads by count with a block.** A call with a block to a
      method that takes none tries `__<name>_<count>_block`, then a
      `__<name>_<count>` that takes a block, before failing (`fill { }`,
      `fill(1) { }`). `Array#fill` covers MRI's start/length/range forms;
      `a[start, len] = v` and `a[range] = v` splice (an Array value's
      elements, else the value), padding with decision 7's zero.
    - **Unannotated `attr_*`** (was an error) stand for the defs they
      define (`def x = @x`, `def x=(v) = @x = v`), inferred like any def,
      as gem code's already were (decision 172): the reader takes the
      ivar's type. A writer no call types takes `untyped` (only a dynamic
      call can reach it), which does not widen the ivar.
    - **Singleton defs on a mock.** `x = Object.new` or `x = mock(..)`
      followed by `def x.m ... end` in the same statement list moves the
      def into the mock's class: a def captures no locals, so its body
      reads the same there.
    - **Matchers.** The runner turns `x.should matcher(args)` into
      `x.should.matcher(args)` (`ProcExpectation.new(...)` for a lambda):
      `be_close(y, TOLERANCE)`, and on a lambda `complain(msg/regexp)`
      (its `$stderr` writes, `$VERBOSE` set as mspec does) and
      `output(out, err)`. `[[recv, *args, expected], ...].should
      be_computed_by(:m, *extra)` with literal rows unrolls into one
      static `recv.m(*args, *extra).should == expected` per row.
    - **Empty-def errors** carry the def's location (the error for a
      def whose inherited signature wants a value was unlocated).
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `ObjectImplicitConversionTest`,
      `array_test.rb` `test_fill_spans_and_blocks`/`test_index_assign_span`,
      `objects.txtar` `struct_member_types_missing` (now a warning).
180. ruby/spec phase C (#49).
    - **Inference rounds survive bodies their parameters break.** A def or
      lambda whose still-untyped parameter makes its body fail in a round
      (`def take(e) = e.select { ... }`: a block on an untyped receiver)
      returns `untyped` for that round instead of aborting the caller, so
      `take([1, 2, 3])` still records its argument and the next round
      types `e` (decision 146). A lambda's literal stays a Proc value with
      its parameters' inference key.
    - **A lambda no call types is untyped** (was a compile error), with a
      warning naming the annotation: its parameters are `untyped`, so its
      body's calls on them are dynamic, as a gem lambda's (decision 169).
    - **`$/`** is the input record separator, `String?`, readable and
      assignable (decision 61's set grows): a Go variable, `"\n"` at start.
      `chomp` with no argument uses it ("\n" also takes "\r\n" and "\r",
      another String is `chomp(sep)`, nil returns the String). Other
      readers (`gets`, `each_line`) keep "\n" (ponytail: read `$/` there
      when a spec needs it).
    - **Regexp twins** (decision 12's `__<name>_regexp`) for `index`,
      `rindex`, `byteindex`, `byterindex`, `partition` and `rpartition`;
      `rindex` tries each start on the rest of the String, so a lookbehind
      sees no text before it. `byteslice(range)`.
    - **Proof:** `infer_test.rb` `test_block_on_a_parameter_its_call_types`,
      `string_test.rb` `StringRegexpTwinsTest`, `arrays.txtar`
      `block_arg_proc` (now a warning).
181. ruby/spec phase D (#49).
    - **A core method truncates a Float where it takes an Integer**, as
      MRI's C methods do through `NUM2LONG`: `[1, 2, 3].first(1.9)` is
      `[1]`, `"ab" * 2.5` is `"abab"`; NaN, infinities and values past
      Integer's range raise MRI's `RangeError` (`float NaN out of range of
      integer`). Only prelude methods of String, Array, Symbol, Hash,
      Enumerable, IO, File, StringIO, MatchData, Regexp and Struct:
      numeric classes, Range, Kernel and Random take a Float as a Float in
      MRI (`2.pow(0.5)`, `rand(1.5)`, `(1..2).step(0.5)`), and a user
      method's `Integer` parameter still rejects a Float (decision 12), as
      Ruby methods do no conversion.
    - **Runner (amends 127):** an example's `class`, `module` and
      receiverless `def` statements move to the program's top level (a
      block opens no constant scope, so MRI defines the constant there
      too, only when the example runs); `class << x` on a mock or
      `Object.new` local folds its body into the mock's class, as `def
      x.m` does (decision 179).
    - **IO breadth.** `File#read(length)` (nil at the end, "" for 0),
      `File#gets(sep)`, `gets(limit)`, `gets(sep, limit)` (`""` is
      paragraph mode, nil the rest; a limit never splits a UTF-8
      character, and counts toward `lineno` only when it did not cut the
      line, as MRI); `IO.new`/`IO.for_fd` take `mode:` and ignore other
      options (ponytail). `File.new`/`File.open` take MRI's mode forms (a
      String, Integer flags, or `mode:`), a permission for a created file,
      and `flags:` or'd in; `File::RDONLY`..`NOFOLLOW` are the platform's
      `syscall` values. `File.expand_path(path, dir)`. `IO.open` (a block
      gets the IO and closes it after, answering the block's value) and
      `IO#read(length)`.
    - **Reflection tables** (decision 77's, beside `_Methods`):
      `_PrivateMethods` backs `Module#private_instance_methods` and
      `private_method_defined?`; `_SingletonMethods` (a class object's own
      `def self.x`, and every Kernel method, each callable as `Kernel.x`)
      backs `singleton_methods` and `public_methods`, which leave out
      Module's and Object's own and an extended module's (ponytail). Each
      table is kept only where its method is called (decision 49).
    - **Proof:** `array_test.rb`
      `test_core_methods_truncate_float_arguments`, `numbers.txtar`
      `float_where_integer_expected` (still an error for a user method),
      `object_test.rb` `test_private_and_singleton_method_tables`.
182. ruby/spec phase E (#49).
    - **An empty `X.new` is an open literal.** `Hash.new`, `Array.new`,
      `Set.new`, `Queue.new` (no arguments, no block) and
      `SizedQueue.new(n)` assigned to a local are typed from what is put
      in them (`[]=`, `<<`, `push`, `add`, `enq`), as an empty `[]`/`{}`
      already is: pass 1 builds them with `untyped` type arguments and
      records the evidence, pass 2 builds the joined type. No evidence
      keeps the "cannot infer" / "needs a type annotation" error.
      *See also decision 310* (a generic struct class's open `X.new`, typed by later calls).
      *See also decision 285* (`Array.new(n, nil)`, `::X.new`, and ivars).
    - **Runner:** a class or module statement in an example whose text
      runs expectations (`should`) or records (`ScratchPad`) is not hoisted
      (it would run at load, outside its example); a crash in the
      generated `rbspec_mocks.rb` is located and cut like any.
    - **Protocol hooks type themselves.** An unannotated
      `respond_to_missing?(name, priv)` is `(Symbol, bool) -> bool` (with
      an optional second parameter, `?bool`) and `method_missing(name,
      *args)` is `(Symbol, *untyped) -> untyped`: Ruby's protocol fixes
      them, and no call in the program passes them anything to infer from.
    - **More:** `File.read(path, length, offset, **opts)` (options
      ignored; past the end is "" where MRI says nil, ponytail), `IO.read`
      likewise; MatchData `begin`/`end`/`offset`/`byteoffset` take a group
      name (String or Symbol, the last group of that name, MRI's
      IndexError otherwise). mspec: `raise(E) { |e| ... }` yields the
      exception; `exist?`, `start_with?`, `end_with?`, `const_defined?`,
      `directory?`, `file?`, `method_defined?` and
      `instance_variable_defined?` are expectations.
    - **A superclass's nil ivar takes a subclass's type.** `@fd = nil`
      in a class where `@fd` has no type yet, and `@fd = 3` in a subclass,
      make one `Integer?` field on the superclass (moved up if the
      subclass declared it first): one object's ivar is one name across
      every class whose methods run on it. (ruby/spec's `before` hooks
      reset ivars that nested describes, subclasses here, assign.)
    - **`Time.new(String)`** (Ruby 3.3): a year alone, or a date with a
      full time and optional fraction, then an optional zone (`Z`,
      `+HH:MM`, `+HHMM`, `+HH:MM:SS`), else `in:` or local; `precision:`
      truncates the fraction (Go keeps nanoseconds, so digits past 9 are
      lost). Any other shape is `ArgumentError: can't parse: ...`, which
      MRI's own error regexps accept beside its specific messages.
    - **Proof:** `hash_test.rb` `test_empty_new_typed_from_use`,
      `object_test.rb` `test_unannotated_protocol_hooks` and
      `test_superclass_nil_ivar_typed_by_subclass`, `string_test.rb`
      `test_matchdata_offsets_by_group_name`, `stdlib_test.rb`
      `test_time_new_from_string`.
183. **Untyped where nothing in the program gives a type** (#49). Inference
    (decision 146) types a parameter from its calls; a def parameter no
    call types was a compile error. It is now `untyped`, with a warning
    naming the annotation (`# @rbs x: T`), as a lambda's already was
    (decision 180) and a gem def's (decision 169): only a dynamic call
    (`send` with a computed name, a hook such as `<=>` from `sort`, an
    untyped receiver) can pass it anything, and the body's calls on it
    are dynamic. Likewise:
    - `include Enumerable` (any generic module) in a user class with no
      `each` of its own to infer from (a subclass defines it) is
      `Enumerable[untyped]`, with a warning. An `each` that yields several
      values still needs the annotation (MRI packs them into an Array).
    - An unannotated def's `&block` is an optional, variadic `untyped`
      block (`{ (*untyped) -> untyped }`); calling it when no block was
      given is MRI's `NoMethodError` at run time (`undefined method 'call'
      for nil`), not decision 132's compile error, since nothing declared
      it required. The Proc type of an optional block keeps its rest flag.
    - An unannotated def whose `yield`'s value is used (`out << yield`, not
      a statement of its own) takes a block returning `untyped`, not
      `void` (it was emitted as an iterator and the value was lost).
    - An ivar a class only ever assigns `nil` is `untyped`, with a warning,
      as a gem's was (decision 169); it was "has no known type".
    - `Compiler.warn` takes a nil node for messages that carry their own
      position, as `errorf` does.
    - **Proof:** `infer_test.rb` `test_untyped_fallbacks`; `infer.txtar`
      `never_called` and `rest_never_passed`, `numbers.txtar`
      `operator_def_needs_signature`, `objects.txtar`
      `include_generic_without_args` are warnings now.
    *See also decision 307:* an unannotated `&block` whose Proc is called with one argument count takes those arguments' types.
184. **A splat into positional parameters** (`f(a, *xs)` where f has no
    `*rest`; #49). The splat must be the last argument, of an Array (an
    untyped one is checked by `rbAs`), into a method without post or
    keyword parameters. Its length is known only at run time: the call
    checks MRI's arity there (`rbSplatArity`: `wrong number of arguments
    (given n, expected lo..hi)`), each parameter past the fixed arguments
    takes its element (`rbSplatElem`), and an optional one the array does
    not reach takes its default: the callee's (rbArgc is the real count)
    or, for a caller-evaluated default, a branch on the length. It was a
    compile error. **Proof:** `infer_test.rb`
    `test_splat_into_positional_parameters`.
185. ruby/spec phase G (#49): small core surface.
    - `RUBY_VERSION` (`"4.0.0"`, what class-body guards fold against,
      decision 167) and `RUBY_PLATFORM` (MRI's spelling of Go's
      `GOARCH-GOOS`, without the OS version MRI appends: `arm64-darwin`)
      are constants; neither existed.
    - `Range.new(b, e, exclude_end = false)` (a nil end is not taken:
      ponytail), `Dir.home(user)` (the user database; MRI's ArgumentError
      for an unknown user), `String#byteindex(s, offset)` (MRI's
      IndexError off a character boundary) and `byterindex(s, offset)`,
      Ruby 3.4's `MatchData#bytebegin`/`byteend`, `File#fileno`.
    - mspec: `suppress_warning { }` (`$VERBOSE` nil around the block),
      `platform_is`/`platform_is_not` as values (their block forms stay
      the runner's static guards), `should.eof?`.
    - **Proof:** `string_test.rb`
      `test_byteindex_offset_bytebegin_range_new`.
186. **A program's own NilClass method answers a nil `T?` receiver** (#49).
    A call on `T?` raises NoMethodError on nil (decision 4's nil guard)
    unless nil answers the method: decision 12's `to_s`/`inspect`/`to_i`/...
    already did. Now a method the program defines on `NilClass` (mspec's
    `nil.should`, a user's `class NilClass; def blank? = true; end`)
    makes the call branch: nil takes NilClass's method, a value T's, and
    the result is their join. Prelude NilClass methods keep the existing
    paths. **Proof:** `object_test.rb`
    `test_nilclass_method_on_optional_receiver`.
187. `format`/`sprintf`/`printf` take positional arguments (`%2$s`, MRI's
    1-based argument N) and `%a`/`%A` hex floats (Go's `'x'` float
    format with the exponent's leading zero dropped, `0x1.8p+1`, then
    flags and width as C pads them) (#49). *Amended:* a negative `%x`/`%o`/
    `%b` without `+` or space is MRI's two's complement with `..` for the
    endless leading ones (`..f3c`; zero padding and precision fill with
    that digit), `#` on zero prints no prefix, a `#0` width counts the
    `0x` prefix as MRI's does, and NaN/Inf keep their case under `%A`.
    **Proof:** `string_test.rb` `test_format_positional_and_hex_float`
    and `test_format_negative_twos_complement`.
188. Fixes the measurement turned up (#49).
    - `$/` and `$VERBOSE` assignments are statements followed by the
      global's value, so one may be a block's or method's value
      (`suppress_warning { $/ = old }`); they were emitted as Go
      assignments in expression position.
    - A Struct/Data's generated source reports its declaration's line in
      errors, warnings and `//line` (`File.shownLine`; positions and
      annotation lookups keep the real line), so cmd/rubyspec can cut a
      failure in it instead of failing the directory.
    - Keyword `new` maps keywords to members only for a generated
      `initialize`; a block's own `def initialize(amount:)` takes them.
      (A Data whose own initialize takes keywords still does not compile:
      known gap.)
    - A user class's generated constructor and class-object `new` carry
      the class's `//line`: their Go errors named a line counted on from
      the previous directive, past the end of the file.
    - `include M` where M's abstract requirement (a prelude stub that
      raises NotImplementedError, as Comparable's `<=>` typed `(self) ->
      Integer`) meets a method the class inherits typed otherwise is a
      compile error at the include, not invalid Go.
    - `Kernel#open(path, ...)` is `File.open` (no `"|cmd"` pipes), and
      `Kernel#test` answers its file tests (`?e ?f ?d ?r ?w ?x ?l ?z ?s`).
    - **Proof:** `string_test.rb` `test_record_separator_chomp`,
      `objects.txtar` `include_comparable_over_inherited_cmp`.
189. `Struct.new(..., keyword_init: true)` (#49; decision 30 rejected it).
    `new` takes keywords only (a positional call is a compile error with
    MRI's arity message, where MRI raises it at run time), keyword `new`
    maps them to members as every Struct's does, and
    `keyword_init?` answers `true`, `false` or `nil` as written.
    **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `test_struct_keyword_init`,
    `objects.txtar` `struct_keyword_init_positional`.
190. `Kernel#caller(start = 1, length = nil)` (#49): the stack as
    backtrace lines (decision 106's frames and labels), from the method
    that calls it for `caller(0)`; nil past the stack's end. Known
    differences: file names are as given to the compiler (decision 61),
    and a statement that compiles to several Go lines can report a later
    line. `caller_locations` answers `Thread::Backtrace::Location`s
    (`path`, `lineno`, `label`, `base_label`, `absolute_path`, `to_s`)
    parsed from the same lines. **Proof:**
    `object_test.rb` `test_caller_labels`.
191. More ruby/spec surface (#49).
    - Ivar discovery reads past a statement it cannot compile, as an
      inference round does (decision 146): a `before` hook's later
      `@flags = ...` still types `@flags`, and the error is reported where
      it is, not as "has no known type" wherever the ivar is read. An
      ivar a class assigns `nil` before discovery types it otherwise is
      that type's nilable form (rack's `@body_retained = nil`, later a
      Boolean, is `Boolean?`).
    - `String#to_r` (the leading rational, `"1.5/2"` is 3/4, 0 when there
      is none, ZeroDivisionError for a zero denominator), over `big.Rat`.
    - `Regexp#encoding`: US-ASCII for an ASCII-only source, UTF-8 with a
      non-ASCII source, a `\u` escape or `//u` (ponytail: `//n` with a
      `\xNN` escape is BINARY in MRI).
    - Implicit conversion (decision 179) takes `to_path` where a String is
      expected and the class has no `to_str`: MRI's path parameters call
      it first, and rb2go types paths as String (more lenient than MRI
      for a non-path String parameter).
    - cmd/rubyspec's mocks also answer an expression that reads nothing
      of where it stands (`and_return(Dir.pwd)`, a receiver's call with
      no local, ivar, self, `__dir__` or block).
    - **Proof:** `string_test.rb` `test_to_r_and_regexp_encoding`.
192. An empty core container nothing types is `untyped` (#49). `Hash.new`,
    `Hash.new(default)`, `Array.new`, `Set.new`,
    `Queue.new`, `SizedQueue.new(n)` whose type parameters neither the
    expected type, a default, an annotation nor decision 182's open-literal
    evidence binds take `untyped` ones, with a warning naming the
    annotation; it was "cannot infer type parameter" or "needs a type
    annotation". Not a block form (`Hash.new { |h, k| ... }`), whose block
    is typed before the fallback could apply. Only these containers, and
    only in user code: other
    generic methods keep the error. **Proof:** `hash_test.rb`
    `test_untyped_empty_containers`, `hashes.txtar` `hash_new_unannotated`
    (now a warning).
193. **A non-empty Array literal widens to what is written to it** (#49).
    An unannotated `x = [1, 2]` is open as an empty `[]` is (decision
    182), its evidence seeded with the literal's own element type: a later
    `x << "s"`, `push`, `x[i] = v` or `x.fill(v)` of another type makes it
    `Array[Integer | String]` (decision 150's union), where it was a
    compile error. Writes of the literal's own type change nothing (still
    `Array[Integer]`, static). A splicing write (`x[range] = xs`,
    `x[i, n] = xs`) is not element evidence, and a seeded literal takes no
    nil "gap" from `[]=`. A local reassigned to another literal type is
    no longer an error either (`a = [1]; a = ["x"]`). **Proof:**
    `array_test.rb` `test_literal_widened_by_later_writes`.
194. `Enumerable#to_h` and unannotated `**opts` (#49). `to_h` reads each
    element as a `[key, value]` pair (a tuple or Array, through `rbAs`;
    MRI's "wrong array length" ArgumentError otherwise) into a
    `Hash[untyped, untyped]`; it did not exist. An unannotated `**opts`
    is `Hash[Symbol, untyped]`, as decision 183 makes an unannotated
    parameter `untyped`; it was an error. mspec: `mock_to_path(path)` for
    a computed path (an object whose `to_path` answers it, which decision
    191's `to_path` conversion takes) and `should.binmode?`.
    **Proof:** `infer_test.rb` `test_enumerable_to_h_and_unannotated_kwrest`.
195. **`autoload` is a require at compile time** (amends 161/175). MRI
    loads an autoloaded file the first time its constant is named; rb2go
    compiles the closed world ahead of time, so a user program's
    `autoload :X, path` loads the file where the autoload stands, as
    `require` would (once: a later require of the same file is a no-op).
    The path may be a literal on the load path or a static expression of
    `__dir__`: `File.join(__dir__, ...)`, `File.expand_path(name,
    __dir__)` or `"#{__dir__}/x"` (`staticPath`); a path that cannot be
    resolved is a compile error. Gem code keeps decision 175's rule (only
    what boot saw load). Differences from MRI: the file's load-time code
    runs at the autoload, not at first use, and `const_defined?` answers
    true before the autoload line (constants are static). **Proof:**
    [example 118](../examples/118_autoload/main.rb), `require.txtar`
    `autoload_missing_file`.
196. **Sorbet signatures are types** (#95). sorbet-runtime's `sig {}`,
    `T.let` and `T.cast` are Ruby calls MRI evaluates at load and run
    time; rb2go reads them at **parse time** (`internal/compiler/sorbet.go`,
    `File.lowerSorbet`, user and gem files, never the prelude) and the
    rest of the compiler stays Sorbet-blind. A `sig { ... }` statement is
    removed and becomes a synthetic `#:` comment on the next statement's
    line: `params` are matched to the def's parameters **by name** into an
    RBS method type (required, `?opt`, `*rest`, `k:`, `?k:`, `**rest`,
    `&blk` as an RBS block, `T.nilable(T.proc...)` as `?{}`), `returns`
    / `void` is the return, `type_parameters(:U)` makes it generic.
    Before `attr_reader`/`attr_accessor` the return, and before
    `attr_writer` the one param, is the attr's trailing `#: T`.
    Modifiers only Sorbet's checker reads (`override`, `abstract`,
    `overridable`, `final`, `checked`, `on_failure`) are ignored. A
    variable or constant write of `T.let(v, T)` becomes `= v #: T`
    (unless the line already has a `#:`); a `T.let`/`T.cast` anywhere
    else compiles as its value converted to the type, unchecked, as `#:`
    is (`genSorbetCast`). `extend`/`include T::Sig`/`T::Helpers`,
    `abstract!`, `interface!`, `final!`, `sealed!` and a `T.bind(self, X)`
    statement are dropped. `require "sorbet-runtime"` loads
    `prelude/sorbet.rb` (decision 155), not the gem: module `T` with the
    empty `Sig`/`Helpers`, `T.must` (raising sorbet-runtime's `TypeError`
    "Passed `nil` into T.must") and `T.unsafe` (`untyped`). Types:
    `T.nilable(X)` → `X?`, `T.any` → union, `T::Array`/`Hash`/`Set`/
    `Range`/`Enumerable`/`Enumerator[...]` → the class, bare → `[untyped]`,
    `T::Boolean` → `bool`, `T.untyped`/`T.anything` → `untyped`,
    `T.noreturn` → `bot`, `T.self_type` → `self`, `T.class_of(X)` →
    `singleton(X)`, `T.type_parameter(:U)` → `U`, `T.proc[.bind(S)]
    .params(..).returns(R)` → `^(..) [self: S] -> R`, `[A, B]` → tuple,
    `{a: A}` → record, and `T.attached_class` → RBS `instance`, which this
    decision adds: the class whose singleton method names it (the defining
    class, not a subclass receiver's). Difference from MRI: what
    sorbet-runtime raises while running (a wrong argument type) is a
    compile error here, as decision 20 does for `#:`; nothing is checked
    at run time. Not supported yet (compile errors): `T::Enum`; overloaded
    sigs. Added since (for Homebrew): `T.all` is its first arm that is no
    core mixin (Comparable, Enumerable, Kernel); `T::Struct` props are
    typed attrs with a keyword `initialize`; `T.type_alias { T }` and a
    fixed `type_member`/`type_template` name their type, and a fixed
    `Elem` is the type argument of an `include`/`extend Enumerable` that
    writes none; a bare `Proc` value type is `untyped` (a Proc is a Go
    func). **Proof:** [example 119](../examples/119_sorbet_sigs/main.rb),
    `sorbet_test.rb`, `sorbet.txtar`.
    *See also decision 295:* `T.bind(self, X)` of a subclass narrows self instead of being dropped.
197. **A traced closed world** (#82, Homebrew). *See also decisions 288, 296, 297* (`-trace` builds the entry from a run; `rb2go_replace` removed) (the magic comments became `-main`;
    `rb2go_fold` is removed). A program too dynamic
    to read its requires from source is compiled from an *entry* file
    that lists what MRI loaded, in require start order
    (`$LOADED_FEATURES` via TracePoint `:script_compiled`), with magic
    comments: `# rb2go_method_requires: runtime` (a require inside a
    method body is resolved at run time against what was compiled, not
    loaded at compile time), `# rb2go_replace: path => file` (compile
    another file in its place), `# rb2go_fold: expr => bool` (a fact the
    host does not have, `OS.mac?`) and `# rb2go_main: file` (compiled
    after the boot, never booted itself). In this mode the `-I` dirs are
    gem dirs: boot capture (161), leniency (172) and pruning (169) apply
    to all of them. No eval at run time: what boot evaluated is compiled.
198. **define_method from boot** (amends 161). *See also decisions 324, 325*. Each `define_method`
    MRI ran at boot (a TracePoint on its `c_call`/`c_return`, with the
    caller's binding) becomes a synthesized `def` of that name on its
    owner, singleton or `module_function` as MRI made it. The block's
    captured locals are written into the def as assignments of the value
    boot saw: a literal, the constant holding that object (`M::C`,
    `M::C.fetch(k)`, `M::C[i]`), or a Proc rebuilt from its literal block
    (`proc@file:line`). A capture boot cannot express, or one the block
    assigns, is a compile error. The `define_method` call itself is a
    no-op. Difference from MRI: a call made after boot defines nothing.
199. **prepend at link time.** *See also decisions 296* (`class << self; prepend M`), *298* (`X.prepend M` inside a body). `prepend M` copies M's methods into the
    class; a method the class defines itself moves to a hidden private
    name the copy's `super` calls (`superTarget`), and `ancestors` lists
    M before the class. A module only ever prepended keeps no methods of
    its own. **Proof:** `object_test.rb` (`ObjMixinFeaturesTest`).
200. *(Withdrawn: a prelude port of concurrent-ruby. The real gem is
    compiled instead; see 169 and 206.)*
201. **Load-time branches fold to what boot ran** (amends 161). Boot runs
    with `Coverage.start(branches: true)`; a load-time `if`/`unless`/
    `case` in gem code (a class body, a constant initializer) whose arms
    boot saw only one of compiles as that arm. Method bodies are never
    folded.
202. **Subclasses of core value and container classes.** A class whose
    superclass is a `@go_type` class embeds it: Hash and Array, Set and
    Queue (generic bases, with untyped elements), String and Pathname
    (value bases). Inherited methods run on the embedded value (a
    `-> self` method answers the subclass object, not the base); a
    subclass of such a subclass (`Concurrent::Set < CRubySet < ::Set`)
    embeds its parent and calls the parent's own methods on it. `new`
    with arguments on a value base without its own `initialize` builds
    the base through the base's `new`. **Proof:** `object_test.rb`
    (`ObjMixinFeaturesTest#test_core_subclasses`).
203. **Class aliases.** `B = A` names the same class object; so does a
    constant whose value boot saw be a class named otherwise (`Impl =
    case ... end`, private constants included). `Thread::Mutex` and the
    other `Thread::` names are the top-level prelude classes.
204. **String and block evals from boot** (amends 161). `eval`,
    `class_eval`/`module_eval` and `instance_eval` of a String, and
    `class_eval`/`module_eval` with a block, that boot ran on a named
    module are compiled into that module as its body (a block run on
    several modules is each one's body); the call site is a no-op. An
    eval on anything else is a compile error. Boot also records
    Forwardable's `def_delegators` lists by call site, for a list built
    at load time.
205. **Poisoning in gem code** (amends 172). A gem method whose body
    does not compile becomes `raise NotImplementedError` naming why, a
    constant that does not compile stays `nil`, and a gem file's
    load-time statement that does not compile is skipped, each with a
    warning; the rest of the program still builds.
    `RB2GO_KEEP_GOING=1` reports every such error in one run.
    *See also decision 293:* a block to a prelude method that takes none is a compile error in gem code too. *See also 301* (a failed attempt leaves no declared locals behind). *See also 303* (a method Go rejects is stubbed in the Go text, without a second compile).
206. **Gem overrides answer their parent's slot** (amends 8, 172). Go
    needs one signature per method name, so an override typed apart from
    the slot it fills gets an adapter under the slot's name. Beyond
    decision 8's superclass case, gem code links: overrides whose
    *inferred* return differs (linked after return inference); overrides
    of an *included module's* method (the module's constraint calls
    through its signature); a positional override of a `*args` method
    (by argument count, else ArgumentError); an `each(head = nil)`
    answering Enumerable's `each()`; and a `<=>` typed `T.nilable(Integer)`
    for any object, answering Comparable through `Op_cmp` on the topmost
    class that has it. A method whose body is only `raise` (the abstract
    idiom) takes its overriders' joined return type. A gem method added
    to a core module (`Enumerable#exclude?`) calls `self` dynamically, so
    it does not widen the module's constraint. A gem module mixed into a
    value class (Pathname) keeps its ivars as per-call locals: memoizing
    recomputes.
207. **A generic class has no Go method whose signature grows the class**
    (#96 phase 1, amends 86; 197–206 are reserved for `rb2go/brew`).
    Decision 86 already made a generic primitive's bodies free funcs;
    what remains as Go methods on `Array[E]` is a forwarder for each name
    a kept interface declares (`Each` for `Enumerable_Self`, `Op_eq` for
    `rbEq`, `Inspect`) and the `Dyn*` wrappers. Go instantiates every
    method of a generic type, so a forwarder for a method whose signature
    names its own class at a type built from its params
    (`Enumerator::Lazy#with_index` returning `Lazy[[E, Integer]]`,
    `Enumerator#__with_index_enum`) is an instantiation cycle whenever it
    exists: only pruning kept it out of programs, and `rb2go stubs`
    filtered it by hand. `wantsForwarder` now never emits one
    (`growsOwnClass`); typed calls already reach the free func, and the
    `Dyn*` wrappers already skipped such methods (`growsTypeParams`).
    - **Proof:** `TestPreludeWhole` type-checks the unpruned
      every-library program with go/types (5s; building it takes 6.8 GB),
      so pruning can no longer hide a cycle or an ill-typed method.
    - **Measured, not done:** in `64_observable` each of `Array`'s 32
      instantiations still compiles 154 methods, ~120 of them `Dyn*`
      wrappers: phases 3–4 of #96. And every type parameter rb2go emits is
      `comparable`, which is not a basic (method-set) interface, so Go
      gives each pointer type argument its own GC shape; under `any`, all
      pointers share `*uint8` (measured on Go 1.27: `Cmp[*A]`, `Cmp[*B]`
      two bodies, `Any[*A]`, `Any[*B]` one). Collapsing pointer shapes
      would drop ~3.1k of `64_observable`'s 17.3k shaped bodies. It needs
      an inference of which params must stay `comparable` (Hash keys, Set
      elements, `==` in `%x{}` bodies, transitively through calls).
208. **A forwarder instantiates `Self` with one type per owner, not per
    class** (#96 phase 2, amends 8). Go compiles a generic body once per
    GC shape and, for each distinct type argument, a small dictionary
    wrapper. An inherited class body was already instantiated with its
    owner's interface (`Module_Constants[ModuleI]`: one instantiation for
    every metaclass), so #96's premise that each class compiles its own
    copy held only for two cases, now fixed:
    - **Universal owners** (`Kernel`, `Object`, `BasicObject`) were
      instantiated with the receiver, `Kernel_FrozenQ[*Foo]`: a wrapper
      per class. A struct class's forwarder now passes `[any]`, as
      decision 121 does for Dyn wrappers. Primitives keep their own type,
      which `any` would box.
    - **Module bodies** were instantiated with each includer's interface
      (`Minitest_Assertions_AssertEqual[FooTestI]`), each a GC shape of
      its own. When the module's constraint names no `Self` in a method,
      the forwarder passes the constraint itself, `Mod_Self[any, E]`,
      which every includer satisfies. Decision 8's `[VersionI]` rule
      stays where it is needed: the constraint names `Self`
      (`Comparable_Self`'s `Op_cmp(other Self)`; `constraintNamesSelf`
      reads the rendered constraint), the signature names `Self` or the
      class (`sigMentions`), or the class is generic.
    - **Rejected:** `*C` for class owners. It shares the shaped body
      (pointers of a method-set constraint share `*uint8`) but adds a
      dictionary wrapper per class: `64_observable` went from 51,939 to
      55,075 compiled functions.
    - **Measured:** `64_observable` 51,939 → 50,855 compiled functions.
209. **Dyn wrappers stop selecting forwarders; a generic class's wrappers
    are one switch; markers are class-ID switches** (#96 phases 3–4,
    amends 32, 85, 87, 121). Measured on `64_observable` (every-name),
    Dyn wrappers on generic types were 10.1k of its 51k compiled
    functions, the forwarders their bodies selected 7.5k, and marker
    stubs 2.2k.
    - **One switch per generic class and batch.** Go compiles every
      method of a generic type for each instantiation, so `Array[E]`'s
      ~120 `DynX` wrappers were a shaped body plus a wrapper each, per
      element type. `emitDynName` holds a generic class's wrappers back,
      and `emitDynGen` emits each `emitDynamic` batch's as one method,
      `_DynGenN(rbDynName string, args []any) (any, bool)`: a `switch`
      over the Ruby names (private ones keyed `_name`), each wrapper body
      with `return v` rewritten to `return v, true` (`okReturns`, on the
      parsed body). The batch number keeps the method unique across
      `emitNext` rounds; a dispatcher asserts its batch's method after
      `DynX` and before the shared arms, and `false` falls through to
      `method_missing` and `NoMethodError` as a missing `DynX` did. The
      pruner parks each `case "name":` of a `rbDynName` switch until
      `rbDynName`'s dispatcher is kept (`visitSwitch`), as a wrapper
      method was kept only while selected: without that, 116_cuba_web
      went from 41k to 49.5k (a batch's late-noted names dragged in
      `Hash#to_a`'s `Array[[K, V]]` and 58 more `Array` instantiations).
      A switch left with only `default` is inlined
      (`inlineDefaultOnly`). Names Go code asserts by method (`<=>`'s
      `DynOp_cmp` in `rbCmp`, `to_json`'s `DynToJson`) keep their
      methods.
    - **Wrapper bodies call the owner's free func.** Decision 121 sent a
      wrapper's call of a Kernel method to `Kernel_X[any](self, ...)`;
      now every `self.X()` a struct class's wrapper body makes
      (`plainCalls`, including the arity overloads such as `__sleep_0`)
      goes to the owner's free func: `[any]` for a universal owner,
      `[OwnerI]` for a non-generic struct class (`ownerIface`), which
      every class below it implements. Modules keep the forwarder call
      (their constraint may name `Self`), and so do class objects: a
      metaclass answers Module's and Class's methods through Go-level
      overrides (the synth `Name()`), and Go's promoted copy of a
      parent's wrapper bypasses them (`klass.name` came back empty,
      `Integer === 1` false: decision 121's failure, again). A shared arm whose body uses
      `[OwnerI](self` asserts `OwnerI` for `self`. Nothing selects the
      forwarder through an interface literal any more, so the pruner
      drops it (`64_observable`: 5,234 → 2,388 forwarders).
    - **Markers.** `rbHas_RespondsX`/`_PrivateX` switch on the class ID
      for every class but `Boolean` (whose values answer TrueClass's and
      FalseClass's IDs): a marker method on each primitive was kept as a
      pruned stub wherever the assertion was.
    - **Measured** (compiled functions; peak compile RSS of `main` with
      `-dwarf=false`, same machine and load): `64_observable` 50,855 →
      36,131 and 1,948 → 1,726 MB; `116_cuba_web` 40,856 → 34,782;
      archive 155 → 122 MB. Peak memory follows the count only weakly:
      the same program compiles in 2,296 MB with DWARF and 1,455 MB with
      `-l` too, so debug info and inlining move it more than the
      wrappers did.
    - **Found, not done:** Go generates a wrapper for every method a
      struct gets by embedding its parent (`super_Foo_`), used or not:
      10,120 of `116_cuba_web`'s compiled functions, 8,937 of them for
      names the subclass's own interface does not declare. Replacing the
      embedding with a named field and explicit `_Ancestor()` accessors
      (prototyped) cut `64_observable` by only 1.7k and 3% RSS, and
      `116_cuba_web` then needs explicit `_ClassOf` and inherited attr
      readers; not pursued.
    *See also decision 294:* an Array of the class's own bare parameter (Set#to_a) gets a wrapper.
    - See also decision 302: Array's and Hash's block-taking wrappers left the per-shape set for the view.
210. **A class body whose signature names no `Self` is a plain function**
    (#96, option A of the split; amends 8, 151, 208). `plainSelf`: a
    struct class's own method, or a universal owner's (`Kernel`,
    `Object`), with no type parameters of its own or its owner's, no
    `SelfType`, and no type variable in its params, block or return,
    is emitted as `func Exception_Message(self ExceptionI) String`
    (`any` for a universal owner) instead of
    `Exception_Message[Self ExceptionI](self Self)`. The body is still
    generated with `self` typed `Self`; when its text names `Self`, the
    function opens with `type Self = ExceptionI`. Forwarders, typed
    calls (`typeArgs`), wrapper bodies (`freeCall`, `plainCalls`) drop
    the type argument, and `dynShareable` reads any
    `Owner_X(self` call as one on the owner's interface. Generic stays
    generic: modules (their constraint is per includer), generic
    classes, `-> self` and other `Self`-typed signatures
    (`Kernel_Dup`, `Exception_Exception`).
    - **Why:** a generic function compiles once per GC shape plus a
      dictionary wrapper per type argument, cannot be a function value
      (so a package split cannot reach it through a slot), and is
      recompiled by every package that instantiates it. In
      `116_cuba_web` 515 of 768 generic free funcs were generic only
      over `Self`; 304 remain generic.
    - **Speed:** decision 151's `[*C]` own-class instantiation made
      `self._C()` a direct call. A plain body is small enough for Go to
      inline into the forwarder, where the receiver is `*C` again and
      the call is devirtualized: a 50M-iteration loop of `Circle#area`
      and an inherited `Shape#sides` went from 1.84 s to 1.55 s user.
    - *See also decision 308:* a generic struct class's instance is a plain class, so its methods are plain functions too.
    - **Measured** (compiled functions; peak compile RSS of `main`,
      `-dwarf=false`): `64_observable` 36,131 → 32,825 and 1,701 →
      1,672 MB; `116_cuba_web` 34,781 → 33,244 and 1,397 → 1,367 MB;
      archives 78 → 72 and 77 → 73 MB; compile CPU −8%.
    - **Split estimate, unchanged:** stubbing every non-generic body of
      `116_cuba_web` still leaves 81% of its compile memory (1,138 of
      1,410 MB; 79% before). Stubbing only the generic bodies leaves
      79% too (1,118 MB) but cuts compile CPU by 41%; the rest is
      declarations, itabs and Go's embedding wrappers (decision 209's
      note). A split that moves only class bodies out of the bottom
      package saves at most ~20% peak memory.
    - **Phase 5 measured, not done (2026-10-11, #96 comment):** the
      unpruned core prelude as a cached `rt` package (63.5k compiled
      functions, 1.9 GB, 36 s once) with the program as `main`
      dot-importing it and the ten whole-program tables (`rbUnbox`,
      `rbCmpBox`, class-ID arrays, ...) as hook variables made
      `05_word_count`'s own package 1,370 → 20,844 functions and
      `116_cuba_web`'s 33,244 → 82,797 (1.35 → 3.1 GB). Go recompiles
      a generic instantiation in every package that reaches it, and an
      instantiated type brings its whole method set, transitively
      through the bodies (`Array[E]` has 120 methods unpruned; a
      40-line module confirmed it, with `-l` too); embedding
      `super_Foo_` promotes every parent method. 402 Dyn wrappers and
      markers on prelude types exist only per program and cannot be
      declared from another package. So the split waits on
      forwarders being replaced by type-switch dispatchers in `main`
      and generic classes keeping a fixed ~12-method set (#96 phase
      3b); until then one pruned package is the cheaper build.
211. **A generic primitive's own methods have no forwarders but the
    protocol names** (#96 phase 3b, amends 86). Decision 86 gave every
    own method of `Array`, `Hash`, `Set`, `Range`, `Enumerator` a one-line
    Go method forwarding to its free func, and relied on the pruner to
    keep only the ones a kept interface declares. Typed calls never used
    them (`callCode`'s `free` is true for a generic primitive), and in an
    unpruned program (`RB2GO_NO_PRUNE=1`, `TestPreludeWhole`, a future
    precompiled prelude) all 124 of `Array[E]`'s methods compiled per
    instantiation, transitively through every instantiation their bodies
    name. `wantsForwarder` now emits a generic primitive's own method as a
    Go method only when something reaches it through a Go method set:
    `stdMethodNames` (`String`, `Len`, ...), `genericProtocol` (what
    prelude Go asserts or calls by method on an unknown instantiation:
    `_to_any`, `_from_any`, the `_eq_rec`/`_eql_rec`/`_hash_rec`/
    `_inspect_rec` recursion guards, `==`, `eql?`, `hash`, `<=>`, `each`,
    `succ`, `to_s`, `inspect`, `to_json`, `pretty_print`), an included
    module's self calls (`includerCalls`), or a universal name
    (`universalHas`: Kernel/Object/BasicObject define it, so `ObjectI` or
    `rbEq` may select it). Dyn dispatch on generic classes already goes
    through `_DynGenN` before any shared arm (decision 209), so no
    untyped path changes.
    - **Measured** (compiled functions; peak compile RSS, `-dwarf=false`):
      unpruned core prelude 63,506 → 34,431, 1,872 → 971 MB, 36 → 6 s,
      archive 127 → 69 MB; `Array[E]` 124 → 31 methods per
      instantiation. Pruned programs move little, as the pruner was
      already dropping these: `116_cuba_web` 33,244 → 31,692 and 1,347
      → 1,288 MB; `64_observable` 32,825 → 32,107.
    - **Proof:** suite unchanged (`hash_test`'s recursive equality found
      the `_eq_rec` guard must stay a method, or `rbEq` recurses forever).
    - **Next (phase 3b):** the same for generic struct classes
      (`Enumerator` and its subclasses, `Queue`), then the 6,015
      metaclass forwarders of the remaining 9,795.
212. **A struct class's method dispatches by type switch, not by Go
    interface method** (#96 phase 3b step 1, amends 8, 89, 210;
    `dispatch.go`). A Ruby call on a receiver statically typed as a
    struct class (or `self` in its body) whose resolved method is
    `dispatchable` compiles to the owner's free function when no class
    below the receiver's static class overrides the name, else to
    `rbSend_<Owner>_<Name>(self OwnerI, ...)`: a type switch over the
    classes whose own resolution differs, each arm a direct call to that
    class's free function, the default the owner's body with the real
    `self`. Such a name has no forwarder on any class and no class
    interface declares it (`dispatchableAll` walks the ancestors'
    resolutions, so one ancestor that keeps the Go method keeps it on
    every descendant). Dispatchable: the owner is a struct class (not a
    module, metaclass, universal owner or `Module` subclass), the method
    is a public plain def that decision 210 emits as a plain function,
    every override below has the same Go signature, and nothing reaches
    the name through a Go method set: `stdMethodNames`, `genericProtocol`
    (decision 211), a universal name (`inspect`, `to_s`, `==`, asserted on
    `any`), an including module's self call, or a selector prelude Go
    calls (`goCalled` scans `prelude/**` for `.Name(`). A `Method`
    object's bound definition (`staticDef`) and a Dyn wrapper body's
    self call keep their direct calls (MRI runs that definition).
    - **Why:** a Go interface needs the method on every implementer; the
      pruner hid that per program (decision 89), a precompiled prelude
      cannot, and Go recompiles every method of a generic instantiation
      and every promoted method of an embedded struct in the importing
      package. The switch is compiled code with inlinable arms, no map or
      reflection, and `self` is never the embedded parent (decision 8's
      hazard), so no forwarder re-declaration is needed.
    - **Speed:** 20M calls of `area`/`sides` over four `Shape`
      subclasses: 0.33 s → 0.23 s user (the arm inlines).
    - **Measured** (compiled functions; peak RSS, `-dwarf=false`):
      `116_cuba_web` 31,692 → 31,075 (forwarders 2,751 → 2,541), 1,288 →
      1,254 MB; unpruned core prelude 34,431 → 33,072. The hand-built
      `rt` split of decision 210's note re-run: `05_word_count`'s own
      package 20,844 → 3,372 functions and 805 → 194 MB (one pruned
      package: 1,370 / 124 MB), `116_cuba_web`'s 82,797 → 36,370 and
      3,128 → 1,463 MB (31,075 / 1,254 MB). What remains there is
      metaclasses: a user class's `Foo_Meta` embeds `Class` and inherits
      ~100 promoted Module/Class/Kernel wrappers (`Constants` ×235,
      `Throw` ×286); the next slice.
    - **Proof:** suite unchanged; `method_test`'s
      `bind_call` found the `staticDef` rule, `object_test`'s module
      constraints the ancestor walk, minitest's reporters the ordering
      against decision 86's free-func shortcut.
    - *See also decision 308:* a generic struct class's instances dispatch the same way (one class each).
213. **Metaclasses and universal owners dispatch too; module constraints
    stop declaring Kernel names** (#96 phase 3b step 2, amends 208, 209,
    212). Decision 212's `dispatchable` now also admits a metaclass or
    `Module` subclass as owner and a class object as receiver (their
    synth `name`/`new`/`to_s` stay Go methods, so an override by synth is
    "not plain" and keeps the interface path), a `%x{}`-bodied prelude
    method (`kindPrimitive`: the same free function as a `def`), and a
    universal owner (`Kernel`, `Object`, `BasicObject`): the dispatcher
    takes `self any` and switches over every struct class whose own
    resolution is not the universal body. `self` in a module body is some
    includer, so a universal name there dispatches over all classes, and
    the module's constraint no longer declares it (`emitModuleInterface`
    skips it; `includerCalls` answers false through `universalDispatch`),
    which is what put `Loop`, `Sleep`, `Display`, `ObjectId` ... on every
    class in an unpruned program. The pruner rewrites a dispatcher whose
    arms were dropped to one or none into an `if` or nothing
    (`inlineDefaultOnly`), as gocritic asked. `RB2GO_DISPATCH_DEBUG=1`
    prints why a universal name stays a Go method.
    - **Measured** (compiled functions; peak RSS, `-dwarf=false`; the
      unpruned core prelude is what a cached `rt` would cost once):
      unpruned core 33,072 → 18,651 and 909 → 656 MB, 8.5 → 3.1 s
      (session start, before decision 211: 63,506, 1,872 MB, 36 s);
      forwarders 9,122 → 2,086. Pruned programs are flat (`116_cuba_web`
      31,075 → 31,025, `64_observable` 31,098 → 30,779). The hand-built
      `rt` split re-run: `116_cuba_web`'s own package 36,370 → 25,986
      functions and 1,463 → 1,209 MB, now below today's single pruned
      package (31,025 / 1,320 MB); `05_word_count`'s 3,372 → 3,040 and
      194 → 165 MB (1,370 / 124 MB). What remains in cuba's package: the
      per-class markers and protocol (`_ClassID`, `Inspect`, `ToS`,
      `Op_eq`, `_Dup`, `_Copy`, `_ClassOf`, ~7 × 430 types), `Itself`/
      `Exit`/`Sleep` on 286 metaclasses (`-> self` and Go-called), and
      9,170 generic instantiations.
    - **Speed:** README benchmarks unchanged (7.6x total both sides);
      the `Shape` loop 0.23 → 0.17 s user.
    - **Proof:** suite unchanged; `95_open_uri`'s lint found the one-arm
      switch.
    - **Amended:** `goCalled` skips a selector on a std package name
      (`os.Exit(`, `time.Sleep(`), which had kept `exit` and `sleep` as
      Go methods on every class: unpruned core 18,651 → 17,640.
214. **A module's own method dispatches through a generic type switch;
    the constraint declares only what Go iteration needs** (#96 phase 3b
    step 3, amends 208, 212). `dispatchable` admits a non-universal
    module's own method when its free func can take the constraint as
    `Self` (no `Self` in signature or constraint, decision 208), it is not
    `each` or an iterator, no other module that includes this one
    self-calls the name, and every includer's own override is its own
    plain method with the same signature read in the module's parameter
    names (`alignedArgs`: `include Enumerable #[E]` names each class
    parameter once; `Hash`'s `#[[K, V]]` would not, but Hash overrides
    none of them). The dispatcher is generic over the module's
    parameters, `rbSend_Enumerable_First[E comparable](self
    Enumerable_Self[any, E], n Integer)`, its arms name includers at
    those parameters (`case *Array[E]: return Array_First[E](v, n)`,
    verified Go accepts parameterized arms), the default is the module
    body with the constraint as `Self`; with no overrider the call is
    that default directly. A call site binds the parameters from the
    receiver's include environment (`moduleCallArgs`). `Enumerable_Self`
    went from eight names to `Each`; `includerCalls` and
    `emitModuleInterface` skip dispatched names, so neither an includer
    nor a generic primitive carries a forwarder for them, and
    `dispatchableAll` asks `includerCalls` so a struct's own method is
    dropped only when no constraint above needs it. Arms are the
    concrete pointer type, never the class interface, which every
    subclass would also satisfy (minitest's `SummaryReporter` answered
    `StatisticsReporter#report`); a primitive overriding a universal name
    (`Monitor#exit`) is an arm calling its direct Go method, and a
    generic primitive overriding one makes the name non-dispatchable.
    - **Measured** (compiled functions; peak RSS, `-dwarf=false`):
      `116_cuba_web` 31,025 → 28,696 and 1,320 → 1,214 MB,
      `64_observable` 30,779 → 28,685, `05_word_count` 1,370 → 1,178;
      forwarders in cuba 2,485 → 1,907; unpruned core 17,640 → 17,411
      (instantiations 6,550 → 5,417; `Array[E]` 31 → 25 methods per
      instantiation).
    - **Proof:** suite unchanged; `enumerable_test`, `object_test`'s
      module cases, minitest's reporters and `Runnable.runnable_methods`
      (a metaclass override the arms must list).
215. **`IO.new(fd, autoclose: false)` and `IO#autoclose=` keep a borrowed
    descriptor open.** An IO over a descriptor another IO owns (`IO.open(w.fileno, "w") { }`) closed
    that descriptor when its block ended, and the owner's later close, or
    MRI's finalizer, closed the same number again: by then a later test's
    listening socket (`stdlib_test`'s WEBrick server answered
    `ECONNREFUSED` under `--seed 1` on MRI itself). MRI's `autoclose:
    false` and `autoclose=` mark the IO as not owning the descriptor:
    `close` flushes and drops its buffers but leaves the fd (`noclose` on
    the Go struct); `autoclose?` reads it (and raises `IOError` on a
    closed stream, as MRI does, so the test reads it before closing).
    - **Proof:** `testdata/test/stdlib_test.rb`
      (`test_io_open_block_and_read_length`), which now passes on MRI in
      every order.
216. **A gem's or user file's method shares a dispatcher arm too; arms
    come first** (#96, Homebrew; amends 85, 209). `dynShareable` admitted
    only prelude and synthesized methods, so brew's `Object#blank?`,
    `deep_dup`, `present?` (its `extend/` files) left a `DynBlankQ`
    wrapper, `return Object_BlankQ(self)`, on each of its 1,629 classes;
    so did every metaclass's `Module_ConstGet(self, ...)`, because
    `ownerIface` excludes class objects. Now:
    - Any method whose wrapper body is the same text for every class
      shares the class-ID arm, wherever it is defined. A class object's
      body that already calls its owner's plain func (decision 210) is
      shared with `self` asserted to the owner's interface: it is the
      same call it made per class, so the synth overrides decision 121
      protects are not bypassed.
    - `_Call` (decision 79) reaches a shared member through the
      dispatcher, `rbDynX(rbFCall, self, args...)`, so minitest's
      `test_*` and predicate names no longer need a per-class wrapper.
    - **The arms run before the `DynX` assertion.** Go promotes an
      embedded parent's methods, so a subclass served by an arm still
      answered `interface{ DynX }` with its parent's own wrapper, `self`
      the embedded parent: `B < A` inheriting `hi`, which calls `who`,
      got `A#who`. Arm class IDs are exact, so checking them first is
      right for every class.
    - **Measured** (generated functions, `rb2go gen`): `64_observable`
      12,199 → 9,876; `116_cuba_web` 11,653 → 11,259.
    - **Proof:** `dynamic_test.rb` `DynamicSharedArmTest`.
217. **A memo read from a signature still being inferred is not kept;
    `super` into a `Self`-naming module passes the class interface**
    (amends 208, 214; concurrent-ruby's `Concern::Obligation`).
    - `constraintNamesSelf` renders the module's constraint, and
      `dispatchable` reads signatures. Both were memoized the first time
      they were asked, which can be from inside an unannotated method's
      return inference: `wait`'s body calls `complete?`, which asks
      whether the module's constraint names `Self`, while `wait`'s own
      `-> self` was still pending and rendered as no return at all. The
      memo said no, so forwarders passed `Obligation_Self[any]` to a
      constraint that requires `Wait() Self`, and the program did not
      type-check (outside any gem method, so decision 205's poisoning
      could not catch it). `sig` now counts signatures rendered with a
      pending return (`pendingSigs`), and a memo taken across one is
      returned but not stored.
    - An includer's override that calls `super` into such a module
      (`Delay#wait`) instantiated the module body at its own generic
      `Self`, whose `Wait` returns the class interface, not `Self`.
      The call now names the class interface, `Obligation_Wait[DelayI]`,
      and converts a `Self` result back with `rbAs[Self]` (it is self).
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `ObligationSelfTest` (did not build
      before).
218. **An unresolved constant in gem code raises `NameError` where it is
    read** (amends 172, 205). Homebrew's `Bootsnap.load!` returns early
    unless bootsnap is enabled, and only then names `::Bootsnap`, a gem
    the compiled program never loads. The unresolved read was a compile
    error, so decision 205 poisoned the whole method and the early return
    went with it: every `brew` run raised `NotImplementedError` at load.
    MRI resolves a constant when the read runs, so in gem code
    (`f.lenient()`) the read now compiles to `rbConstRead[any](false,
    nil, "Bootsnap")`, untyped, which raises MRI's `NameError`
    ("uninitialized constant Bootsnap") only if reached. User code keeps
    the compile error.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`Gemish#setup_optional`).
219. **`any?`, `all?`, `none?`, `one?` without a block** test the elements
    themselves; with an argument, `pattern === element`. A blockless call
    to a method whose block is required now tries `__<name>_<count>`
    (`__any_q_0`, `__any_q_1`) before the "requires a block" error, the
    mirror of decision 179's `__fill_0` block overloads. The truthiness versions are
    `%x{}` over `rbTruthy`, since a generic `E` may hold nil
    (`Array[Integer?]`) and rb2go's static truthiness calls `E` always
    true. Brew's `kegs.any?`, `external.any?`.
    - **Proof:** `array_test.rb` `ArrayBlocklessPredicateTest`.
220. **A gem's ivar with no known type is untyped** (amends 172). Decision
    172 already typed an undeclared module ivar `untyped` in gem code; a
    class's ivar (Homebrew's `Tty.@escape_sequence`, read before the
    `define_method` bodies that assign it) was still a compile error,
    which poisoned the method (205). Once discovery ends, every ivar a gem
    class's own method bodies name (`eachIvarName`, as
    `declareGemModuleIvars` already did for gem modules) that discovery
    left untyped is declared `untyped`, before any struct is laid out. A
    declaration while bodies are emitted would name a field the struct
    never got, and the discovery dry run of a boot-born body (Tty's
    `define_method` colours, concurrent-ruby's `attr_atomic` evals) can
    fail before it reaches the ivar. User code keeps the error.
    - A gem body that fails to compile before discovery ends (a return
      inference reaching it early) is poisoned in that run's discarded
      output only, not for good: discovery may yet type what it lacked.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`Gemish#never_set_or`, `Gemish.cached`).
221. **`Kernel#freeze` and `frozen?` on user objects** (amends 96). Decision
    96 left a user object's `freeze` a compile error; Homebrew freezes its
    own objects everywhere (`Options#freeze` calling `super`). When a user
    or gem file names `freeze` at all, each root struct class carries a
    `frozen_ bool` (a program that never freezes pays nothing), and a
    generated `rbObjFrozenFlag(a any) *bool` switches on the concrete class
    (`case *Foo: return &v.frozen_`, a type-hash search, not a map).
    `Kernel#freeze` sets it (Array and Hash keep their own flags) and
    returns self; `frozen?` reads it.
    - **Not done:** an ivar write does not check the flag, so a frozen
      object's setters still run where MRI raises `FrozenError`. Generic
      struct classes (Set) have no flag: `freeze` leaves them as they are.
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `ObjectFreezeTest`.
222. **`a, b = pair_or_nil`** destructures a `[A, B]?` into `A?, B?`: nil
    gives each target nil, as Ruby's nil (no `to_ary`) does
    (`tag, digest = hash.find { }`). Only a plain list of targets; a
    splat or nested target on an optional tuple stays an error.
    - **Proof:** `control_test.rb` `ControlOptionalTupleTest`.
223. **`super` into a method that takes a block** passes the super call's
    literal block, else the current method's own block (Ruby passes it on
    implicitly, with or without `&block`), else nil when the parent's
    block is optional (`Resource::Local#initialize` →
    `Resource#initialize(name = nil, &block)`). An iterator's `super` (its
    block is a Go `yield`) is still an error, now saying so.
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `ObjectSuperBlockTest`.
    *See also decision 305* (`super(&:sym)` and `super(&proc)`).
224. **Blocks on untyped receivers in gem code, for Enumerable's
    non-mutating names** (amends 176). `JSON.parse(s)["kids"].select { }`,
    `__getobj__.reject { }`, `Pathname.glob(..).max_by { }`: the receiver
    is switched on at run time; a `Hash` takes `Hash[untyped,
    untyped]`'s method, anything else `Array[untyped]`'s over its
    elements (`rbElemsAny`: an Array's own, else what its `each` yields
    through decision 176's `rbEachAny`). Each branch compiles on its own,
    and one that cannot (a Hash-only name reaching the Array side) raises
    `NoMethodError` if taken. The receiver is a copy, so only names that
    leave it unchanged are in the list (`dynEnumNames`). Other names with
    a block on an untyped receiver stay an error.
    - `Hash#fetch` and `Array#fetch` with a block (`__fetch_block`):
      in gem code the block was dropped (172), so a miss raised
      `KeyError` instead of calling it.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`Gemish#json_*`), `hash_test.rb`
      `HashFetchBlockTest`.
225. **A gem's class value where its signature names another class is
    asserted, not rejected** (amends 172). Ruby checks no types:
    Homebrew's `Tab.empty` gets `super`'s `AbstractTab`-typed value (a
    `Tab` at run time, `T.attached_class`), a loader returns `nil` under
    a non-nil sig, a union of loaders meets another union. In gem code a
    struct-class target (an interface in Go) takes nil as is and any
    other struct value or union of them through `rbAs[ToI]`, and a union
    target takes a struct-valued source through its union conversion;
    both raise `TypeError` only if the value really is something else
    (MRI would fail later, at the call that needs the method).
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`Gemish#tab_for`).
226. **A yield inside a block the method passes on** (amends 6, 183;
    concurrent-ruby's `def synchronize; if owned? then yield else
    @lock.synchronize { yield } end; end`). It was a compile error.
    - In a closure-block method it is a call of `blk`, like any yield.
    - In an iterator it calls Go's `yield`: the inner block runs during
      the iteration, which Go allows. A consumer's `break` (yield returned
      false) cannot `return` through the closure, so it panics
      `rbIterBreak{}`, and an iterator with a nested yield
      (`containsNestedYield`) defers `rbIterBreakRecover`.
    - A gem wrapper's tail yield is its value: in a gem method that also
      yields inside a block, a yield in tail position (the method's last
      statement, through `if` branches, not into blocks) counts as using
      the block's value (`yieldValueUsed`'s `tailUsed`), so the block
      returns `untyped`, not void, and `synchronize { @value }` is the
      value rather than nil. Not every tail yield: that makes the method a
      closure-block one, and Cuba's `on` returns from inside `try`'s block,
      which a closure cannot do until non-local return exists (decision 4).
    - `-> {}.freeze` (and `.itself`) with an expected Proc type generates
      the lambda at that type: Homebrew's `EMPTY_BLOCK = T.let(->
      {}.freeze, T.proc.void)` built a `func() any` for a `func()` slot.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`Gemish#synced`), `control_test.rb`
      `ControlNestedYieldTest`.
227. **Gaps on Homebrew's `brew info` path** (#81-style stdlib items,
    amends 13, 172):
    - **A tuple's Array methods, in gem code.** `[t - 1, 0.0].max`,
      `pair.second`, `[a, b].hash` were "undefined method for tuple". A
      gem call on a tuple of a name `Array` has goes to `Array[E]` of the
      fields, `E` their join (untyped when mixed), which answers as
      MRI's Array does.
    - **Splats into a dynamic call or computed send.** `callback.call(*a,
      *b)`, `send(m, state, *args)`, `curl(*args, "--x", **opts)`: the
      arguments become one `[]any` (`anySpread`: `rbArgsCat` of the
      pieces, an untyped splat through `rbSplatAny`, MRI's to_a for nil,
      Array and Hash).
    - `Pathname#open` with a block; `Dir.mktmpdir` without a block (the
      path, left in place) and with `prefix_suffix`/`tmpdir`; `IO#write`
      and `StringIO#write` of several values (`__write_2`...), the byte
      counts summed.
    - **Proof:** `dynamic_test.rb` `DynamicSplatTest`, `stdlib_test.rb`
      `StdlibPathOpenTest`, `testdata/boot` (`Gemish#remaining`).
228. **`is_a?(Module)` at run time** (amends 21). Decision 21 made
    `x.is_a?(Mod)` a compile error when x's static type could not decide
    it (untyped, or a class a subclass of which includes Mod): rb2go had
    no runtime record of included modules. The closed world is that
    record: `rbHasIsA_Mod(x)` switches on x's class ID over every class
    whose ancestors include Mod (decision 85's marker switch, emitted with
    the program's tail since a body is being written when the test is
    met). A type variable (`[T] (T)`) and `case`/`when Mod` on such a
    value stay errors.
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `ObjectModuleIsATest`.
229. **`$stdout.extend(M)` is `IO` including `M`.** Homebrew's
    `Utils::Output` ends with `$stdout.extend Mixin` and `$stderr.extend
    Mixin`, so `$stderr.opoo(...)` works; rb2go raised NoMethodError
    (`extend` on an IO) at load. The closed world has no per-object
    singleton classes, so an `extend` of a module onto `$stdout`,
    `$stderr` or `$stdin`, in user or gem code, is collected as an
    `include` into `IO` and the statement itself is gone: every IO answers
    M's methods, which only `respond_to?` on another IO could tell apart
    from MRI.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`Demo::Out`).
230. **A `define_singleton_method(name)` template becomes the receiver
    class's `method_missing`** (amends 31, 198; *see also decision 324*). Homebrew's
    `CLI::Parser#set_args_method(name, value)` runs `@args.
    define_singleton_method(name) { instance_variable_get(:@table).
    fetch(name) }` for every parsed flag, and `Cmd::Info#run` calls
    `args.sizes?`: a compile error, which poisoned `run` itself. The
    block reads nothing but the name it is given, so whatever MRI defines
    at run time is that body at some name. In gem code, such a call on an
    ivar whose class an assignment's `#:`/T.let annotation names gives
    that class `method_missing(name, *_)` with the block's body (built as
    decision 198 builds a def from a block), kept by the gem pruner
