    (`keepNames`), and the run-time call becomes a no-op. Decision 31
    then compiles `args.sizes?` to `method_missing(:sizes?)`.
    - **Differs from MRI:** `respond_to?(:sizes?)` is false (no singleton
      method exists), and a name never defined reaches the body (here
      `fetch`'s KeyError) rather than NoMethodError.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`Demo::CliParser`).
231. **Homebrew's command DSL** (amends 145, 164, 196, 225). Every `brew`
    command class runs `cmd_args do switch "--json" ... end` at load; the
    block is `T.proc.bind(CLI::Parser)`, stored in an ivar, handed on as
    `Parser.new(self, &@parser_block)` and `instance_eval`'d there, and
    `cmd_args` also makes `const_set(:Args, Class.new(CLI::Args))`.
    - **An annotation keeps a value's bound self.** Sorbet's `T.let(block,
      T.nilable(T.proc.void))` drops the bind its types cannot carry;
      rb2go's Proc type has self as a parameter, so an annotation naming
      the same Proc shape without one adopts the value's (`adoptBind`).
    - **An untyped value under `#:`/T.let is converted** (`rbAs`) where it
      was only relabeled, which Go rejected (`@args = T.let(parser.parse,
      Args)` with an untyped parse).
    - **In gem code an untyped value may land in a typed ivar**
      (`@value = T.let(value, T.untyped)` into `Integer | String | nil`),
      converted there, as decision 225's assignments are.
    - **`class << self` defs get decision 145's hidden classes**: their
      `Class.new` literals were never scanned.
    - **`Module#const_set` returns its value.** The constant table is
      fixed at compile time, so a run-time const_set is not seen by
      `const_get`/`constants`, and the anonymous class keeps its
      `#<Class:file:line>` name where MRI names it on assignment.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`demo/cli.rb`).
232. **More of Homebrew's `brew info` path** (amends 21, 171, 207, 229):
    *See also decision 290*.
    - **`keys`/`values`/`to_a` on an untyped Hash or Array.** Decision 207
      gives a generic class no Dyn wrapper for a method whose result grows
      its type (`Hash#keys` is `Array[K]`), so on JSON data they raised
      NoMethodError. A dynamic call of such a name first switches on
      `Hash_Any`/`Array_Any` and calls it on the `[untyped]`
      instantiation, which grows nothing (`dynContainerCall`); anything
      else goes on to the dispatcher. `@self`-typed methods
      (`transpose`) are not covered.
    - **`ENV.extend(M)`** and `STDOUT`/`STDERR`/`STDIN.extend(M)` are
      decision 229's include, into `ENVClass` and `IO`. A gem module's
      Enumerable call on `self` with a block takes decision 224's path,
      where ENV, Hash-like, takes the Hash branch through `to_h`.
    - **`Regexp.last_match`/`last_match(n)`** read the caller's `$~`
      (decision 171 counts them as reads of it; `__last_match_at` indexes
      it), and English's `$CHILD_STATUS`, `$PROCESS_ID`/`$PID`,
      `$LAST_MATCH_INFO`, `$RS` are the globals they alias.
    - **Hook overrides calling `super`** (`inherited`, `initialize_dup`,
      `initialize_clone`, `included`, `extended`, `prepended`,
      `method_added`): with no parent method, the super is a no-op, as
      Class's and Object's hooks are; a prelude method would have been a
      slot every metaclass overriding it duplicates. A struct's `_Dup`
      calls a class's own `initialize_dup` (ponytail: its super does not
      go on to the class's `initialize_copy`, as MRI's Object#initialize_dup
      would).
    - `Array#flatten!` (nil when nothing was an Array), `MatchData#to_a`.
    - **Proof:** `dynamic_test.rb` `DynamicContainerTest`, `object_test.rb`
      `ObjectHookSuperTest`, `array_test.rb` `ArrayFlattenBangTest`,
      `string_test.rb` `StringLastMatchTest`, `testdata/boot`
      (`EnvSensitive`).
233. **Homebrew's subprocess layer and more gem leniency** (amends 4, 6,
    97, 107, 146, 172, 183). `SystemCommand#exec3`/`each_line_from` run
    every child (curl for the API data) through `Process.spawn(env,
    [exe, exe], *args, in:, out:, err:)`, `Process.detach`, `IO.select`
    and `ReadlineNonblock`'s `read_nonblock(n, @buffer)`.
    - **`Process.spawn`'s general form**: an env Hash (nil unsets),
      `[program, argv0]`, and `in:`/`out:`/`err:` (an IO, a path, :close),
      `chdir:`, `pgroup:`, `unsetenv_others:` (`rbSpawnFull`, reached as
      `__spawn_hash`/`__spawn_array` by the first argument's class;
      decision 12's class overloads now allow a rest parameter).
      `Process.detach` is a Thread whose value is the Process::Status.
      `Kernel#fork` raises NotImplementedError where reached (Homebrew
      forks only when spawn fails); `Thread.handle_interrupt` yields.
    - **`IO.select`, `read_nonblock`, `readpartial`** on Go's poller: a
      pipe's read deadline (a millisecond's grace, since a deadline already
      past hides an EOF) and `bufio.Reader.Peek`, which tests readability
      without consuming. `IO::WaitReadable`/`EAGAINWaitReadable` move from
      socket.rb to core. `IO#winsize` (TIOCGWINSZ; Errno::ENOTTY,
      ENODEV), `Time#to_r`, `Etc.uname` (from uname(1)).
    - **String mutation in gem code** (decision 172's rebinding) reaches
      ivars too, `concat` (several arguments), `slice!(range)`, and an
      out-buffer argument: `io.read_nonblock(n, buf)` rebinds buf to what
      was read. MRI's aliasing (an Array holding the buffer sees later
      fills) is not reproduced: Strings stay immutable.
    - **Signatures**: an unannotated `initialize` returns void; a gem
      def that does not yield takes the block an override below it yields
      (`AbstractLockableObject#synchronize`), with that override's answer
      to whether the block's value is used; a gem def whose body ends in
      `raise` (abstract) returns untyped, so overrides inheriting its
      signature keep their values.
    - **Gem leniency**: a local assigned two unjoinable types is untyped;
      nil where a Boolean is kept is false (MRI's value is nil, which
      `p` would show); a T? or another union into a union converts at run
      time; a tuple destructured into another count of targets, or
      yielded to another count of block params, gives the first fields,
      then nil; `raise x` and `raise klass, msg` with x or klass untyped
      (or a union) decide at run time (`rbRaiseValue`), and a bare `raise`
      outside a rescue is an empty RuntimeError; an iterator call's value
      is the receiver for `each_line`/`each_char`/`each_byte`/`times` too,
      and nil for others (`loop`).
    - **Proof:** `stdlib_test.rb` `StdlibSpawnTest`, `testdata/boot`
      (`Demo::Counter`, `Demo::Lenient`).
    - **Amended (2026-10-13, ruby/spec `core/enumerable`):** the
      inherited block applies only to an override of the parent's
      parameter shape (`sameParamShape`), and `iterAdapterBody` passes
      nothing for a rest parameter the slot lacks (`EachCounter#each(*arg)`
      answering `Numerous#each`) instead of indexing it; any other extra
      parameter is a compile error, not a crash. The adapter yields the
      slot's element type (`iter.Seq[Integer]` for a parent whose `each`
      yields Integers, converting the override's elements with `rbAs`),
      not always `any` (ruby/spec `core/range`).
    - **Also found (2026-10-13):** decision 202's inherited call on a
      subclass of a non-generic `@go_type` class (`Thread`, `Time`)
      emitted the free function (`Thread_Name(&self.super_Thread_)`), which
      only generic bases have; it is the base's Go method now
      (`containerCallCode`, `isDirectMethod`; ruby/spec `core/thread` and
      `core/marshal`, broken since eaf6397). A `Thread` subclass's `join`
      waited forever: decision 239.
234. **Fewer per-class functions for Homebrew's build memory** (#96,
    amends 209, 214, 216). The capped brew build was killed at 7.39 GB.
    - **`Kernel#exit` is private**, as MRI's is (`exit` and
      `Kernel.exit` work; `obj.exit` is NoMethodError). It was public, and
      a Go-side `Monitor.Exit()` call kept the name out of type-switch
      dispatch, so every class interface listed it with a forwarder:
      about 1.6k functions in brew.
    - **A module calling a method of a module it includes reaches that
      method's dispatcher**: decision 214 kept a module method out of
      dispatch when another module including it called it on self
      ("self-called by including module"), so `Utils::Output::Mixin`'s
      30 methods had a forwarder on each of its 278 includers. Everything
      that includes the calling module includes the callee's, so its
      self is asserted to the callee's constraint (`any(self).(M_Self[any])`)
      and passed to the dispatcher.
    - **A dispatched module method's Dyn wrapper is one shared arm**: its
      body is instantiated at the module's constraint (decision 208), the
      same text for every includer, with self asserted to it. Without
      this the wrappers grew by what the forwarders shrank.
    - Decision 216's class-name guard matches the class's own Go names
      (`A`, `AI`, `A_Meta`, `A_class`), not any substring (`rbArity`
      holds an `A`).
    - **Measured** on brew's generated Go: 77,381 → 75,788 functions from
      `exit`; the dispatch change cut forwarders 17.6k → 12.8k. The net
      count with the shared arms, and a capped build, are not measured yet.
235. **Fewer generic bodies per element type for build memory** (#96,
    amends 209, 137, and the computed-send rule of decision 32). Brew's
    generated Go compiles 68k source functions into 274k: 42% are generic
    instantiations, and the largest share is `Array[E]`'s `_DynGenN`
    switch (decision 209) compiled once per element shape, 336 times
    (213 tuple shapes, 31 class-interface shapes), each pulling every
    body it names. Measured by stubbing (compile only, `-p 2 -c=1`,
    `-dwarf=false`): every `_DynGenN` body gone is 6,761 → 4,597 MB and
    677 → 128 s; every function body gone still leaves 2,852 MB of
    declarations, so a package split cannot go below that. Three
    compile-time changes, no runtime cost:
    - **Kernel names on generic containers share one class-ID arm.**
      `dynShareable` admits a generic class when the wrapper body is a
      universal owner's and names none of the class's type parameters:
      the same text for every instantiation, so `frozen?`, `object_id`,
      `instance_variable_get` ... on an untyped `Array[E]` are one arm
      in `rbDynX`, not a case in every instantiation's switch. A name
      with `E` in it stays in `_DynGenN`.
    - **Marshal's key switches are constant cases.** The pruner reads
      `case rbKeyed[T]("k"):` for its type hint, then `unkeyCases`
      rewrites it to `case "k":`: a string switch of constants compiles
      to a binary search, and no `rbKeyed` instantiation is left per
      class (brew: 12k lines of them). Two classes with one tag (a class
      alias, decision 203: ruby/spec's `core/dir`) were two legal call
      cases and are one constant case: the first stays, as it won before.
    - **A computed `send`/`respond_to?` in gem code names what gem and
      user code can answer.** `dynAll` made every method name of every
      class dynamically reachable; brew has 155 computed sends, 145 on
      receivers of known class and ~10 on untyped ones
      (`config.send(method)`, both parameters), so no exact rule covers
      it. From a gem file (decision 172's lenient mode) the name set is
      now every name a non-prelude class answers, own or inherited
      (`MyError#message`, `Kernel#frozen?`, a `Hash` subclass's names),
      plus names literally called dynamically, plus every symbol
      written in a gem or user file (`:sort`, `&:name`, `%i[a b]`,
      `symbolLiterals`: what a computed call can name without building
      a string; `assert_respond_to(Regexp, :=~)` and a delegator's
      `respond_to?(:upcase)` found this). A name only a prelude class
      answers that no file spells as a symbol is left out, so
      `x.send(:"so#{rt}")` on an untyped `x` raises `NoMethodError`
      where MRI would answer: the decision 205 family. A computed send
      in user code keeps the every-name set (`dynAllStrict`); so does a
      prelude forwarder's (`SimpleDelegator`'s `respond_to_missing?`,
      minitest's `assert_respond_to`) outside a gem program
      (`pruneGems`), since it passes on whatever a user file gave it.
    - **Not done, measured:** giving dynamic methods only to the
      instantiations that can be held untyped (SSA `MakeInterface`
      roots over the generated program) would save nothing: 97 of
      brew's 97 `Array` shapes can (73 without the Marshal tables).
      Scoping a computed send by its receiver's class alone does not
      help either, as one untyped site flips the global flag. Sharing
      the expensive bodies through `Array[any]` with a copy at the
      untyped boundary (−32% memory, −81% compile time upper bound)
      costs a copy on `x.sort` where `x` is an untyped view of a typed
      Array; not taken.
    - **Phases 5 and 6 of #96, closed with data.** A precompiled prelude
      package (hand-built, decisions 210/213): `116_cuba_web` −15% wall
      and −16% functions, `05_word_count` +0.3 s (it links the whole
      prelude's type tables), memory flat; the prelude is 9% of brew's
      Go lines. Sharding the program into packages: brew's declarations
      alone are 2.85 GB, and cross-package calls through slots undo the
      inlining decisions 212–214 bought. Neither pays.
    - **Measured** (compiled functions; peak compile RSS, `-dwarf=false`):
      `64_observable` 29,936 → 29,624 and 1,649 → 1,487 MB;
      `116_cuba_web` 29,345 → 28,585 (its computed sends are pruned);
      brew's capped cross-build (`-p 2 -c=1`, `GOMEMLIMIT=4GiB`)
      68,155 → 68,143 source functions and 6,911 → 6,535 MB with the
      first two changes; 65,600 and 6,439 MB (−7%), 708 → 696 s, with
      the third: `rbSendByName` 4,540 → 4,118 names, but `Array`'s
      switch keeps 86 of its cases because brew spells `:sort`,
      `:first`, `:map` ... as symbols, so the per-element-type cost
      stays. What would remove it is the `Array[any]` copy path above,
      or fewer element shapes; neither is free.
    - **Proof:** `dynamic_test`'s Kernel-names-on-containers case,
      `marshal_test`, `testdata/boot`'s `dyn_send`/`dyn_responds`.
236. **Whole-container methods on an untyped receiver run through a
    view, one body for every element type** (#96, amends 209, 211). A
    dynamic call of `sort`, `join`, `uniq`, `-`, `merge`, `except` ... on
    an `Array[E]` or `Hash[K, V]` held untyped used to run that class's
    `_DynGenN` case, a method, so Go compiled every body the case names
    once per element shape: 336 `Array` shapes in brew, 213 of them tuple
    element types. Now `Array[E]` carries six one-line accessors
    (`_len`, `_at`, `_set`, `_append`, `_trunc`, `_make`) and `Hash[K, V]`
    seven (`_len`, `_key_at`, `_has`, `_get`, `_store`, `_delete`,
    `_make`), protocol names so they stay Go methods, and
    `rbViewCall`/`rbHashViewCall` (`prelude/go/view.go`) answer 37 Array
    names and 13 Hash names against those interfaces: compiled once, no
    copy of the container, mutation in place (`sort!` is the same object,
    `uniq!` truncates and appends), results made by the receiver's
    `_make` so they keep its type. `_at` hands over the raw `E` (a
    `*String` for `String?`), which `_set`/`_append` take back as is;
    `rbUnbox` gives the Ruby value where semantics apply (`nil`, `==`,
    `<=>`, `to_s`). Arguments that are views are read raw too, so a `T?`
    value round-trips between same-typed containers; an untyped value
    written into a `T?` slot still hits `rbAs[*T]`'s gap, as `[]=` from
    untyped did before (follow-up). The dispatcher asks the view after
    `DynX` (a user subclass's own method wins) and before `_DynGenN`.
    Names that stay per element type: the O(1) ones (`[]`, `[]=`,
    `fetch`, `push`, `first`, `size` ...), the protocol, and names gem
    code defines by reopening (`blank?`, `deep_dup`, `exclude?`).
    `Hash#to_h` answers self, as MRI's. Prelude helpers starting with
    `_` are not dynamic names (a brew `Array` subclass had put `_at` in
    the every-name set). Not interfaces instead of generics for typed
    code: that boxes every element everywhere (see the 2026-10-13
    research in #96); the view confines boxing to the untyped path.
    - **Measured** (compiled functions; peak compile RSS): `64_observable`
      29,624 → 26,720 and 1,487 → 1,248 MB (`Array`'s switch 75 → 38
      cases, `Hash`'s 50 → 29); brew (`-c=4`, no memory limit: 10 cores,
      32 GB) build 104 → 87 s and 10.6 → 10.0 GB, `Array` 80 → 49 cases,
      `Hash` 50 → 37.
    - **Found on the way, amending 215:** `IO.new(fd, autoclose: false)`
      wrapped the borrowed descriptor in an `os.File`, whose finalizer
      closes the fd when the IO is collected, so a later `IO.pipe` that
      got the same number broke (`write |1: bad file descriptor`,
      `stdlib_test`, only when GC timing shifted). It now wraps a
      `dup(2)`: close and the finalizer close the dup, the owner's fd is
      untouched, `autoclose?` still answers false (`borrowed`). A late
      `autoclose = false` pins the `os.File` (`rbPinFiles`).
    - **Proof:** `dynamic_test`'s `test_view_*` cases (MRI-compared,
      identity, frozen, nested join/flatten, empty), `hash_test`,
      `array_test`, `stdlib_test`.
    - See also decision 302: the block-taking names go through the view too. *See also 304:* the element-level names (fetch, [], []=, push, pop, shift, ...) go through `rbViewCore`; `_trunc` became `_resize`, and `_drop` was added.

237. **Generation and compilation profiled; the slow parts were not the
    code's shape** (2026-10-13, after #96). Brew's `brew info wget` entry
    generated in 686 s and built in 641 s. Profiles:
    - **Compile: the harness.** The capped build used `-c=1` and
      `GOMEMLIMIT=4GiB` on a 6.4 GB live heap, so the collector ran
      nonstop (3,488 CPU-s for 654 wall-s, five cores busy on a
      one-thread backend). The same `main.go` with `-c=4` and no limit:
      **95 s** (51 s of function bodies at 5,150 funcs/s, 16 s parse and
      typecheck of 640k lines, 19 s writing the object), 10.9 GB. GOGC
      tuning changes nothing; `madvise` in the profile is page reuse. The
      harness (`hb/hbuild.sh`) is `-c=4`, no limit, a 14 GB guard.
    - **Generation: `gemFile`.** 44% of brew's 930 CPU-s (54% of cuba's
      17 s) was `realPath` → `filepath.EvalSymlinks` → `Lstat`: `lenient()`
      is asked per expression from 41 sites, and each ask re-resolved
      every gem directory's symlinks. The gem dirs are resolved once and
      each `File` remembers its answer. cuba gen 17.3 → 5.9 s (sys 7.9 →
      0.5 s); brew 686 → 242 s.
    - **Generation: `discoverIvars`.** Its two dry-run rounds generated
      every method body; only a body that names an instance variable
      (read, write, `||=`, `instance_variable_get/set`) can tell it
      anything, so `mentionsIvar` (a Prism walk, memoized on the
      `Method`) skips the rest. Output is the same text with dispatchers
      in another order (discovery no longer notes dynamic names first)
      and regexp literals renumbered. Measured small: cuba 5.9 → 5.6 s,
      brew within noise (the 29% in the first profile was mostly the
      `Lstat` above, charged to the bodies discovery generated).
    - **What is left in cuba's 4.5 s:** GC 35%, `formatGo` 25% (gofmt of
      100k lines), `emitDynamic` 19%.
    - **Measured:** see each bullet; brew end to end 686 + 641 s →
      242 + 87 s with decision 236's view.
238. **An anonymous class is named by where its literal stands** (amends
    145, 146). `Class.new`, `Module.new` and an unassigned `Struct.new`
    literal declared `RbAnon1`, `RbAnon2` ... by a counter. Decision 146's
    rounds carry a parameter's inferred type from one compile to the next
    by class name (`rehome`), and a counter renumbers every later literal
    when an earlier one appears or drops out between compiles, so
    ruby/spec `core/kernel`'s `KernelSpecs::M.extended(o)`, typed
    `singleton(RbAnon18)` in a round, met the same literal as `RbAnon17`
    in the final compile and failed to build (since 62a6035, which made
    the hook calls reach it). The name is now `RbAnon<fnv32 of the
    file>_<offset>` (`anonName`), stable across compiles of the same
    source; a second parse of one file in one compile gets `_2`. A
    literal is declared once even when the scan reaches its node twice
    (`scanAnonClass`, as the `Struct.new` path already did). The `RbAnon`
    prefix, which `Object.constants` leaves out, is unchanged.
    - **Measured:** ruby/spec `core/kernel` builds again: 601 of 1,896
      pass (604 before the regression).
    - **Proof:** `go run ./cmd/rubyspec core/kernel`; `dynamic_output.rb`
      (no `RbAnon` constant visible).
239. **A `Thread` subclass starts on its own embedded `Thread`** (amends
    202, 223). `class SubThread < Thread` embeds a `Thread` value (decision
    202), and `SubThread.new { ... }` compiled to `NewSubThread()`: the
    block dropped, the thread never started, `done` a nil channel, so
    `join` waited forever (ruby/spec `core/thread`, hidden until decision
    233's amendment let the directory build). Copying a started `Thread`
    into the subclass cannot work: the goroutine writes `val` and `err` on
    the original, and the struct holds `sync.Map` and atomics. Now
    `rbThreadStart(t, run)` starts a thread in place (`rbThreadRun` is it
    on a new `Thread`), and `Thread#initialize` (`(*untyped) ?{ () ->
    untyped }`) calls it on `self`. `genGoTypeNew` takes a subclass whose
    inherited `initialize` takes a block down the path a subclass with its
    own `initialize` takes: the empty object, then `initialize` on it, which
    reaches the embedded base. `super { ... }` into it passes its block
    (`genContainerSuper` uses decision 223's `superBlock`, and calls a
    non-generic base's Go method, not a free func). Without a block,
    MRI's `ThreadError` ("must be called with a block"). `Thread.new` itself
    is unchanged.
    - **Not done (ponytail):** a zsuper's arguments are accepted and not
      passed to the block (a block taking them is a compile error);
      `Thread.current` inside the thread is the embedded `Thread`, not the
      subclass object.
    - **Measured:** ruby/spec `core/thread` 55 → 57 pass.
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `GoTypeSubclassTest#test_thread_subclass_runs_its_block`.
240. **Generation profiled again: memos for what each call recomputed, and
    a GC that trades memory for speed** (2026-10-13, after decision 237).
    Brew's `brew info wget` generated in 165 s (quiet machine). Profile:
    `sendArms` 62 of 274 CPU-s (23%), of it 28 s in `isSubclassOf` and
    ~25 s sorting: it copied and sorted the class list and walked every
    class's ancestry for each dispatchable call, again on every trial
    generation; GC 105 CPU-s (38%) at GOGC=100 with a 1.1 GB peak.
    - **Memos, valid while the class list does not grow:** `sendArms` per
      (owner, method), `privateIn` per dynamic name. Per compile:
      `findRequire` per required name and `realPath` per path (`c.real`; the
      require scan probed every load-path directory for every `require`),
      compiled patterns (`c.re`, `dynShareable`'s per-owner and per-class
      regexps), and the overload-suffix `strings.Replacer`, built once.
    - **GC:** the CLI sets GOGC=400 and a 4 GiB soft memory limit
      (`tuneGC`), unless GOGC or GOMEMLIMIT is set: cuba's generation CPU
      halves (9.5 → 4.5 s user, 225 → 490 MB); the limit tightens the
      collector again on the largest programs. Tests compiling in-process
      keep Go's defaults.
    - **Measured** (brew, same output byte for byte): 165 s, 1.1 GB →
      102 s with the `sendArms` memo → 86 s with the GC setting, 2.5 GB →
      82 s with the rest. cuba and `64_observable` output unchanged.
    - **What is left (82 s):** `formatGo` 21 s (`emitDynamic` 14 s of it),
      decision 146's inference rounds (`collectUses`) 15 s, ivar discovery
      10 s, method lookups 6 s; one core. Generation is single-threaded,
      and the rounds regenerate whole bodies: the next levers are
      structural.

241. **More of Homebrew's `brew info` stub list (#100), as general
    *See also decision 291*.
    features** (amends 4, 141, 227, 233; 2026-10-13). Each was a stub on
    brew's traced path; each now works in any program, checked against MRI
    in `testdata/test` (control, object, string, stdlib).
    - **`[*x]` of a non-Array** (`Tap.fetch`, `uses_from_macos`,
      `root_url_if_match`): `x.to_a` spread when x's class answers `to_a`
      (MatchData, Hash, Range), the fields of a tuple, nothing for nil
      (`T?` checks at run time), else x itself (`[*"a"]` is `["a"]`).
    - **Read-only Array methods on a tuple** (`["to_str", :to_str].include?`,
      `pair.size`, `join`) answer on an Array of its joined fields in user
      code too, not only gem code (227). Mutators (`<<`, `push`, `[]=`, a
      `!` name...) and iterators (`each`) stay errors: they would act on a
      copy, or need statement form. `tuple.to_a` is that Array.
    - **`String#scan` with an interpolated pattern**: the groups are counted
      from its literal text (an interpolation counts as plain text), so
      `%r{/#{x}(\d+)}` yields each match's groups as a literal would.
    - **`x &&= v` on a local** is `x && (x = v)`, as `@x &&= v` already was.
    - **`@@x` resolves lexically too**: a module's method copied into a
      class that prepends or includes it reads the module's class variable
      (Ruby's cref), when the class's own ancestry has none.
    - **`super` in a `respond_to?` override** is Object's answer, by name at
      run time (`rbRespondsByName`); `respond_to?(:initialize, true)`
      through it answers false (ponytail: the by-name table has no private
      `initialize`).
    - **`x.singleton_class < M`** (and `<=`, `include?`) for a module M is
      `x.is_a?(M)`, nil instead of false for `<`/`<=` as MRI's unrelated
      comparison; no singleton class object exists.
    - **`break` in a closure block** (amends 4): a valueless `break` that
      leaves a block passed by a call *statement* panics `rbBreak{id}` (the
      block's file and offset), and that statement, wrapped in
      `func() { defer rbBreakRecover(id); ... }()`, recovers it. `ensure`
      runs; `rescue` passes `rbBreak` (and `rbIterBreak`, which a catch-all
      rescue used to swallow) on. When the call compiles to a loop the
      break is a Go break and nothing is wrapped. A `break` whose call's
      value is used, or `break v`, stays a compile error.
    - **Subprocesses** (233): a receiverless `exit!` calls the prelude's
      `__exit_bang` (a public `exit!` joined every Kernel dispatcher: +35
      functions in `64_observable`); `Process.detach`'s thread answers
      `pid` (a field on Thread; any other thread raises NoMethodError, so
      only `respond_to?(:pid)` differs from MRI's `Process::Waiter`
      subclass, which cost +93 functions as a class); `IO.popen(cmd, mode,
      opts)` takes spawn's options and an env Hash leading an Array
      command, or options as the Array's last element, sharing
      `rbSpawnCmd` with `Process.spawn`. Its `opts` defaults to `{}`: a
      `Hash?` parameter added `**Hash` arms to every program's unbox
      switches (+83 compiled functions in `05_word_count`).
    - **Build cost** (`TestCompileCost`): `05_word_count` and `116_cuba_web`
      unchanged; `64_observable` +3 (Thread#pid, kept by its dynamic sends).
    - **`raise err, msg[, backtrace]`** with err an exception object raises
      `err.exception(msg)` (a copy with msg), as MRI; `set_backtrace`
      takes nil. **`e = nil` widened by a later `e = err`** no longer fails
      `raise e` in the first local-analysis pass, which reads e before the
      write widens it.
    - **Destructuring a union** (`a, b = str_or_pair_or_nil`): a type switch
      gives each target what each member gives it (a tuple's field, an
      Array's element, a non-Array the first target alone, nil the rest),
      typed as their join. An optional tuple takes fewer targets with a bare
      rest (`tap, = pair_or_nil`).
    - **The pruner kept no type-switch arm naming a tuple**: `TupleN` is
      declared in a later batch than the code that names it, so the arm
      read as a never-loaded lib type (155) and was dropped, silently, from
      every union switch with a tuple member. Tuple names are exempt.
    - **`@x ||= v` on a union holding nil** reads as the union without nil
      (one member left: asserted to it), as `T?` already read as T.
    - **A gem's local retyped** (`uri = ref.to_s; uri = URI(uri)`) reads as
      its widened join (a union, or untyped) after the write in the first
      local-analysis pass too, not only in the second.
    - **`\Z` is translated** (amends 24): `(?:\n?\z)`, the end or before a
      final newline. RE2 has no lookahead, so the newline is part of the
      match: `$&`, `post_match` and a capture-less `str[re]` hold it where
      MRI's do not; whether a string matches, and every group, is the same.
      Homebrew writes `\Z` 46 times, some in patterns built at run time.
    - **Smaller gem leniencies**: nil where a gem's sig says a non-nil union
      passes as nil (MRI without Sorbet's run-time checks); a wider union
      into a narrower one is checked on arrival; `Array[untyped]` where the
      sig says `Array[A | B]` is the same Go type and passes as it is; a
      block's untyped value where the method wants `Array[U]` (`flat_map`)
      binds U untyped. **Core additions**: `Errno::ESRCH`/`EPERM` (kill's
      errors) in `prelude/errno_process.rb`, a lib with no require of its
      own that loads when a program names either constant (as core classes
      they cost every dynamic program ~27 functions each; a program that
      rescues only SystemCallError still gets IOError from kill, as before),
      `Process.setpgid`, `Dir.mktmpdir(prefix, Pathname)`;
      `Kernel#fork` is typed `Integer?` and `Thread.fork` is rewritten to
      `Thread.start` (MRI's alias), so the two no longer collide.
    - **Homebrew's load-time blockers** (found with `RB2GO_GEM_WARNINGS=1`,
      which prints the warnings gem files otherwise keep quiet: nil
      constants, skipped statements):
      `reduce(&:merge)` (a `&:sym` block taking several values calls the
      first with the rest as arguments); a gem method's param reassigned
      what its type does not hold (`rebuild = ".#{rebuild}"`) works on a
      widened copy from the second pass on; a gem block's top-level
      `p = v` of its own param starts a new local for the rest of the block
      (its Go type is the block signature's), unless an earlier closure
      captured p; `::X = v` / `Other::X = v` in a body declares that
      constant; a top-level `Klass.private_constant :X` is as inside Klass;
      a gem block's params past what is yielded are nil (a defaulted
      `|access = "Accessibility"|` yielded nothing); a Proc reaches
      Object's methods through untyped dispatch.
    - **An override adding a keyword** (`install!(name, with: nil, ...,
      **_options)` over `install!(name, ..., **options)`, amends 8): when
      both take keywords, the adapter calls the override with the slot's
      positionals, its keywords by name and its `**rest` splatted, so the
      override's own keyword comes from the caller's options, as MRI binds
      it. ponytail: a slot keyword with a callee-side default is passed as
      given.
    - **`xs + ys` / `xs | ys` of different element types** (`Array[Symbol] +
      Array[String]`) converts both to an Array of the join first; Array#+
      is typed `Array[T]` on both sides.
    - **Sorbet's `mixes_in_class_methods(M)`** in a module: each class
      including the module extends M at link, as Sorbet's included hook does
      at run time (`UnpackStrategy::ClassMethods`).

242. **Regexps RE2 cannot match run on regexp2** (amends 24; 2026-10-13). *See also decision 326* (purego, the second runtime module).
    Homebrew writes lookaround 38 times and `\Z` 46 times; decision 24
    rejected both, and a hand-made lookaround on the in-house backtracker
    would have been one more special case. The user approved one dependency
    for this: `github.com/dlclark/regexp2` (a .NET-style backtracking
    engine).
    - **RE2 first.** `rxCompile` compiles the translated pattern with RE2
      (linear time) and falls back to regexp2 only when RE2 rejects it:
      lookahead and lookbehind, backreferences (`\1`, `\k<n>`, `\k'n'`),
      atomic groups `(?>...)`, inline `(?x)`. regexp2 runs in its RE2 mode,
      so `\w`, `\d` and `\s` stay ASCII as Ruby's; the translator's output
      (`\x{...}`, `\p{...}`, `(?m)`/`(?s)`) reads the same in both.
      Offsets are converted from regexp2's runes to bytes (`rxAlt2`).
    - **`\Z` is exact** now: `(?=\n?\z)` (241 matched the newline).
    - **Still rejected**: `\g<n>` (regexp2 would read a literal `g`),
      possessive quantifiers, the absent operator, `\G`/`\K`/`\R`/`\X`.
    - **Only programs that may need it get it.** `rxalt.go` (the adapter,
      which registers `rxAltCompile`) is emitted into a program, and
      regexp2 imported, only when a pattern literal needs it, an
      interpolated pattern embeds another pattern's text (`.source`, a
      Regexp or untyped value), `Regexp.new`/`compile`/`union` gets
      anything but literals RE2 compiles, or gem code builds patterns at
      run time. Prelude code does not count. Package-level pattern
      variables initialize through `rbRegexpNewAfter(rxAltReady, ...)`, since
      Go runs `init` after them. Every generated module's `go.mod` requires
      regexp2 (`rb2go.WriteModule`, with its `go.sum` lines); Go loads it
      only when `main.go` imports it, from the module cache.
    - **Build cost**: `05_word_count` unchanged; `64_observable` +2 (the
      routing in `rxRegexp`); `116_cuba_web` gets the engine (rack builds
      patterns at run time).
    - **Kept**: the in-house backtracker for Onigmo's `^` (never after a
      final newline; regexp2's multiline `^` would match there) and its
      Unicode `\b`, and `rxFold`'s multi-character case folds (regexp2
      does not fold `ß`/`SS` either).
    - **More of brew's load path** (2026-10-13, after 242):
      `ENV.fetch(k, d)` is `ENV[k] || d` (an ENV value is a String, so this
      is fetch's answer for any d: nil, false, a String); a mixed-in `inherited` calling `super` reaches Class's
      no-op (hook bridges return a zero value); a metaclass carrying a
      mixed-in module's ivars answers `instance_variable_set`/`get`, so an
      `inherited` hook's `child.instance_eval { @env = ... }` works;
      `h.merge(g)` of different value types joins them, as `Array#+` does
      (in gem code an untyped side makes the result untyped); a gem's
      blank-slate `(instance_methods - ...).each { |m| undef_method m }` is
      skipped (ponytail: the methods stay; Homebrew's InstallSteps DSL uses
      it only as a sandbox); a T::Struct's props declared by a load-time
      loop under computed names (Homebrew's FormulaStruct predicates) come
      from the boot snapshot, which now records each T::Struct's props
      (name, Sorbet type text, literal default), and the loop is skipped;
      `def self.included(base) = base.extend(M)` is
      mixes_in_class_methods(M); a class-body call of a class method whose
      body is `extend M` (`safe_initialization!`) extends that class at
      load, and a run-time `extend M` is a check that the class has M;
      `$1`/`$~` inside a `sub`/`gsub` block are that match; a gem block's
      or lambda's `&blk` param is a nil local (ponytail: the caller's block
      is dropped) and a gem lambda's defaults bind as a block's do.
    - **`A, B = 1, 2` in a body** declares each constant. **A gem method's
      param guessed from its default** (`level = :FATAL`) that is
      reassigned an untyped value (`const_get(level)`) is widened to
      untyped, as one reassigned another type already was; a signed
      param keeps its type.

243. **Calls with a block on an untyped receiver, in gem code** (#100's
    "blocks on untyped receivers, beyond Enumerable"; extends 176, 224).
    concurrent-ruby's mixins call `synchronize { ... }` on self, which their
    includers define. The call passes its block as a last argument,
    `rbBlockArg{fn: func(...any) any}` (the block's params untyped, missing
    ones nil). Dyn wrappers are emitted for block-taking methods, but only
    for names some gem call site sends with a block (`dynBlockNames`), so
    other programs gain nothing. The wrapper pops the block
    (`rbPopBlock`) and adapts it to the method's typed block, each value
    boxed in and the result asserted out; a block-optional method gets nil
    when the call passed none. An iterator method is ranged over, each
    yield into the block, and the wrapper answers the last block value, as
    `synchronize`'s yield-once does in MRI. Not covered: blocks with a rest
    or destructuring parameter, keyword-taking iterators, blocks bound to
    another self.
    Block-taking generics (`Mutex#synchronize`'s T) take their type
    params untyped there; Kernel's own (`tap`, `then`, `open`) get no
    wrapper, since one would land on every class (+5,000 functions in brew;
    a class's own override still gets one).
    Also on brew's path (decision 241's list): a reopened TrueClass,
    FalseClass or NilClass method answers for true/false/nil values ahead
    of Object's (`false.present?` with brew's `FalseClass#present?`), by
    the value's truth for a Boolean; a gem hook's (`included`, `extended`,
    `inherited`) base parameter is untyped; a traced entry's `$0` is its
    `rb2go_main` file, so brew.rb's `$PROGRAM_NAME == __FILE__` guard
    holds; a union switch whose arms answer nil or void declares its
    result `any`.
    - See also 281: bound and typed forwarded blocks.
    *See also decision 295:* a computed send's block may be any Proc value; block-less wrappers drop it.

    - See also decision 302: a literal block of two or more parameters auto-splats a lone Array argument.
    *See also decision 307:* an optional typed block is passed as the adapter itself, nil when the call gave none.
244. **Toward compiling MRI's own optparse.rb for Homebrew** (#99; 2026-10-13).
    Homebrew's CLI::Parser reaches into OptionParser (`base.long`,
    `make_switch`'s Switch, `summary_indent`, `Array` coercion), which
    rb2go's 217-line prelude copy lacks. Following "prefer real gems", the
    goal is MRI's unmodified 2,463-line optparse.rb compiled as gem code;
    a probe of it (lib copy, gem mode) now builds and runs into
    `make_switch`'s inner workings. General features on the way:
    - `class NoArgument < self` names the enclosing class.
    - A constant assigned inside another's value
      (`NoArgument = [NO_ARGUMENT = :NONE, nil]`) is its own constant,
      initialized just before.
    - `$!` in a rescue clause is the exception it handles (elsewhere nil:
      ponytail).
    - `case s when /re/` in a frame that reads `$~`/`$1` is an if/elsif
      chain of `re =~ s` tests, in order, so the matching arm reads its own
      match (a non-String subject is tested `String === s` first; `when
      Class` becomes `s.is_a?(Class)`, which narrows a local subject;
      `when *list` is `list.include?(s)`, ponytail: `==` per element).
    - `Array === x` in a condition narrows a local x as `x.is_a?(Array)`.
    - `q, a = nil` assigns nil to each target.
    - `String.try_convert`, `Array.try_convert` (untyped, so no optional
      Array type joins every program's unbox switches), `Enumerable#collect`.
    - Gem leniency: a computed send passing a block (an rbBlockArg, and
      every block-taking method a computed name reaches gets a block
      wrapper); an untyped, union or optional receiver's `method(:x)`, and
      an untyped Method's `to_proc` (a variadic Proc calling it
      dynamically); `(o = v).bang!`; a void call's value used is nil; a
      `when` arm that does not compile raises NotImplementedError if
      reached, rather than costing its method; a gem's top-level
      `ARGV.extend(M)` is dropped (ponytail); parameter inference takes a
      gem caller's untyped container over a narrower type from another
      call, and makes a parameter untyped when gem calls pass kinds that do
      not join; a gem's nil-only local is hoisted as untyped.
    - Codegen bugs: a failed `try` left the locals its attempt declared in
      scope (an assignment inside a splat argument then had no
      declaration); a stubbed iterator lost its `return func(yield ...)`
      opener; a widened param's copy was taken before its rest param was
      bound. `RB2GO_GOPOISON_DUMP=path` writes each Go-stubbing round's
      code before stubbing (`path.0`, ...), for debugging.
    - Build cost: `05_word_count` and `116_cuba_web` unchanged;
      `64_observable` +8 (its dynamic sends keep every public method of a
      live class: `collect`, `try_convert`).
    Not yet: the rest of optparse.rb (lambdas and blocks with rest
    parameters on dynamic calls, `$DEBUG`, `compact!` on an untyped Array,
    ...), and routing brew's `require "optparse"` to it.

245. **Extend the prelude OptionParser for Homebrew's CLI parser, instead of compiling optparse.rb** (#99; 2026-10-13). This was the user's choice: 244's path through MRI's file needed too many dynamic features for what brew uses.
    - `OptionParser::NO_ARGUMENT`/`REQUIRED_ARGUMENT`/`OPTIONAL_ARGUMENT`
      (`:NONE`/`:REQUIRED`/`:OPTIONAL`). A literal constant in `on` types
      the block like a `--x X` string (decision 101). At run time,
      `rbOptBuild` reads the Symbol and an `Array` class argument, so a
      runtime-decided switch coerces too.
    - `on` with switch strings that are not literals (`*names`, a local)
      stays the untyped `on`. A String first argument no longer selects
      the class twin `__on_string`: OptionParser's on-methods overload by
      switch kind, never by argument class (`optOn`).
    - `summary_indent=`, `base.long.delete(name)` (removing the officious
      `--help`/`--version`, recorded in the parser's `off` set),
      `make_switch(opts) -> [Switch]` and `OptionParser::Switch` with
      `short`/`long`/`desc`/`arg` and `summarize` in MRI's layout: what
      Homebrew::CLI::Parser reads to build its help.
    - Not covered: completion lists, patterns, `OptionParser::Switch`
      subclasses, and `on` returning the parser for chaining with a switch
      object.
    - Build cost: `05_word_count` unchanged (OptionParser loads only on `require "optparse"`).
    *See also decision 293:* run-time OPTIONAL_ARGUMENT switches: true when omitted, placed arguments, unboxed values.

246. **Brew's CLI parser setup: Sorbet `Object`, `|a,|` blocks, symbol literals for computed sends** (#100; 2026-10-13).
    - A Sorbet sig's `Object`/`BasicObject` is `untyped`. Go already spelt
      them `any`, but the class type kept an override's
      `T::Array[Object]` from adapting a parent's `T::Array[Entry]`
      (Homebrew::Bundle::VscodeExtension.cleanup_items: the subclass lost the
      parent's interface slot and the program did not build).
    - `|a,|` (an implicit rest) splats a yielded Array as `|a, *|` does.
      It had bound the whole element, as `|a|` does: a silent miscompile
      in user code too (`pairs.reject! { |name,| ... }`).
    - The symbol-literal scan behind decision 235 (prelude methods count
      for a gem's computed send only when a gem or user file writes their
      name as a symbol) never captured a name: `:x` did not match and
      `&:x` captured "". So `public_send(method, ...)` with `method:
      :on_tail` found no `on_tail`. Build cost: `05_word_count` and
      `64_observable` unchanged, `116_cuba_web` +42 functions (names it
      was missing).
    - A require of a name built at run time, under decision 197's
      `rb2go_method_requires: runtime` (receiverless, or `Kernel.require`):
      `rbRequireComputed` switches over the compiled files' absolute paths
      and their names relative to each -I or gem dir (with or without
      `.rb`), then the prelude's and MRI's library names, and returns false
      (loaded at start); any other name raises MRI's LoadError. It had
      returned false for every name, so Homebrew's
      `Commands.valid_internal_cmd?` (`Utils::Ruby.require?(path)`) could
      not tell a missing command from a compiled one. The switch is
      emitted only when such a require is compiled.
    - A traced entry's `rb2go_main` path is made absolute, so `__dir__` is
      absolute, as MRI's. `TestTracedEntry` (testdata/traced) is the first
      test of a decision 197 entry.
    - `Module#include?(mod)`: whether mod is among self's ancestors'
      modules; a non-module argument raises MRI's TypeError (brew.rb's
      `cmd_class&.include?(Homebrew::ShellCommand)`). Written in Go: as Ruby,
      its `raise` with `mod.class` cost `64_observable` +1,216 functions
      through the dynamic `include?` dispatcher; in Go it costs +155.
    - An entry's `# rb2go_fold: expr => bool` facts (decision 197) fold
      `if`/`unless` conditions in method bodies too, not only class bodies:
      a fact, or `&&`, `||`, `!` and parentheses over facts, decided only
      when the facts decide it (an unknown side is never dropped). Only the
      taken branch is compiled. Homebrew's `Utils.popen` forks with
      `IO.popen("-")`, which a Go program cannot do; the brew entry folds
      `ENV["HOMEBREW_SPAWN_SYSTEM"] == "1" => true`, Homebrew's own spawn
      path, so the fork branch is never compiled. `OS.mac?`/`OS.linux?`
      now drop macOS-only method branches as well.
    - Brew harness: `utils/backtrace`, required only on brew.rb's error
      path, is added to the traced entry.

247. **Non-local `return` from a closure block in gem code** (#100; 2026-10-13). Amends decision 4.
    - A gem method (decision 172) whose block, compiled as a Go closure,
      `return`s now returns from the method, as MRI does, instead of
      raising NotImplementedError when it runs. The method allocates a
      per-call token (`rbRetTok_ := new(byte)`) and defers
      `rbReturnRecover`. The block's `return v` panics `&rbReturn{tok, v}`
      with v coerced to the method's return type, and the recover sets the
      named result `ret_`. The token, unlike decision 241's `rbBreak` id,
      keeps a recursive call of the same method from taking another call's
      return.
    - `rescue` passes rbReturn on, as it does rbBreak (rbWrapPanic), so
      Homebrew's `IO.popen(...) do |pipe| return pipe.read ...; rescue
      Exception; ...; raise; end` returns, and `ensure` runs.
    - Return-type inference counts these returns (a bare `return` is nil).
    - `lambda do return end` and nested defs are not matched. An iterator
      method's blocks are loops, where return is plain Go already.
    - Not covered: `return a, b` from a block (still the decision 4 stub),
      and a block that returns after its method has returned (MRI's
      LocalJumpError; here the panic is uncaught). User code keeps decision
      4's compile error.
    - Build cost: none for programs without such a method. rbReturn is
      non-comparable (`_ [0]func()`), so rbWrapPanic's case for it adds no
      generated equality function (`05_word_count` stays 1178).

248. **Brew batch: nilable `T.let`, `||=` after a branch assignment, forwarded rest blocks, `tap`, top-level `ENV.extend`** (#100; 2026-10-13).
    - `@x = T.let(v, T)` (an annotated ivar write) of a `T?` value no
      longer relabels it as T (the Go code kept the pointer). In gem code
      it unwraps with `rbMust`, which raises TypeError on nil as
      sorbet-runtime's T.let does. In user code it is decision 20's
      possibly-nil error.
    - `x ||= v` reads x first (definite-assignment walk, decision 14). A
      local assigned on some paths only (`libs, deps = f.linkage if
      installed; libs ||= []`) is T? there, so `||=` tests it. `||=` also
      finds a local assigned in an earlier branch (`sameScopeLocal`), as
      reads and plain assignments do. It had overwritten the branch's
      value unconditionally: a silent miscompile in user code too.
    - Gem code, concurrent-ruby's executors and promises:
      - A method's own `&block` passed to a method on an untyped receiver
        or self (`ns_initialize(opts, &block)`) goes through the dynamic
        call as an rbBlockArg. A callee's unannotated `&block`, typed
        `(*untyped) -> untyped`, gets a Dyn wrapper that passes that func
        through, nil when the call gave none.
      - A block forwarded across a variadic difference (`&task` typed
        `func(any)` from its callers, into a `(*untyped) -> untyped`
        block, or back) is adapted (`restBlockAdapter`).
      - `task.call(*args)` on a `(*untyped)` Proc spreads the splat into
        the Go variadic call instead of a dynamic call.
    - A method's own void block forwarded where the callee's block returns
      an unbound type variable (`return super if block_given?` in
      Homebrew's Cask#tap and CLI::Args#tap, over Kernel#tap's `[X] {
      (self) -> X }`) binds X to untyped, the block adapted to return nil.
      Go could not infer X from a func returning nothing. (Typing tap's
      block void instead gave every class a tap forwarder.)
    - Pruner: an interface that type switches or assertions test is never
      slimmed to empty, which would match every value. It keeps one method
      no type has (`_rbNever()`). `rbClassOf`'s `case Method_Any:` had
      answered Method for every object in programs without Method values.
    - A top-level `ENV.extend(M)` / `$stdout.extend(M)` includes M into
      the one object's class (decision 229's handling), as in a class body.
      It was dropped as an object-constant extend (decision 244), so
      Homebrew's EnvActivation and EnvSensitive methods were missing.
    - A gem's `each_key do ... end` (an iterator method) on an untyped or
      module self (`dynEnumBlock`) runs as a range loop in each branch,
      valued its receiver, instead of raising NoMethodError.
    - `noteConv` ignores a nil node (an adapter's own conversion).
    - Build cost: `05_word_count` 1178, `64_observable` and
      `116_cuba_web` unchanged.

249. **Brew batch: dynamic override slots, module overrides, retried param widening, guard on `(x = v).nil?`, untyped splats, `decorator.all_props`** (#100; 2026-10-13).
    - A gem override that no typed adapter fits (concurrent-ruby's
      `TimerSet#post(delay, *args, &task)` over
      `RubyExecutorService#post(*args, &task)`) no longer drops the
      parent's slot, which left the class failing the parent's interface.
      The slot is kept as dynamic: its adapter boxes the positional
      arguments and an untyped block, calls the override through
      `rbDyn<Name>`, and converts the result. So a parent method calling
      `post` on a TimerSet runs TimerSet#post, as in MRI. Only for a
      superclass's slot (a module's is its Self constraint's, which names
      the module's own signature), not `<=>`'s Op_cmp (emitCmpAdapter's),
      and for overrides without keywords, callee defaults, iterators or
      typed blocks (`dynAdaptable`).
    - An included gem module's method that overrides a superclass method
      of an includer with another Go signature
      (`ConditionSignalling#ns_broadcast` returning self over
      `AbstractLockableObject#ns_broadcast`) gets its own Go name
      (`NsBroadcast_ofConditionSignalling`, shared by every includer), and
      the includer answers the superclass's slot through an adapter, as a
      class's own override does (decision 8).
    - A gem parameter reassigned what its type does not hold (decision
      241) is widened from the next run, but that same run then failed at
      the next use and took the whole method down. `analyze` now reruns a
      failed pass 1 with the widenings it found. Homebrew's
      `SystemCommand#each_line_from` rebinds `sources: T::Array[IO]` to a
      Hash. An iterator call on such a union (`sources.each_key`) raises
      NoMethodError in the arms that lack the method instead of failing to
      compile.
    - `return if (data = build).nil?` narrows data after it, as `return if
      data.nil?` does (user code too).
    - Gem code: `[*x]` of an untyped x spreads at run time (`rbSplatAny`:
      nil nothing, an Array its elements, a Hash its pairs, else x), and
      `**h` of an untyped h converts to `Hash[untyped, untyped]`.
    - A T::Struct answers sorbet-runtime's `decorator.all_props`: the
      synthesized `self.decorator` is the class and `self.all_props` the
      props, boot's included (Homebrew's `FormulaStruct.from_hash` slices
      by it). ponytail: the class itself answers all_props, which MRI's
      does not.

250. **Brew batch: typed-container `T.let`, value blocks into void ones, `yield(*args)`, untyped blocks in module slots** (#100; 2026-10-13).
    - `@x = T.let(v, T)` of a same-class container with untyped elements
      (`T.let(Array(input), T::Array[String])`, Homebrew's SystemCommand)
      converts a copy in gem code (`lenientClass`, decision 172), where it
      had relabeled the value.
    - Gem code:
      - A method's own value-returning block forwarded where the callee's
        block is void (concurrent-ruby's `ns_execute` passing `&task` to
        `fallback_action`) is adapted, its value dropped.
      - `yield(*args)` to a block of fixed parameters spreads the values
        into one untyped slice, each parameter its position, nil past the
        end.
      - An override whose block its callers typed (`func(any) any`) where
        the slot's is the untyped `(*untyped) -> untyped`
        (concurrent-ruby's `IVar#add_observer` over
        `Concern::Observable#add_observer`) takes the slot's block type,
        which is wider, so it keeps the slot. The module's Self constraint
        had failed at every instantiation.
      - `rbDynProcCall` calls such a Proc held untyped
        (`observer.public_send(:call, v)`), arguments as they are.

251. **Brew batch: blocks passed to blockless methods, module slots through a superclass, last-resort lenient branches** (#100; 2026-10-13).
    - A Dyn wrapper of a method that takes no block drops a block the
      dynamic call passed (`rbPopBlock`), as MRI ignores it.
      concurrent-ruby's `ns_initialize(opts, &block)` reaches
      `RubyThreadPoolExecutor#ns_initialize(opts)` and had raised
      ArgumentError (given 2, expected 1).
    - Dynamic override slots (decision 249) are refused only for a module
      the overriding class includes itself. A module method seen through
      a superclass (concurrent-ruby's ExecutorService#post, included by
      AbstractExecutorService) is that superclass's interface slot, so
      RubyExecutorService and TimerSet answer it. A slot's typed block
      reaches the override's Dyn wrapper as an untyped rbBlockArg, each
      value asserted in. A class's own override takes the slot's untyped
      block as decision 250's module overrides do.
    - A gem method that does not compile is retried, before it is
      poisoned (decision 205), with each if/else branch that does not
      compile raising NotImplementedError if reached
      (`fctx.branchLenient`). concurrent-ruby's
      `CachedThreadPool#ns_initialize` has an `if Concurrent.on_jruby?`
      body calling `java.util...`. Only as a retry, since a branch's error
      is what drives local retyping (`xs = ["a"] if flag`).
    - ponytail: an unannotated gem override typed by its parent's return
      (decision 36's inherited signature) gives that type's zero value
      where its body gives nil (the `if` above, nil on MRI). Fix with a T?
      slot and an adapter.
    - Brew harness: the entry folds `Concurrent.on_jruby?` and
      `on_truffleruby?` to false and `on_cruby?` to true (decision 246's
      method-body folds).

252. **Brew batch: nil's arm of a `Self` result, gem locals rebound to another type, gem locals always used** (#100; 2026-10-13).
    - A call on a `T?` whose method is a program's Object method (Homebrew's
      blank.rb `presence`, `self if present?`) runs it on a typed-nil
      NilClass in the nil arm (decision 186). A `NilClass`/`NilClass?`
      result there is nil, not that receiver, which Go saw as a non-nil
      value. `curlrc&.start_with?("/")` had raised on a nil curlrc.
    - Gem code: a local rebound to a value its type does not hold (`data =
      { "version" => 2, **fingerprint, **data }` over a typed Hash in
      PackagesIndex.write!, `rebuild = ".#{rebuild}"`) is generated as
      itself when generating it as the local's type fails, and the local
      widens (decision 233).
    - Gem code: every declared local is marked used (`_ = x`). A read can
      sit in an arm or branch that raises instead of compiling (decisions
      244, 251), which left Go's "declared and not used"
      (CurlDownloadStrategy's `parse_content_disposition` lambda).

253. **concurrent-ruby's executors and Promises: private dispatch, template keys, alias of new, shadowed Kernel methods** (#100; 2026-10-14). *See also decision 297* (a module's includers count as overriders), *298* (initialize through an interface).
    Driven by compiling Homebrew's vendored concurrent-ruby directly (a
    FixedThreadPool, `future_on`, `then_on`, an Event): a one-minute loop
    instead of a brew round.
    - A private method a subclass overrides dispatches on self, as MRI's
      (`staticPrivate`). It had been called directly, the base's method
      always: a silent miscompile in user code too (`private def hook`
      overridden by a subclass). An override of such a method (private or
      not) answers its slot.
    - Parameter inference keys add the method's owner: the defs one
      `class_eval` template makes in several classes share a source
      position (attr_atomic's accessors), and one class's argument types
      had typed every class's.
    - `singleton_class.send(:alias_method, :[], :new)` /
      `singleton_class.alias_method` in a class body is a class-method
      alias. An alias of the synthesized `new` is a class method passing
      initialize's required parameters to new, untyped, so nil and any
      value pass as in MRI.
    - Gem code:
      - A class method's `new(...)` on self, where a subclass defines its
        own initialize, builds the class self is at run time
        (`inheritedSelfNew`).
      - A call that resolves to a prelude Kernel/Object method on a struct
        class some subclass defines the name on (concurrent-ruby's Event
        `alias then chain` over Kernel#then) dispatches at run time.
      - An untyped argument into a rest parameter makes its elements
        untyped (`paramUse.anyRest`).
      - `x.nil?` on a struct-class value checks it at run time
        (`lenientObject`), as such a value may hold an untyped nil.
      - The vendored signature `sig/gems/concurrent-ruby` types
        `MutexAtomicReference`'s value, compare-and-set and update as
        untyped (one class holds every attr_atomic field's values), and
        `Concurrent.monotonic_time` as Float.
    - Runtime: `rbAsSlow` converts nil to an object (interface) type as
      nil, where it raised TypeError. MRI never type-checks an object
      argument; a call on the nil raises there.
    - Prelude: `Process.clock_gettime(id, unit)` (MRI's units, untyped
      result), and `ConditionVariable#wait(mutex, timeout)` with a nil or
      Integer timeout.
    - A value-type class answering `to_path` (Pathname) where a path
      String is taken converts through to_path (implicitConv), as MRI's
      path parameters do.
    - Brew harness: the entry requires `concurrent/options`, an autoload
      boot never triggers.
    - Not yet: the Promises chain past a worker's run_task (a nil into a
      Float in Logging#log), so `brew info`'s download queue does not
      finish yet.

254. **Brew batch: Pathname into a dynamic String parameter, Kernel calls on main** (#100; 2026-10-14).
    - A dynamic call's String parameter (`rbAsSlow`) takes a Pathname as
      its path (`File.basename(self)` in Homebrew's Pathname#extname when
      the call is dynamic). A single-type `case Pathname`, so programs
      without Pathname drop it. ponytail: any String parameter takes it,
      where MRI's non-path parameters raise TypeError.
    - Decision 253's run-time dispatch of a Kernel method shadowed by a
      subclass skips Object and the other universal classes: main's `exit
      1` (Thread defines exit) had gone to a dispatcher with no arm for
      main.
    - A Dyn wrapper calls a method with post parameters (`evaluate_to(*args,
      block)`): the leading parameters from the front of the arguments,
      the posts from the back, the rest between (`emitDynPostCall`;
      required, rest and post parameters only). It had raised
      ArgumentError (the decision 23 ponytail), killing concurrent-ruby's
      worker threads.
    - Prelude: `Queue#pop(timeout:)` (and shift/deq) take a nil, Integer
      or Float timeout; nil waits, as concurrent-ruby's idle workers pass.
    - With these, Homebrew's vendored concurrent-ruby runs a
      FixedThreadPool, `future_on`, `then_on`, `on_resolution!`, `value!`
      and `wait_for_termination` as MRI does.
    - `String#[]` takes a Regexp (the match, or capture group `n`) and a
      String (itself when contained): class twins `__idx_regexp(re,
      capture = 0)` and `__idx_string`. A Dyn wrapper picks a twin taking
      another count of arguments than its method by the first argument's
      class, before the method's arity check (`dynWideClassOverloads`):
      Homebrew's `basename[/(\.tar\.gz)\Z/, 1]` on an untyped basename.

255. **Class and module comparison** (#100; 2026-10-14).
    - `C < D`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` on a value typed as a class or module
      object (a metaclass, Class or Module, as `using.is_a?(Class) &&
      using < AbstractDownloadStrategy` narrows it) compile to
      `rbModuleCmp`, an ancestry test: true or false when related, nil
      when unrelated, TypeError for a non-module, as MRI's. Not Module
      methods: through dynamic `<` every class object would answer them
      (+1,180 functions in `116_cuba_web`). ponytail: an untyped
      receiver's `<` on a class object is not answered.
    - The Dyn wrapper's wide class twins (decision 254) emit one `if` per
      argument count (gocritic rejects a one-case switch in generated
      code).
    - `String#[](re, capture)` takes a group name (Symbol or String) as
      well as an index (Homebrew's `ref[HOMEBREW_DEFAULT_TAP_FORMULA_REGEX,
      :name]`); the duplicate `__idx_regexp` decision 254 added to
      string.rb is gone (regexp.rb's is the one).
    - Gem code, from Homebrew's API download:
      - A Dyn wrapper of a synthesized `new` takes initialize's block
        (`dynCallBlock`). Decision 251's dropped block had left
        ChainPromise's @Task nil, so every future's task raised
        NoMethodError on nil.
      - `f(x, *args)` of an untyped args spreads at run time
        (`rbSplatAny`), as an Array literal's splat does.
      - `**opts` into typed keywords converts a value whose static type
        does not fit at run time (Utils::Curl's `**command_options`
        holding Strings, Booleans and Arrays), where it raised TypeError.

256. **Gem Hashes that gain another kind of key** (#100; 2026-10-14).
    - `h.merge(g)` in gem code where g's keys are another type than h's
      (`options.merge(Sandbox::INHERITANCE_FD => fd)`, an Integer into a
      `**options` Hash of Symbols) converts both sides to Hash[untyped,
      V] (V untyped too when the values differ) and merges those
      (`mixedArrayOp`); it had converted g's keys to h's at run time and
      raised.
    - A gem param given a Hash with untyped keys where its type's keys are
      typed widens to that Hash (`widenParam`): the converted copy fit by
      type but failed on another key.
    - A def inheriting its parent's signature (decision 36) takes the
      parent's block whole: `Rest`, `Self` and `Untyped` were dropped,
      so concurrent-ruby's ThenPromise#initialize took one value of the
      `(*untyped)` block BlockedTaskPromise takes, and `then_on(pool, a,
      b) { |v, x, y| }` saw x and y nil.

257. **Gem objects that hold nil their type does not say** (#100; 2026-10-14).
    - In gem code, a condition on a struct-class or pointer-typed value
      tests it against nil at run time, where it was always true: an
      ivar typed by an attr_writer's sig (`sig { params(x: IO) }`) is nil
      until set, and `rbAsSlow` (decision 253) passes nil into object
      types. Homebrew's `if (inheritance = @sandbox_inheritance)` had
      merged nil under an Integer key on every spawn.
    - A gem local rebound to a Hash of untyped keys from a typed-key Hash
      takes that type (`hashKeysWider`), as a param does (decision 256).
    - A gem method that names its block (`def post(*args, &task)`) keeps
      it `(*untyped) -> untyped` rather than borrowing an override's
      yield shape (`overrideYieldShape`): concurrent-ruby's
      RubyExecutorService#post took ImmediateExecutor's one-value block,
      so a posted `|args, task|` saw task nil and every brew download
      failed with "undefined method 'call' for nil". A splat of an
      Array whose element type Go spells `any` (a union) passes `.s`
      directly.
    - `reduce(nil) { |acc, x| ... }`: a type variable bound only by a
      nil argument takes what the block returns, made optional (`A` =
      `T?`), with the block's params re-substituted. It had stayed nil,
      so the block and a method ending in it compiled as void (user
      code too: `def f(xs) = xs.reduce(nil) { ... }` returned nothing).
    - In gem code, a `T?` where object type `T` is wanted passes nil on
      as a nil interface (`rbDerefOrNil`) instead of raising: MRI's
      variable holds nil, and gem truthiness already tests `!= nil`.
      Non-object `T` still raises (`rbMust`).
    - `Thread#raise` raises when called on the current thread;
      on another thread it does nothing (ponytail: an async interrupt
      needs a per-thread pending-exception check, which only
      Timeout-style code relies on).
258. **Brew downloads: Array#prepend, join(nil), pairs and to_str** (#100; 2026-10-14).
    - `Array#unshift` takes `*objs` and `prepend` is its alias, as in
      MRI (Homebrew's `args.prepend("--time-cond", target.to_s)`).
    - `Thread#join(nil)` waits as `join` does (`__join_1` takes
      `Float?`): Homebrew joins with `Utils::Timer.remaining(end_time)`,
      nil without a deadline, and every download raised "no implicit
      conversion of nil into Float".
    - In gem code, a tuple (a Hash arm's `|pair|` on an untyped
      receiver) where a non-Array class is wanted is checked at run time
      (`rbAs` of its Array) instead of being a Go type error that stubs
      the whole method: CaskDependent#deps' `formula.map { |f|
      Dependency.new f }`, MacOSRequirement's `map { |s|
      MacOSVersion.from_symbol(s) }`.
    - A value-typed class that defines `to_str` converts where a String
      is wanted (Homebrew's `class Pathname; alias to_str to_s; end`, so
      Mktemp#run's `Dir.chdir(@tmpdir)`); only to_int/to_ary/to_hash
      keep decision 179's core-value rule.
    - A generic value into a union that has its type variable as a
      member (`U` into `Array | Hash | String | U`) is boxed, not checked
      by a package-level helper that cannot name `U`.
259. **A fresh class object per `Class.new` evaluation** (#98, #100;
    amends 145; 2026-10-14). Homebrew's `load_formula_from_struct!` runs
    `Class.new(::Formula) do @x = ...; desc st.desc; define_method(:m)
    { ... } end` once per formula, the block reading the method's locals.
    - A non-module `Class.new` block whose statements are not all
      declarations is *per-run*: its declarations make the hidden class
      as before (one Go type per literal), and its other statements
      become the singleton method `rb2go_class_body`, whose parameters are
      the enclosing locals they read (an assignment to one is a compile
      error). Each evaluation makes a new metaclass object (`&X_Meta{}`),
      calls `Super.inherited` with it, runs the body on it, and is that
      object: class-level state lives on each object, as MRI's.
    - Instances made through that object's `new` keep it (`rbCls_`, set
      before `initialize` runs by `NewXIn`), so `self.class` is their own
      class object. A `new` reaching the type statically keeps the shared
      global one.
    - `define_method(:name) do ... end` in the block is a def of the
      hidden class at compile time when its block reads no enclosing
      local (decision 198's rebuild); otherwise a compile error.
    - In gem code an ivar a subclass assigns belongs to the topmost
      ancestor with a reader of it (Formula's `class << self;
      attr_reader :loaded_from_api`), not a shadowing field; metaclasses
      with ivars get `_Ivars`/`_IvarSet` so `child.instance_eval { @x =
      v }` on an untyped child sets them.
    - Still one Go type per literal: nothing but data is made at run
      time. `Module.new` stays decision 145's one module per literal.
    - See also 281: classes without initialize.
260. **Source names are sanitised, the bad-output dump is unique, require stays on the load path** (#101; 2026-10-14).
    - `parseFile` maps control characters, DEL, C1, U+2028/9, U+FEFF and
      invalid UTF-8 in a name to `_`. `File.Name` is what `//line`, the
      `// Code generated ... from` header, errors and `__FILE__` show, so
      a newline in a file name cannot end a comment and land as Go source.
      `File.path` keeps the real path for I/O.
    - Unparsable generated Go is dumped to `os.CreateTemp("",
      "rb2go-bad-output-*.go")`: a fixed name followed a planted symlink
      and was shared by concurrent compiles. A failed write is reported
      in the error, not claimed as written.
    - `require "x"` on the load path (decisions 131, 173) skips a join
      that escapes its -I or gem dir (`within(dir, p)`: rel is `..` or
      begins `../`); an absolute name loads as itself, as MRI does.
      `gemFile` uses the same test, so a first element named `..foo` is
      inside. The check is lexical; symlinks are followed.
    - Proof: `security_test.go` and `paths_test.go` in internal/compiler.
261. **Net::HTTP: the request path never names the host; connections are released** (#101; amends 27; 2026-10-14).
    - The path is MRI's request-target verbatim: `"@evil.com/x"` is sent as
      `GET @evil.com/x`, not folded into the URL where `host:port` would
      become userinfo. An empty path is `ArgumentError`
      ("HTTP request path is empty"). Paths starting with `/` keep their
      escaping and query handling.
    - Each request closes its own transport's idle connections (no
      `Connection: close` header, which servers can see), so a request
      loop no longer leaks an fd and two goroutines per call.
    - A timeout while reading the body is `Net::ReadTimeout`, as one
      classification (`rbHTTPFailure`) serves both the round trip and the
      body read.
262. **Compile-time boot runs the program: trust model and limits** (#101; amends 161, 173; 2026-10-14).
    *See also decision 296* (`-trace` runs the whole program, not only its load phase).
    - Compiling a gem-requiring program under `rb2go build|run|gen`
      EXECUTES its load phase under MRI at compile time: the entry file,
      and the Gemfile beside it (or `BUNDLE_GEMFILE`) via `bundler/setup`
      in `FindGems`. It runs with the user's full environment and cwd,
      as `ruby main.rb` does. Do not point rb2go at untrusted source.
    - There is no sandbox and no environment allowlist: an allowlist
      would break decision 161's baked-in ENV (the boot's ENV values are
      what the compiled program sees). The wasm playground never starts
      Ruby (`internal/boot` is stdlib-only and compiles into it, but with
      no file on disk `bootSources` skips `Capture`).
    - Limits, package variables so tests can shrink them: 10 minute
      timeout on each of `Capture` and `FindGems` (`context.WithTimeout`
      on the caller's ctx, the earlier deadline wins); `cmd.WaitDelay` of
      5 s so a pipe held by a grandchild cannot block `Wait` after the
      kill; stderr kept to its first 64 KiB with a truncation note; the
      manifest and `FindGems` output capped at 256 MiB.
    - The entry follows `--`, so a file named like an option is a file.
    - Known gaps: the kill reaches ruby only, not its grandchildren (a
      process-group kill needs build-tagged files, as this package also
      builds into wasm); the program's `$LOAD_PATH` still shows the
      hook's temp dir.
263. **Non-compile panics are internal errors** (#101; amends 154; 2026-10-14).
    - `compileWith` recovers three kinds of panic: `compileError` (a
      user-facing compile error), `needInfer` (another inference round)
      and anything else (a failed assertion, an index out of range),
      which is a compiler bug. A bug used to re-panic: the CLI died with
      a raw Go trace and the playground dropped the HTTP connection. It
      is now returned as `internal error: <value> while compiling
      <file>`, and `errors.Is(err, compiler.ErrInternal)` recognises it.
      `RB2GO_ERR_STACK=1` prints the stack to stderr, as it already did
      for compile errors. `runtime.Goexit`, `needInfer` and
      `compileError` are untouched.
    - Fuzz rule: `FuzzCompile` fails on `ErrInternal` (and on its own
      "compiler panic" catch), because a returned internal error would
      otherwise hide the bugs fuzzing exists to find. "Generated Go does
      not parse" is still tolerated, as before.
    - `loadPreludeGo` merges every prelude/go file's imports into
      `stdImports` once (before, only the first file's reached the
      table). A hand entry wins a base-name clash (`rand` is
      crypto/rand), and `TestPreludeImportsResolve` fails if an import is
      missing or collides.
    - `PreludeWhole` no longer sets the process environment; it passes
      `options.noPrune`. `RB2GO_NO_PRUNE` still works from the environment.
264. **Hostile and malformed input gives an error, not a crash** (#101; 2026-10-14).
    - A class cycle (`class A < B; class B < A`, `A < A`) and mutually
      including modules are a compile error (`inherits from itself
      (A < B < A)` / `module M includes itself (...)`), checked in `link`
      before any Super/Includes walk. Those walks (`metaFor`,
      `isSubclassOf`, `hasClassMethod`) would otherwise overflow the Go
      stack, which is fatal and uncatchable.
    - The RBS parser bounds type nesting at 200 (`maxDepth`) and nested
      declarations at 200, and returns a parse error instead of recursing.
    - `String()` of an RBS type reparses to the same structure: a Union or
      Proc nested in an Optional or Union is parenthesised, and the
      structural round trip is fuzz-checked. `IncludeDecl` has a kind
      (include/prepend/extend); the compiler does not read it yet.
    - `.rbs` signatures merge per class name: a reopened class keeps all
      its members, and on a conflicting method, attr, const or alias the
      first declaration wins. User sig dirs load before vendored gem sigs,
      so the user wins.
    - Boot `path:line` locations split at the last colon, so a gem path
      containing `:` is found; boot captures non-finite Floats as
      `Float::NAN`/`Float::INFINITY`/`-Float::INFINITY`.
    - Sorbet `params(**h)` and `prop :x, T, **o` are compile errors with
      `file:line`, not a nil dereference.
265. **gsub/sub/scan/split follow Onigmo; regexp2 times out; one pattern entry point** (#101; amends 24, 115, 242; 2026-10-14).
    - `String#gsub/sub/scan/split` take their matches from
      `rxRegexp.FindAllStringSubmatchIndex`, which applies the same Onigmo
      corrections as `Match`: a pattern that can match the empty string,
      or uses `^` or `\b`, runs on the in-house backtracker (RE2 only when
      no correction can apply), with Ruby's scan rule (an empty match is
      kept right after a match; after an empty match the next search
      starts one character on). `split` ports `rb_str_split_m` over those
      matches. `"a\n".gsub(/^/, "> ")` is `"> a\n"`, as MRI.
    - The regexp2 engine gets its `^` as `(?:\A|(?<=\n)(?!\z))` (Onigmo's
      `^` never matches after a final newline).
    - regexp2 matches stop after `rxMatchTimeout` (10 s) with
      `Regexp::TimeoutError` < `RegexpError` ("regexp match timeout"); any
      other regexp2 error is a `RegexpError`, never a short result.
      `Regexp.timeout=` and `timeout:` are not supported (ponytail:
      regexp2's MatchTimeout is per compiled regexp; a global would
      race).
    - `rxPrepare(src, extended)` = `stripExtended` + `translateRegexp` is
      the one entry point from Ruby source to a pattern (regexp.go,
      erb.go). `EXTENDED` and `"x"` in `Regexp.new` work; they no longer
      raise `NotImplementedError`. Known: `noteRuntimeRegexp` (expr.go)
      still calls `translateRegexp` raw.
    - Measured (20000-line string, 5 reps): `gsub(/\s*/)` 0.53 → 0.59 s,
      `scan(/\w*/)` 0.46 → 0.22 s, `gsub(/o+/)` 0.19 → 0.21 s,
      `gsub(/^/)` 0.13 → 0.24 s (MRI 1.22, 0.83, 0.10, 0.11).
266. **Integer edges: exact where the result fits, RangeError where MRI makes a Bignum** (#101; amends 35; 2026-10-14).
    - Where the true result fits Go `int`, rb2go returns MRI's answer
      exactly: iteration up to and including MaxInt/MinInt terminates
      (`Range#each`/`reverse_each`, `upto`, `downto`, `step`,
      ArithmeticSequence, `bsearch`); shifts by any count give 0/-1/MRI's
      value (`-1 << 63` fits); `gcd` is computed unsigned.
    - Where MRI makes a Bignum, rb2go raises `RangeError`:
      `Range#size`/`count`/`sum`, `upto`/`downto`/`step` sizes (counts
      past MaxInt: `(MinInt..MaxInt).size` is 2**64), `gcd` past MaxInt,
      `x << n` or `x >> -n` past 64 bits (`x >> MinInt` is `RangeError`
      where MRI raises `NoMemoryError`), endless iteration passing MaxInt,
      `format("%d"/"%x", Float)` past ±2**63. NaN and Inf stay
      `FloatDomainError`.
    - The loop helpers test the bound after the yield (`i == MaxInt`)
      instead of widening: one compare, no other runtime cost.
    - Not fixed: `Range#step(n) { }` with a block still walks every
      element; `(MinInt..MaxInt).min(2)` iterates the whole range.
267. **Enumerable and Lazy: streaming each_cons, one-pass minmax, more lazy steps** (#101; 2026-10-14).
    - `Enumerable#each_cons(n) { }` slides a window over `each` instead of
      calling `to_a`, so endless ranges and generators stop on `break`
      like `each_slice`. It still returns void: iterator methods cannot
      return `self` (decision 4).
    - `Enumerable#minmax` is one pass over `each` with MRI's pair ordering
      (enum.c `minmax_i`): a side-effecting `each` runs once, and ties
      keep the earlier element.
    - `Enumerator::Lazy` gains `find_all`, `grep`, `grep_v` (block form via
      `__grep_block`/`__grep_v_block`), `collect_concat`, `chunk_while`
