      and `slice_when`: they stay lazy and return a Lazy on infinite
      sources, and `collect` is labelled "collect". Steps are named as in
      MRI (`grep(2..5)`) through `__relabel`. The chunking steps' inspect
      shows `:chunk_while` where MRI prints a Generator address.
    - Eager `Enumerable#grep`/`grep_v` accept a block through the same
      twins (it was a compile error).
    - Proof: `enumerator_test.rb` StreamingTest and LazyMoreTest.
268. **Core container corrections: Array#<=>, Hash#merge/to_h, Range#include?/==** (#101; amends 5, 165, 236; 2026-10-14).
    - `Array#<=>` returns the sign of the length difference after a
      common prefix (-1/0/1). It still raises `ArgumentError` where MRI
      answers nil for incomparable elements: the signature is `Integer`,
      and `rbCmp` and sort rely on it (an `Integer?` would force nil
      handling on every `a <=> b` and slow sorts).
    - `Hash#merge` (typed, block form and the dynamic view path) starts
      from `dup`, so `default` and `default_proc` survive, as MRI's
      `rb_hash_dup`. The view protocol gains `_dup` (an 8th Hash accessor).
    - `Hash#to_h` is self, as MRI's and as the view path already was. A
      subclass's embedded Hash cannot tell it is one, so `to_h` there
      returns the embedded Hash, not MRI's plain copy (decision 165: not
      yet).
    - `Range#include?`/`member?` iterate String ranges with `rbStrUpto`, as
      `rb_str_include_range_p` (`("a".."z").include?("bb")` is false;
      endless/beginless raise `TypeError`). Everything else stays
      `cover?`, which matches MRI for numbers and Time (Date-like
      `succ` types still use `cover?`).
    - `Range#==` compares ends with `==` and handles other instantiations
      through the untyped view, so `(1..2) == (1.0..2.0)` is true. `eql?`
      still uses `eql?` on the ends.
    - Not done: multi-argument `Hash#merge`/`update`.
269. **Operand generation under nesting is polynomial, not exponential** (#101; amends 240 "what is left"; 2026-10-14).
    - `genOr`, `genUnionOr`, `genAnd`, `genSafeNav`, `nilClassCall` and
      `lift` each generated their operand twice (a dry run for its type,
      then for real), so d nested levels cost 2^d: `(a || (a || ... b))`
      at depth 22 took 26 s, `&.` with a block at depth 18 took 368 s.
    - Inside a probe the output is discarded, so `genOperandOnce`
      generates the operand once there (statements captured) and
      `operandAgain` replays it; `lift` does one branch-typing pass in a
      probe and skips the coerce pass. Outside a probe the dry run stays,
      so output, temp numbering and warnings are unchanged (generated Go
      byte-identical over `examples`, `testdata/run` and `testdata/test`;
      one warning is emitted fewer times, same set). Cost is quadratic in
      depth: the top-level real pass still dry-runs each level once
      (depth 22 `||`: 0.7 s).
    - `probe` and `capture` restore their state in a `defer`, so a compile
      error unwinding through a probe no longer leaks it.
    - Check: `TestNestedOperandsGenerateOnce` compiles 15 depth-14
      nestings and fails if `genExpr` calls pass the depth-1 count plus
      150*d^2 (`genCount`/`genLimit` on `options`; no wall clock).
    - Left: a linear fix needs a splice or a type memo (`coerce(l)`
      precedes the right side's temps in the output). The `gen.go` "always
      true" warning is still not deduped.
270. **Runtime system helpers hardened** (#101; amends 63, 66, 97, 107, 138; 2026-10-14).
    - `Open3.capture2` leaves the child's stderr on the program's, flushing
      stdout first (it went to /dev/null).
    - `IO.popen` with an empty or env-only command raises MRI's
      `ArgumentError` ("wrong number of arguments (given 0, expected 1+)")
      instead of indexing an empty slice.
    - Spawn and popen path redirections (`in:`/`out:`/`err:`) are closed in
      the parent after the child starts (`rbSpawnCmd` returns them,
      `rbStartChild` closes them), also when a later option fails.
      `rbSpawn` and `rbSpawnFull` share `rbStartChild`, which maps start
      errors through `rbExecErr`: a non-executable file is `Errno::EACCES`.
    - `Process::Status` carries the terminating signal: `exitstatus` and
      `success?` are nil (types `Integer?`/`bool?`), `signaled?`/`termsig`
      report it, `to_s` is "pid N SIGKILL (signal 9)", `to_i` is the raw
      wait status; `system` is false for a signalled child.
    - `Find.prune` sets a per-goroutine flag (`rbFindActive`), not a package
      global, so threads cannot prune each other; outside `Find.find` it
      raises `UncaughtThrowError`. MRI's `throw :prune` cannot wrap the
      yield because the block is a Go iterator.
    - `Dir.glob`'s `**` neither lists nor descends into symlinks to
      directories, so a symlink loop terminates and matches MRI.
    - `rbRootFibers` drops a thread's entry when its goroutine ends.
      `rbPinnedFiles` is a map behind a leaf mutex held only around the
      map; it never shrinks (an `os.File` finalizer cannot be cleared).
    - Proof: `testdata/test/process_test.rb`, `testdata/run/process_stderr.rb`.
271. **Stdlib hardening** (#101; amends 25, 63; 2026-10-14).
    - CSV parse raises `ArgumentError` for an empty `col_sep` or a
      `quote_char` that is not one character (MRI's messages); the
      generator never raises (an empty sep/quote matches nothing, as in
      MRI). `quote_char: nil` is still treated as the default `"` (MRI
      disables quoting).
    - `SecureRandom` size arguments below 0 raise `ArgumentError`.
      `random_number(0)` stays Integer-typed (MRI answers a Float: the
      static signature cannot express it).
    - `Base64.strict_decode64` (and urlsafe) reject line breaks.
    - `Tempfile` and `Tempfile.create` keep the `os.CreateTemp` handle
      instead of reopening by path; `Dir.mktmpdir` errors name the
      requested directory.
    - StringScanner's anchored twin of a Regexp lives in the Regexp
      (`anch`), not a global map, so it is collected with it.
    - `JSON.dump(obj, io)` writes to io and returns it (overload
      `__dump_2`, as `Marshal.dump`); `dump` allows NaN/Infinity by
      default; an Integer limit raises `NotImplementedError`.
      `JSON.parse`/`load` implement `freeze: true` and raise
      `NotImplementedError` for truthy `allow_nan`, `object_class`,
      `array_class`, `decimal_class`, `create_additions`, `match_string`;
      other keys are ignored, as the generator does.
    - OptionParser converters turn only `ArgumentError`/`TypeError` into
      `InvalidArgument`; any other panic propagates (an integer past 64
      bits is now a `RangeError`).
272. **Harness and process-boundary hardening** (#101; amends 88, 102, 127; 2026-10-14).
    - MRI oracle cache: key = Ruby description, TZ, checkout,
      sha256(Gemfile.lock), args, sources (prefix v5). It stores only runs
      MRI itself finished (exit >= 0, no `LoadError` / "cannot load such
      file" in stderr: those reflect the machine and would replay
      forever). Writes go to a temp file in the cache dir, then
      `os.Rename`. Suites fail on an empty glob (`mustGlob`). `TestMulti`
      requires MRI exit 0.
    - Signals: `rb2go build|run|test` installs one SIGINT/SIGTERM
      cancelled context before compiling; the compile is abandoned on
      cancel, `go build` gets SIGINT, the temp module is always removed,
      and the exit status is 128+signal on every path. `run` and `test`
      share `runProg` (forwarding signals to the child).
    - Playground `/run` (decision 102 stays single-user, no semaphore):
      the child's environment is only PATH, HOME=<its temp dir>,
      TMPDIR=<its temp dir>, so submitted code cannot read the developer's
      tokens. A handler panic becomes a 200 JSON error (web.html turns a
      non-OK response into raw text).
    - `cmd/rubyspec`: one panicking program is that program's FATAL
      result; any FATAL program makes the exit status 1; the ruby/spec
      cache is fetched at the pinned `specCommit` (0816047, 2026-10-14)
      into `<dir>.tmp`, then renamed; a cache at another commit warns.
      `scripts/rubyspec-coverage` still clones unpinned HEAD.
    - The generated module pins regexp2 once (`regexp2Version`);
      `TestGoModMatchesRepo` ties it to go.mod and checks GoSum is
      non-empty.
273. **Allocation sizes a program controls are checked** (#101; amends 20, 35; 2026-10-14).
    - A Go out-of-memory is fatal and `rescue` cannot catch it, so sizes
      are checked before `make`/`strings.Repeat`. `rbCheckAlloc(n,
      elemSize)` (prelude/go/alloc.go) raises `NoMemoryError` ("failed to
      allocate memory") above 4 GiB (n*elemSize, overflow-safe; negative n
      counts as too big); `rbAllocTooBig` is the same test for sites whose
      MRI error is an `ArgumentError`.
    - Per site, as MRI at 2**62: `String#*`, `ljust/rjust/center` and pack
      `a/A/Z/B/H/x/@` raise `NoMemoryError`; `Array.new` (both forms)
      raises `ArgumentError` "array size too big"; `Array#*` raises
      `ArgumentError` "argument too big" (and no longer hangs on `[] * 2**62`);
      unpack int/float counts >= 2**60 raise `IndexError`; format widths
      or precisions past INT_MAX raise `ArgumentError` "width too big" /
      "precision too big" (a `*` width past int is `RangeError`); a
      strftime width past INT_MAX prints the directive as is; a strftime
      result over 3,932,160 bytes (MRI's measured limit) raises
      `Errno::ERANGE` (added to the prelude).
    - Accepted divergence: between 4 GiB and MRI's own limit rb2go raises
      where MRI might succeed (the cap guards against absurd sizes, not
      a precise limit); a real OOM below the cap stays fatal.
    - The quadratic fills (strftime zero padding, `rbFormatTwos`) use
      `strings.Repeat`/`bytes.Repeat`.
    - Hello-world output grew 61 bytes (the `Errno::ERANGE` type).
274. **Small compiler fixes** (#101; amends 169, 209, 241, 242; 2026-10-14).
    - A tuple's `first`/`last` with arguments, and `<=>`/`==` with the
      wrong argument count, fall through to the Array of its fields
      (decision 241) instead of reading `.F0`/`args[0]` unchecked.
    - The Data/Struct keyword paths share `kwMap` and report the first
      unknown keyword in source order (they ranged over a map).
    - "condition of type X is always true" and "cannot be called
      dynamically" warnings go through the deduping `warn` path (pass 2
      only for `fctx`).
    - `analyze` truncates `retTypes` to its entry length on every re-run,
      so the untyped first run's `Array[untyped]` cannot join a refined
      `Array[Integer]` ("method f returns both ..."). `fctx.try`'s own
      rewind is not done yet.
    - `_DynGen` keys a private method with a NUL prefix (`dynPrivKey`), so
      a public `_foo` no longer collides with a private `foo`.
    - A string-literal name in `send`/`public_send`/`method`/
      `respond_to?` reaches the method as a symbol literal does.
    - Struct/Data member names must be plain identifiers: a compile
      error, not an internal error. (MRI accepts `:a?`; supporting it
      needs ivar-name mangling in `valueClassSource`.)
    - `Regexp.new/compile/union` noting covers `::Regexp` and decides via
      `rxPrepare`.
275. **Prelude method twins are aliases; non-MRI Enumerable helpers removed** (#101; amends 12; 2026-10-14).
    - Removed the non-MRI public Enumerable methods `count_if`,
      `with_index_pairs`, `uniq_by` and `each_with_index_from` (no
      callers anywhere): `respond_to?` was true where MRI's is false, and
      calls compiled instead of raising `NoMethodError`. `with_object` is
      Enumerator's only, as in MRI. `Hash#first_pair` became
      `__first_pair`.
    - Core method twins (`map`/`collect`, `select`/`filter`/`find_all`,
      `find`/`detect`, `reduce`/`inject`, Hash `each`/`each_pair`,
      `select`/`filter`, Array `select!`/`filter!`) are `alias` lines.
      `copyAlias` renames the method, so each name keeps its own
      backtrace frame label; a forwarding `def` would add a frame.
    - `Range#__span(n)` is the shared `[]=`/`fill` range arithmetic
      (returns `[start, count]` or nil; the callers raise `RangeError`,
      since raising inside would add a backtrace frame). It returns a
      tuple, so a program whose only tuple use is `fill(range)` emits the
      Tuple2 boilerplate (+3 functions); programs that also slice with a
      range shrink. `Range#__slice` keeps its own clamping copy.
    - Measured (generated bytes): 64_observable 2,861,546 → 2,854,504;
      116_cuba_web 3,012,365 → 3,007,862; word_count and hello unchanged.
276. **A compile error's location is data, not text** (#101; 2026-10-14).
    - `srcPos{File, Line}` (`String()` is `name:line`) is embedded in
      `Class`, `Method`, `Const`, `Include`, `typeScope`, `gemNilIvar` and
      `verbatim`, and held by `compileError{pos, msg, untyped}`. `Error()`
      composes `pos: msg`, or just `msg` when no pos is set.
    - `errorf(f, n, ...)` locates the error at n's line; `errorAt(pos,
      ...)` (and `warnAt`) at a declaration's pos, so no site formats
      `"%s:%d:"` by hand. Code that needs the bare message (`unionSwitch`
      wording an arm failure) reads `err.msg`; code that needs the full
      text (kept errors, poison reasons, warnings) calls `err.Error()`.
      Nothing strips a location back off a string, so a file name with
      `:` or whitespace is safe.
    - Rendered text is unchanged (checked on 1422 inputs: examples,
      testdata/run, testdata/test and all 270 testdata/errors cases).
      Exception: the `restBlockAdapter` error (gem code only) now carries
      a `file:line:` prefix.
    - Left: `ivarDecl` has only a line (its file is its class's);
      `sorbet.go`/`source.go` parse errors are plain `fmt.Errorf`.
277. **File provenance is a sealed Origin** (#101; amends 172; 2026-10-14).
    - `Origin` (`originSource`/`originUser`/`originGem`/`originPrelude`)
      replaces `File.prelude bool` and `gem *bool`. The prelude origin is
      set at construction (`parseFile` takes an `Origin`) and gates the
      `%x{}` Go escape hatch, prelude-only branches and `dynRaise`. A file
      loaded from disk starts as `originSource`; `gemFile` classifies it
      as user or gem on the first ask and memoizes it, so the gem-dir
      symlink resolution (44% of brew's gen CPU, decision 237) is still
      paid once per file, and only if asked.
    - Source re-parsed from a declaration (`Struct`, `delegate`,
      `def_delegators`, `define_method`, anon classes) gets
      `f.synthOrigin()`: prelude stays prelude, anything else starts
      unclassified and classifies through the `path` copied from the
      originating file, so a gem's synthesized code stays gem code
      (decision 172) and a path-less synthetic file is never a gem's.
    - Behaviour and positions unchanged (byte-identical `gen` over 730
      inputs and the gem-style fixtures; compiled function counts
      identical).
278. **Block parameters are a struct, not an encoded string** (#101; amends 152, 241; 2026-10-14).
    - `blockParamList` (expr_block.go) is the one place a block's or
      lambda's Prism parameters become `blockParams`: `blockParam{Name,
      Rest, Sub}`. `Sub` is non-nil for a `(k, v)` destructuring and holds
      plain names; `Rest` marks `*rest` (`Name` "" for an anonymous one or
      a bare `|a, |`); numbered `_1` and `it` are plain names. Before,
      they were the strings "(k,v)", "*rest" and "*", which a dozen
      consumers re-parsed with HasPrefix, Split and Trim.
    - Consumers ask `hasRest()`, `special()`, `hasTuple()` or read the
      fields. `bindBlockParam` is the one bind-and-skip-`_` sequence for
      single params. `rbFrame.locals` takes `locals()`, the plain names
      only, exactly what the old strings could match.
    - Emitted Go and error texts are unchanged (byte-identical `gen` over
      730 inputs). Not fixed: a block's rest-param name is not in
      `rbFrame.locals`, so `localKey` resolves it to method scope.
279. **Inference keys are typed** (#101; amends 146, 164, 180, 183; 2026-10-14).
    - `pendingKey` names a parameter typed from use (decision 146): an
      unannotated def parameter, a yielded value, a lambda parameter or an
      escaped block's self (decision 164). Its text,
      `file:line:col:[Owner#]name`, is printed by `RB2GO_INFER_DEBUG` and
      appears in warnings, so each kind has exactly one constructor:
      `paramKey`, `yieldKey`, `selfKey`, and `lambdaSrcAt(...).arg(i)` for
      lambdas. The inference, uses and pendingSeen maps and the `Param`,
      `BlockSig` and `TFunc` fields use it instead of `string`.
    - The resolution order lives once, in `Compiler.pendingType`: the last
      round's type, else untyped during a round, else the `needInfer`
      abort, else untyped for a gem (decision 169), else the caller's
      outcome. `resolvePending` (decision 183 warning), `lambdaParams`
      (decision 180 warning) and `blockSelf` (decision 164 error) differ
      only in what they do when the outcome is `pendNone`.
    - No behaviour change: `gen` output and `RB2GO_INFER_DEBUG` output are
      byte-identical on 731 inputs.
280. **Go names a class generates come from one place; closed string sets are types** (#101; amends 3; 2026-10-14).
    - `Class.ifaceName/ctorName/selfName/anyName/metaName/freeFunc` (plus
      `classVar`) are the only spellings of `XI`, `NewX`, `X_Self`,
      `X_Any`, `X_Meta` and `X_m`. Both the emitters and `goNames`
      (`nameGo`'s clash set) call them, so a new derived name cannot reach
      one and miss the other. Names outside `goNames` (`_Ivars`,
      `New<X>In`, `rbSend_`, `_Super_`, `rbHasIsA_`, `X_cv_name`,
      `Foo__Singleton`) are still built ad hoc: folding them in would
      change what counts as a clash.
    - `valueKind` (`valueStruct`/`valueData`; `String()` keeps the
      "struct"/"data" text that appears in errors and generated inspect
      output) and `attrKind` (reader/writer/accessor) replace string
      parameters. A Struct/Data class's `valueMembers`, `valueKind` and
      `kwInit` live in `Class.value *valueSpec`.
    - `nilIvars` and `ivarReadFirst` are keyed by `ivarKey{cls, name}`
      instead of `Owner.Name+"#"+name`; they are only point-queried, never
      iterated, so order cannot reach output.
    - `Display`/`specChild`/`specTests` are not grouped (`Display` is also
      set for anonymous classes). `rbs.Literal.Kind` stays a string.
    - No behaviour change: `gen` output is byte-identical over examples,
      testdata/run, testdata/test, every testdata/errors case and
      whole-prelude (`RB2GO_NO_PRUNE`) gen of two examples.
281. **Homebrew batch 14: RSA verify, bound blocks through dynamic calls, run-time `instance_method`, per-run classes without `initialize`** (#100, #101; amends 141, 243, 259; 2026-10-15).
    - `require "openssl"` loads `prelude/openssl.rb` (net/http loads only
      `openssl_ssl.rb`, its `OpenSSL::SSL::VERIFY_*`, so a web program
      does not compile RSA). Only what Homebrew's JWS check
      needs: `OpenSSL::PKey::RSA.new(pem)` (PKIX/PKCS #1 public, PKCS #1/#8
      private; anything else is `PKeyError`, which `RSAError` names, as in
      MRI), `#verify` (PKCS #1 v1.5), `#verify_pss` (`salt_length:
      :digest/:auto/:max/Integer`), `public?`/`private?`, on Go's
      `crypto/rsa`. Go's MGF1 always uses the message digest, so another
      `mgf1_hash:` returns false where MRI could verify. No signing, key
      generation, TLS or X509 objects.
    - A method's own typed `&block` passed on to a call on an untyped
      receiver (`def bottle(&block) = stable.bottle(&block)`, `stable`
      returning `T.untyped`) becomes an `rbBlockArg` whose values are
      asserted into the block's types. A `T.proc.bind(X)` block is marked
      `bound` and takes self first. The receiving adapter of a bound-block
      method passes self first only to a bound `rbBlockArg`, so a literal
      block (which cannot rebind self, decision 164) still sees just the
      yielded values. Bound-block methods now get dynamic-call adapters.
    - An optional block's adapter passes nil when the call gave none
      (decision 243 said so but always passed a raising stand-in).
    - `recv.instance_method(:m)` on a non-constant class object
      (`self.class.instance_method(:brew).owner`, Formula#initialize):
      built on the receiver's static class, but `bind`/`bind_call`
      dispatch through the object, and `owner`/`inspect` come from a type
      switch on the class object's metaclass type over the subclasses
      that override `m`. A constant receiver keeps decision 141's static
      binding. A union of class objects is still an error.
    - Under decision 197 (a traced entry), a literal `require` of a
      prelude library inside a method loads that library at compile time:
      MRI's trace never lists one a method requires late (Homebrew's
      `require "openssl"` in `verify_jws_signature`). Other in-method
      requires still resolve at run time.
    - A Method of a method whose block is required (gem code only:
      `self.class.instance_method(:brew).owner`) is built for its
      reflection; calling it raises LocalJumpError without running the
      method (MRI would run it up to its first yield).
    - `FileUtils.touch(list, mtime:, nocreate:)` as MRI's (a missing
      path under `nocreate: true` is ENOENT from `apply2files`), and
      `MatchData#values_at` takes group names as well as indexes
      (Homebrew's `Tap.with_formula_name`).
    - A dynamic call on an untyped nil goes to a reopened `NilClass`'s
      method before Object's or Kernel's, as decision 241's typed nil
      does (Homebrew's `compact_blank`: `case v when Object then
      v.blank?` must see `NilClass#blank?`).
    - An untyped Array converted to a tuple (`rbAs`, decision 22)
      converts each element as an Array's are (`rbConv`), so
      `["u", {tag: "v"}]` from JSON fills a `[String, Hash[Symbol,
      untyped]]` prop (Homebrew's `FormulaStruct#stable_url_args`).
    - `Digest::SHA2.digest/hexdigest/base64digest/file` are SHA-256's, as
      MRI's class methods are (Homebrew's `Formulary.namespace_key`).
    - Decision 241's param widening judges a `T?` value by its `T`:
      `fits` lets any non-class type through, so `rebuild = (".#{rebuild}"
      if x)` into an Integer param never widened it (Homebrew's
      `GitHubPackages.version_rebuild`).
    - A gem's String bang method rewritten to its plain form (decision
      136) keeps its block: `gsub!(re) { ... }` lost it and called
      `gsub(re)` (Homebrew's `Formulary.class_s`).
    - A per-run `Class.new` class (decision 259) whose ancestry defines no
      `initialize` also gets `New<X>In`, so its instances' `self.class` is
      their own class object, not the shared one.
    - Proof: `testdata/test/openssl_test.rb`, `method_test.rb`
      (`test_instance_method_on_run_time_class`), testdata/boot
      (`DemoBoundFormula`, `DemoBareLoader`, `DemoNeedsBlockSub`, `DemoUrlStruct`,
      `DemoRebind`, `DemoClassS`),
      testdata/traced (`TracedReq.late_lib`), `fileutils_test.rb`
      (`test_touch_mtime_and_nocreate`), `string_test.rb` (`test_matchdata`),
      `object_test.rb` (`test_reopened_markers`).
282. **Homebrew batch 15: a prepended module's class variables** (#100; amends 241; 2026-10-15).
    - A class variable assigned in a module body and used by that
      module's methods after `Klass.prepend(Mod)` is the module's: the
      copies prepending makes no longer declare the class's own
      method-first `@@x` (#52), which shadowed it and read as unset.
    - A reached method's `@@x` read or write keeps a gem body's
      assignment through constant pruning (decision 169).
    - Homebrew's `OS::Linux::DependencyCollector` (`@@global_dep_tree`,
      `@@building_global_dep_tree`).
    - Proof: `object_test.rb` (`test_prepended_module_class_var`),
      testdata/boot (`DemoCvCollector`).
283. **An assignment narrows a union local** (#100; amends 150, 241; 2026-10-15). Gem code is to stay typed where
    *See also decision 318*.
    the program is (the 2026-10-15 review of Homebrew's untyped values: `RB2GO_UNTYPED_WHY` names each dynamic
    receiver's root).
    - `x = v` into a local or parameter typed as a union, with `v` of one class that fits it, leaves `x` that class
      until its next write or the end of the scope: one Go assertion (`t := x.(Pathname)`) at the assignment, and
      reads of `x` use `t`. Before, `src = Pathname(src)` with `src: String | Pathname` kept the union, so
      `src.dirname` failed on the String member: an error in user code, a dynamic call (and the parameter widened
      to untyped, decision 241) in gem code.
    - A later write of the same class updates the view's variable as well as the local (`x = x.dirname / ...`
      inside an `if` keeps `x` a Pathname after it); another class drops the view, as any other narrowing.
    - Not when a block or lambda in the method writes the local: its write would not reach the view.
    - `FileUtils.touch` takes `String | Pathname | Array[String]` instead of decision 12 class twins: a dynamic
      call's wrapper cannot pass a twin its keywords (decision 281 added `mtime:`/`nocreate:`).
    - Effect on `brew info wget`'s program: untyped receivers of dynamic calls 1,829 → 1,779, dynamic call sites
      2,693 → 2,607, `rbAs` conversions 8,295 → 8,246.
    - Proof: `union_test.rb` (`test_assignment_narrows`).
284. **A SimpleDelegator typed by its initialize; `Pathname.glob`** (#100; amends 118; 2026-10-15).
    *See also decision 291*.
    - A `SimpleDelegator` subclass whose own annotated `initialize` sets the wrapped object only by `super(p)` from
      a typed parameter `p` (a rest parameter wraps `Array[T]`), with no `__setobj__` call and no subclass
      `initialize`, wraps that type: `__getobj__` is one assertion to it, and forwarded calls are typed instead of
      dynamic (Homebrew's `Dependencies#initialize(*args)` with `args: Dependency`: `deps.select`, `reject`,
      `filter_map`). Anything else keeps decision 118's untyped object.
    - `Pathname.glob(String | Pathname | Array[String])` (only the instance form existed, so a call on the class
      was dynamic).
    - Effect on `brew info wget`'s program: untyped receivers of dynamic calls 1,779 → 1,702, dynamic call sites
      2,607 → 2,482.
    - Proof: `stdlib_test.rb` (`test_simple_delegator_typed_by_initialize`, `test_pathname_class_glob`),
      testdata/boot (`DemoDlg`).

285. **concurrent-ruby's Promises internals get vendored sigs; sig ivars resolve in their class's nesting;
    `Array.new(n, nil)` is an open literal** (#100; amends 160, 182; 2026-10-15).
    - concurrent-ruby has no annotations, and 241 of brew's untyped receivers sat in it, mostly promises.rb: the
      state from `attr_atomic(:internal_state)` (a string `class_eval`, so nothing to infer from), the
      promise ↔ future links (`@Promise`, `@Future`), `new_blocked_by*`, and the `send`-reached callbacks.
      `sig/gems/concurrent-ruby/` now types that graph. ruby/gem_rbs_collection's concurrent-ruby sigs were
      checked and not used: they type only the public API, as generic classes (`Future[T, E]`) with bounded
      method generics rb2go's RBS grammar does not read, while the untyped sites are internal. A future's value
      stays untyped (it is whatever the task returned).
    - A sig file's `@x: T` resolves `T` in the class's nesting (`module M; class F; @p: P` finds `M::P`), as its
      defs do. Before, the type resolved at top level and, in a gem, failed silently to untyped.
    - `Array.new(n, nil)` (and `::X.new` for any of decision 182's forms) is an open literal, for an ivar as
      for a local, typed by what is stored over its nils: `@a = ::Array.new(n, nil); @a[i] = s` is
      `Array[S?]`. Before, an ivar stayed `Array[untyped]` and a typed `[]=` into it did not compile.
    - Effect on `brew info wget`'s program: untyped receivers of dynamic calls 1,702 → 1,589 (concurrent-ruby
      241 → 128), dynamic call sites 2,339 → 2,268, two fewer methods that fail Go type-checking
      (`delay_on`, `schedule_on`).
    - Proof: `TestRBSIvarNestedType` (rb2go_test.go), `array_test.rb` (`ArrayNewNilTest`).

286. **An unannotated `initialize` inherits its parent's signature only through a pass-through `super`** (#100;
    amends 146; 2026-10-15).
    - Decision 146 lets an unannotated override with the same parameter shape inherit the parent's signature.
      For `initialize` the shape says nothing: `ImmediateEventPromise#initialize(default_executor)` calls
      `super Event.new(self, default_executor)` under `AbstractPromise#initialize(future)`, so typing the
      parent (decision 285) typed the child's executor as a future, and `brew info wget` failed at run time
      ("no implicit conversion of Concurrent::FixedThreadPool into ...AbstractEventFuture").
    - Now an `initialize` inherits only when every `super` in it is a zsuper or passes only locals (each maybe
      splatted, `super(*)` included): ThenPromise's `super delayed, blockers_count, ...` still inherits
      (decision 256). Otherwise its parameters are typed as any def's (decision 146). Other methods are unchanged:
      an override's arguments mean the parent's by Liskov; a constructor's do not.
    - Proof: `infer_test.rb` (`InitInheritTest`; before, a compile error "Ev where String is expected").

287. **Brew batch 16: Comparable ops through a `<=>` that takes untyped; `String#start_with?(Regexp)`; Set's
    union family takes an Array** (#100; amends 9; 2026-10-15).
    - Comparable's `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` on a class whose own `<=>` takes `untyped` pass the argument to that
      `<=>` as it is, as MRI's Comparable does, instead of converting it to Comparable's `(self)` parameter:
      `version < "2.39"` (Homebrew's `Glibc.below_ci_version?`; `Version#<=>` is Sorbet `other: T.untyped`, the
      authors' type, not one rb2go widened). A nil result is MRI's ArgumentError ("comparison of Version with 3
      failed": special constants show their inspect, others their class). Any other `<=>` signature keeps
      decision 9's static `(self)` check.
    - `String#start_with?(Regexp)` is a typed overload (`__start_with_q_regexp`: a leftmost match at 0); it was
      a dynamic call that failed (Homebrew's `Ld.library_paths`). `$~` is not set (ponytail).
    - `Set#merge`, `|`, `union`, `+`, `&`, `intersection`, `-`, `difference` take `Set[E] | Array[E]`, as MRI's
      take any enumerable: `paths = Set.new; paths += array` widened the local to untyped and then failed.
    - Proof: testdata/boot (`DemoCmp`), `string_test.rb`, `stdlib_test.rb` (`test_set_ops_take_an_array`).

288. **Traced mode is the CLI's `-main`; `rb2go_main`, `rb2go_method_requires` and `rb2go_fold` are gone** (#100;
    amends 197, 246; 2026-10-15). *See also decision 296* (`-trace`: no entry file).
    - Magic comments in the compiled program made rb2go a dialect: the source said how to compile it. Decision
      197's entry (the requires MRI loaded) is now a plain Ruby file that MRI can run too, and `rb2go gen|build|run
      -main brew.rb entry.rb` names the program it boots into. `-main` is traced mode, which always resolves a
      require inside a method at run time (decision 246): the two comments only ever appeared together.
      `compiler.CompileTraced` / `rb2go.CompileTraced` are the API.
    - `rb2go_fold` (build-time facts, `OS.mac? => false`) is removed. Measured on `brew info wget`: dropping its
      four OS/engine facts added 114 functions (0.16%) and no method that fails to compile, since brew's macOS
      code is in files a Linux trace never loads; `ENV["HOMEBREW_SPAWN_SYSTEM"] == "1"` is now an environment
      variable brew reads at run time (its own spawn mode), and the fork branch compiles.
    - `rb2go_replace` remains until the brew patch series replaces its two shims (`standalone/init.rb`,
      `standalone/sorbet.rb`). *Removed by decision 297: brew's boot files compile as they are.*
    - Proof: `TestTracedEntry` (testdata/traced, no magic comments).

289. **Pathname is an ordinary object** (#100; amends 65; 2026-10-15).
    - Pathname was a `@go_type string`: a Go value with no room for state, so a Pathname could not hold instance
      variables, nor could a subclass or an included module with state. Homebrew gives Pathname state in four
      places (`DiskUsageExtension`, `EagerInitializeExtension`, `ObserverPathnameExtension`, `ELFShim`'s caches);
      MRI's Pathname is an ordinary object holding `@path`. Now rb2go's is too: a struct class with `@path:
      String`, its Go escapes reduced to two string helpers (`__join`, `__rel`). The compiler's value-base paths
      (decisions 172, 202) no longer apply to it.
    - `self` of a struct class where its class does not fit converts through MRI's implicit protocols
      (`File.basename(self)` in a Pathname: `to_path`, decision 253). The generic Self type fit anything, so it
      was passed unconverted.
    - `Pathname#read(length, offset)`, as MRI's.
    - Found on the way: `collectBodyConst`/`liftConstWrites` rewrote the parsed prelude's AST in place, which every
      compile shares (a data race, and a second compile saw the first's rewrite). Both are copy-on-write now.
    - Cost: `05_word_count` unchanged, `64_observable` −1 function, `116_cuba_web` +105 (+0.4%). Brew's
      Go-rejected/uncompilable methods 209 → 206 (`Pathname#initialize`, disk usage).
    - Proof: example 55, `stdlib_test.rb` (`test_pathname_subclass_with_state`), `TestPreludeWholeNoEnv` (no race).

290. **Brew batch 17: absolute RBS names, bangs on fresh strings, bound blocks passed to super, nil's freeze/dup,
    `new` on a run-time subclass, mixed-in `initialize_dup` super, `ERB::Util.url_encode`** (#100; amends 136, 232;
    2026-10-16).
    - An RBS/Sorbet type written `::Pathname` resolves from the top level (`rbs.Name.Absolute`); the parser dropped
      the `::`, so inside Homebrew's `OS::Linux` it named `OS::Linux::Pathname`, the ClassMethods module.
    - In gem code a bang method on a fresh String (`x.to_s` of a non-String, `dup`, `+s`) lowers like one on a local,
      without a write-back: no other reference sees the string, so only its value (nil when unchanged) matters
      (Homebrew's `basename.to_s.delete_prefix!(SONAME)`). Amends 136, which needed a local or ivar target.
    - `super(..., &block)` from a method whose block binds a subclass (`T.proc.bind(Resource::Patch)`) to one that
      binds the superclass passes an adapter asserting self back down: Go func types are invariant, and the self
      the callee binds is this object.
    - `freeze`, `frozen?` and `dup` on a `T?` answer nil's own (nil, true, nil) instead of NoMethodError, as
      NilClass has them (`@name.freeze` with `@name` nil).
    - `klass.new(v)` where klass is a class object of unknown exact class and some subclass's `new` takes other Go
      parameter types is a type switch over those subclasses, each argument converted to its `new`'s parameter
      (through untyped where the subclass takes less); one interface assertion could not name them all
      (Homebrew's `Version::Token.create`).
    - A module's `initialize_dup`/`initialize_clone` calling super with no class method above it reaches Object's
      no-op, as a static super already did (decision 232).
    - `ERB::Util.url_encode` / `u`.
    - Proof: testdata/boot (`DemoFreshBang`, `DemoBoundBlk`, `DemoTokNew`, `DemoDupSuper`), `object_test.rb`
      (`AbsoluteRbsNameTest`, `NilFreezeTest`), `erb_test.rb`, `internal/rbs` tests.

291. **Brew batch 18: one ivar across a module and its includer; a delegator over `Set.new(p)`; true/false answer
    their reopened classes dynamically** (#100; amends 147, 241, 284; 2026-10-16). With it and brew patches 0001–0006,
    the compiled `brew info wget` prints MRI's output exactly.
    - A class declaring an ivar an included module already holds uses the module's field (`declareIvar` walked
      only superclasses): Homebrew's Downloadable `attr_reader :checksum` and Resource `attr_writer :checksum` were
      two Go fields, so a bottle's checksum was nil. Attr readers and writers address the field by `ivarPath`.
    - A SimpleDelegator subclass whose annotated initialize does `super(Set.new(p))` wraps `Set[T]` (decision 284
      took `super(p)` only): Homebrew's Requirements.
    - Boolean's Dyn wrapper for a name the program's TrueClass or FalseClass reopening defines dispatches on the
      value, as the static call does (decision 241): through an untyped value `false.present?` took Object's and
      was true, which turned on `--debug` and `--verbose` from brew's environment defaults.
    - Proof: testdata/boot (`DemoSharedIvar`, `DemoSetDlg`, `DemoBoolDyn`).

292. **`overrides/<program>/`: compiling a pinned upstream program from the repo** (#100; 2026-10-16). Each large
    upstream Ruby program rb2go compiles lives under `overrides/<name>/`, with the brew harness as the first:
    a Dockerfile pinning the upstream commit, a `git apply` series (`patches/`) of upstream-shaped changes that
    replace dynamic features with ordinary typed Ruby and leave MRI's output unchanged (`./mricheck` proves it),
    traced entries (`entries/<cmd>.rb`, decision 197), the launcher's environment, and the MRI output each command
    must match (`expected/`). Patches, not compiler directives, are the default fix for a construct only that
    program uses; the remaining `rb2go_replace` overlays are to be replaced by patches. Not run by `go test`: one
    round needs Docker and ~10 GB.

293. **Brew batch 19: more `info` targets; absent-constant guards end a method; run-time optional switches;
    blocks a core method lacks** (#100; amends 167, 205, 245; 2026-10-16). `overrides/brew/check` now compares
    `brew info` for wget, jq, git, python@3.13 and `--json=v2 wget` with MRI in one container; the first four match.
    - `return unless defined?(C)` (or an if/else whose taken branch returns) for a constant no file defines always
      returns, so the statements after it, which name C, are not compiled (decision 167 dropped only the guarded
      branch). Brew patch 0008 guards `Utils::Backtrace` with it: compiled brew has no RubyGems.
    - A switch whose kind is read at run time (an untyped block, decision 245) with `OPTIONAL_ARGUMENT` and no
      placeholder yields true when the argument is left out, as MRI does; only a placed `" [V]"` argument takes
      the next word (`"=[V]"`, `"[=V]"` and the bare constant do not); and the untyped block gets the String, not
      the `String?` box the typed `_opt` overloads take (`brew info --json=v2` failed "no implicit conversion of
      String into Boolean | String").
    - In gem code a block passed to a prelude method that takes none is a compile error, as in user code: MRI's
      method uses it, so dropping it (decision 205's rule for gem methods that never yield) gave a different answer
      (`related.uniq(&:full_name)` deduplicated by identity). `Array#uniq { }` added.
    - `[a, b, *xs]` where xs's element type is not the literal's (`Formula` into `Formula?`) appends element by
      element through the element conversion instead of converting the Array as a whole.
    - `T.cast(x, [A, B])` converts: from untyped or an Array (`xs.partition { }`) through rbAs (decision 22), from a
      wider tuple element by element,
      a narrowing element checked at run time as from untyped (it was passed through unconverted, so a multiple
      assignment of it read tuple fields off `any`: Homebrew's `named_formulae, named_casks = T.cast(...)`).
    - `x&.m` on a union holding nil (`@url&.to_s` of `String | URL | nil`) is nil for nil; it called `m` on nil,
      so a formula without a head spec had `url == ""` and printed `HEAD`.
    - An if/case in value position types its branches with the expected type as a hint (`tail.hint`), so a branch
      literal takes it as it would alone: `T.let(if a then [c ? [] : xs, ys] else ... end, [A, B])` was
      `[Array[untyped], B] | [A, B]`. The hint only steers literals; the value's type is still the branches' join.
    - A hoisted gem local (other than `_`) is marked used (`_ = x`): a when arm that does not compile (decision 244) may have been its
      only reader, and Go then rejected the whole method.
    - `Pathname#read` takes `File.read`'s options; `Array#fetch(i, default)`.
    - Brew patches 0007 (`pour_bottle?` presets call PourBottleCheck directly, no `instance_exec` of a lambda that
      `T.bind`s self) and 0009 (`info --json=v2` builds its pair without reusing the targets as scratch locals).
    - Proof: `control_test.rb` (`test_defined_guard_return`), `stdlib_test.rb` (`test_runtime_optional_argument`,
      Pathname#read), `array_test.rb` (uniq block, splat into `T?`, fetch default), `sorbet_test.rb`
      (`test_cast_tuple`), `union_test.rb` (`test_safe_navigation_on_nil_union`).

294. **Brew batch 20: `brew info --json=v2` deeper; delegators through untyped code; forwarded optional blocks**
    (#100; amends 118, 99, 209; 2026-10-16). Each was one layer of `info --json=v2`'s `Formula#to_hash`; the remaining
    static stubs on that path (`overrides/brew/hits`) are four, in cask loading, and the rest show only at run time.
    - A Forwardable/`delegate` forwarder of a method whose block is optional declares `?{ ... }` and passes the
      caller's block on (`&blk`), or none: it always passed a block, so Homebrew's `livecheck` (read without one,
      configured with one) took the configuring path. A target block binding self keeps `[self: T]`.
    - `s.scan(C)` with C a constant holding a Regexp literal with groups (`.freeze` allowed) picks `__scan_groups`, as
      the literal would (Homebrew's `Patch.extract_cves`).
    - `x.instance_eval(&blk)` on an `X?` receiver passes the object (nil raises before the block runs; ponytail:
      MRI runs it with self nil).
    - A dispatcher (`rbDynName`) given a SimpleDelegator that does not define the name retries on its `__getobj__`
      before method_missing, as MRI's delegator method_missing does; a static call keeps decision 118's typed
      rewrite. (A prelude `SimpleDelegator#method_missing` in Ruby did the same but changed which overloads
      unrelated classes' operators compiled to.) Untyped iteration (`rbEachAny`, `rbElemsAny`) looks through a SimpleDelegator, so
      `deps&.reject(&:implicit?)` of Homebrew's Dependencies iterates its Array.
    - A generic class's method returning `Array[E]` of its own bare parameter gets a Dyn wrapper (Set#to_a through
      untyped); `growsTypeParams` (decision 209) still leaves out other classes of bare parameters (Hash[K, V]'s pairs
      close a Go instantiation cycle) and any union naming a parameter (its rbUnion helper is package-level).
    - `Array#union(*others)`, `Hash#transform_values!`.
    - Brew patch 0010: `to_formulae_and_casks` wraps `load_formula_or_cask` in `Array()` for `flat_map`.
    - Proof: `object_test.rb` (`test_optional_block_delegator`), `regexp_findall_test.rb`
      (`test_scan_groups_through_constant`), `stdlib_test.rb` (`test_untyped_simple_delegator`), `hash_test.rb`,
      `array_test.rb`, testdata/boot (`DemoDelegBlock`, `DemoHoistUnderscore`).
    *See also decision 295:* every Delegator (rbDelegateObj), not only SimpleDelegator.

295. **Brew batch 21: casks** (#100; amends 294, 243, 150; 2026-10-16). `brew info --cask` firefox, visual-studio-code
    and font-fira-code join targets.txt; the info entry gains the 23 files their MRI traces load.
    - Every Delegator, not only SimpleDelegator, stands for its object in untyped code: each delegator class gets a
      `_DelegateObj` method calling its own `__getobj__` (a LazyObject's evaluates its block), and `rbDelegateObj`
      backs the dispatcher fallback (replacing decision 294's SimpleDelegator case), untyped iteration and
      `rbSplatAny`; `[*x]` of a statically typed delegator spreads it the same way (Homebrew's
      `*default.fetch(:languages)` of a LazyObject).
    - A computed send's `&block` may be any Proc value, typed (`T.nilable(T.proc...)` from a tuple) or untyped
      (`rbBlockArgAny`), not only the method's own block; with one anywhere, a block-less method's wrapper drops a
      trailing block as MRI ignores it; `**kwargs` of an empty Hash passes nothing (Homebrew's cask loader
      `public_send(key, *args, **kwargs, &block)`).
    - `rbConv` boxes into a scalar's `T?` (String, Integer, Float, Symbol, Boolean, through `rbOptFrom`), so a JSON
      Hash converts into `Hash[Symbol, String?]` (CaskStruct `ruby_source_checksum`).
    - A call binding a method type parameter to a class's `T?` records it; `rbOptClassFrom` boxes an untyped value
      (nil, or the object) into that pointer type where the generic body converts with `rbAs[U]` (Homebrew's
      `set_unique_stanza[U]` returning an ivar as `DSL::Version?`). Generic Go cannot tell the pointer's element type
      without reflection, so the cases are the instantiations the program makes.
    - In gem code any value where a Boolean is expected is its truthiness (a predicate block's
      `select { |var| ENV.fetch(var) }`); user code still requires a Boolean or `T?`.
    - A local assigned on the right of `||` in a condition is hoisted, as `&&`'s already was (`next if k.nil? ||
      (blk = h[k]).nil?` then `blk`: Cask::DSL#language_eval).
    - `T.bind(self, X)` narrows self to X for the rest of its block or method when X is a subclass of self's static
      type (asserted once); otherwise it is dropped as before (amends 196). Homebrew's MacOSRequirement and
      XcodeRequirement satisfy blocks, bound to Requirement by patch 0013, call their own class's methods.
    - StringScanner `scan`/`skip`/`match?`/`check` take a String pattern, matched literally.
    - Brew patch 0011: CaskStruct `sha256` and `version` are nilable; brew's silenced sorbet-runtime builds a struct
      with a missing required prop as nil, which rb2go's typed initialize rejected. Patch 0012: `print_info` types
      `resolved` as the `T::Array[[T.untyped, T.nilable(Tap)]]` its callee takes: a tuple branch beside an Array
      literal one otherwise joins to a union rb2go cannot convert. Patch 0013: Requirement's satisfy blocks are typed
      `T.proc.bind(Requirement)`, as `satisfied?` instance_evals them on the requirement (decision 164's escaped
      block), and ArchRequirement's block binds to its class as its siblings' do.
    - Proof: testdata/boot (`DemoLazySplat`, `DemoComputedSendBlock`, `DemoTruthySelect`, `DemoSymbolBlank`),
      `hash_test.rb` (`test_untyped_into_optional_values`), `control_test.rb` (`test_condition_or_assigns`),
      `stdlib_test.rb` (`test_scanner_string_patterns`),
      `sorbet_test.rb` (`test_bind_narrows_self`). All eight targets.txt commands equal MRI's output.
296. **`-trace`: compile what a run of the program loaded** (#100; amends 197, 288, 199, 262; 2026-10-16).
    - `rb2go build|run|gen -trace main.rb args...` runs `ruby [-rubyopt ...] -I <-I dirs> main.rb args` once under a
      load tracer (`internal/boot/trace_hook.rb`: TracePoint `:script_compiled` plus `$LOADED_FEATURES` and `$LOAD_PATH`
      at exit), then compiles as `-main main.rb` with the entry built from that run (`compiler.TracedEntry`). A program
      that picks its files at run time (Homebrew's `require "cmd/#{name}"`, `extend/os/#{OS}`) no longer needs a
      hand-traced entry file, an `-I` list or a container: the trace is on the machine it runs on, so a Mac trace
      compiles brew's `extend/os/mac` files and a Linux one its `extend/os/linux` files.
    - The trace EXECUTES the whole program, not only its load phase (decision 262's trust model, widened): a
      command with side effects (`brew install`) performs them at compile time too. Its stdout is dropped and its
      exit status ignored; only a run that wrote no load list fails the compile.
    - Each loaded file becomes a `require`: a gem the prelude is (`json`, `sorbet-runtime`) by its name; a file of
      MRI's own library by its name when rb2go knows the library (`mriLibs`, `preludeLibs`), else dropped (encodings,
      portable Ruby's helpers); any other file by its path under the longest `$LOAD_PATH` dir, which becomes a gem dir
      (`-I`). A file under no load-path dir was reached by `require_relative` from a compiled file and is compiled
      from there. A gem that loaded a C extension (`fiddle`) is left out, as rb2go cannot compile its Ruby half
      against a missing native one; a method using it is stubbed (decision 205).
    - In traced mode the `setup.rb` that `bundle install --standalone` writes compiles to nothing: its `$LOAD_PATH`
      edits are what the trace already resolved, and the rest shims RubyGems' `Kernel#gem`.
    - `class << self; prepend M; end` is `singleton_class.prepend(M)` (amends 199).
    - A top-level constant whose initializer reads a top-level local (brew's `dir = __dir__ || raise(...)`, then
      `HOMEBREW_LIBRARY_PATH = Pathname(dir).parent.realpath.freeze`) is assigned where it stands in its file's top
      level, with the locals (`Const.inMain`; closes decision 170's "not yet"). Its type is inferred by first typing
      the file's top-level local assignments before it in the constant's own probe; a `#: T` still wins.
    - RbConfig's `rubylibdir` is `/rb2go/lib/ruby/4.0.0` (and `archdir` under it), not `""`: MRI's library is compiled
      in, so a computed require of a path under it is already loaded (false), whatever the file (brew's
      `FastBootRequire.from_rubylibdir("pathname")`, and `from_archdir("portable_ruby_gems")` in the arm boot ran
      under portable Ruby).
    - The prelude gains what brew's MRI boot plumbing (`standalone/init.rb`, `standalone/sorbet.rb`, which the two
      `rb2go_replace` overlays used to replace) reads: `require "rubygems"` loads `prelude/rubygems.rb` (`Gem::VERSION`,
      `Gem::Version` comparison, `Gem.paths=`, `ruby_api_version`, `discover_gems_on_require`; no gem loading, as
      MRI under `--disable=gems`), and sorbet-runtime's `T::Configuration` setters are no-ops (signatures are checked
      at compile time). Statements editing `$LOAD_PATH` stay skipped (decision 205).
    - `T.bind(self, X)` also makes X the owner of the block's ivars (decision 295's narrowing; ArchRequirement's
      `@arch` in its satisfy block).
    - A run sees only the paths that ran: the main file's own literal requires outside its methods, of files on the
      traced load path, are compiled too (brew.rb's `rescue` requiring utils/backtrace).
    - `fiddle` (a C extension) is a prelude library: MRI's LP64 constants, so code that builds C signatures at load
      (Homebrew's os/mac/ffi) loads; `Fiddle.dlopen` raises `Fiddle::DLError`, as without cgo nothing can be called
      (*see also decision 326*: Fiddle now calls C through purego).
      `fcntl` is one too (`IO#fcntl` with an integer argument); `File#stat`, `File::Stat#ino`/`#dev`,
      `File.identical?` and `FileUtils.ln_s(force:)`/`ln_sf` over Pathnames join the prelude (brew's LockFile and
      cached downloads).
    - Ancestors: a module the superclass already has, included again (directly or through another module), keeps its
      place after the superclass, as MRI's include skips it: CurlDownloadStrategy's `ohai` is
      AbstractDownloadStrategy's override, not Utils::Output::Mixin's again through Utils::Curl. Method lookup used to
      put it before the superclass.
    - Typed fixes the Mac path needed: `return x unless x.nil?` narrows a `T?` local (only unions narrowed);
      Comparable's ops on `self` in a generic body go through a `<=>` that takes untyped (decision 287 for
      self: MacOSVersion#prerelease?'s `self >= "27"`); a `-> self` call on self asserts back to Self
      (MacOSVersion#strip_patch calling Version#major_minor); `&p` of a nil `T?` Proc passes no block, as MRI's,
      instead of raising.
    - A gem method's first pass that a compile error unwound from inside nested blocks left its closure depth behind,
      so the retried pass (decision 251) compiled a plain `return` as a non-local one that raises
      (Resource::BottleManifest#manifest_annotations). Each pass now starts at the body's own nesting. No small
      reproduction was found; the Mac `brew info` run is the proof.
    - Brew patch 0014: `Formula.service` stores its block as `T.proc.bind(Homebrew::Service)`, as its parameter
      already says and Service#initialize instance_evals it.
    - `RB2GO_RUBY` names the MRI for boot, gem lookup and the trace (default `ruby` on PATH). The boot hook calls
      Module's own methods through `instance_method(...).bind_call`, as a module may make them private for itself
      (brew's `Cask::Quarantine`), and tests a constant with `Module === v` (a BasicObject has no `is_a?`).
    - overrides/brew's `host` clones the user's Homebrew at the pinned commit into `work/homebrew`, applies the
      patches, and replaces its portable Ruby with `tools/ruby-wrapper`: `bin/brew` sets up its environment as always
      and then runs `rb2go run -trace brew.rb <args>`; any other Ruby call (and a nested `bin/brew`) goes to the real
      Ruby. No brew patch: brew.sh runs `$HOMEBREW_RUBY_PATH` explicitly, so a `#!` line is never consulted.
    - Proof: `TestTraceRun` (testdata/traced from a run: a standalone Bundler setup, a constant reading a local, a
      require under rubylibdir), `TestTracedEntry` in internal/compiler, `object_test.rb` (`test_prepend`),
      `control_output.rb`, `control_test.rb` (`guarded_unless_nil`), `stdlib_test.rb` (`test_gem_version`,
      `test_fiddle_constants_and_dlerror`, `test_file_stat_ino`), `fileutils_test.rb` (`test_ln_s_force`),
      `sorbet_test.rb` (`test_bind_narrows_self`), testdata/boot `DemoCmp`; `brew info wget` on macOS equals MRI's.
297. **`-trace-also`; `rb2go_replace` is gone; the brew harness traces** (#100; amends 197, 288, 296; 2026-10-17).
    - `rb2go build|run|gen -trace main.rb args -trace-also "other args"` (repeatable) traces more runs of the same
      program; their loaded files join the first run's (load order kept, each file once), so one binary serves
      several commands: overrides/brew's targets.txt (formulae, `--json=v2`, casks) builds into one work/brew-bin.
    - `# rb2go_replace: path => file` is removed (`readMagic`, `Compiler.replaced`): with decision 296 brew's
      `standalone/init.rb` and `standalone/sorbet.rb` compile as they are, so no directive remains in rb2go.
    - overrides/brew drops entries/, overlay/, env.txt, dirs.txt and ./trace. Every script runs brew through its own
      `bin/brew` with tools/ruby-wrapper in place of its Ruby (tools/install-wrapper, tools/in-container.sh):
      `./gen` traces every targets.txt line (`HOMEBREW_RB2GO_ALSO`), `./build` cross-builds work/brew-bin, `./check`
      compares each line against unpatched fork-mode MRI in the same container. The wrapper takes a prebuilt rb2go
      (`HOMEBREW_RB2GO_EXE`) where there is no checkout to build it from.
    - A private method is called directly only when nothing overrides it; a module's includers (and their
      subclasses) now count, not only subclasses (`Class.includers`). Utils::Curl is `module_function`, so its
      `curl` is private, and CurlDownloadStrategy overrides it to add its headers and `--silent`: `curl_download`
      called the module's own `curl`, so the compiled brew's bottle manifest download went out without its
      Authorization header and failed (amends 253).
      An includer's own private override of a module's dispatched private method (UnpackStrategy::GenericUnar's
      `extract_to_dir`) is dispatched too, so it gets the forwarder the module's constraint needs.
    - Several `**` splats into named keywords (`super(*a, **x, **y, **z)`) are one `**{**x, k: v, **y}` from the
      first splat on: later keys win and **rest keeps MRI's order. It was a compile error.
    - Brew patch 0015: `PourBottleCheck#satisfy` stores its block on the formula class (`pour_bottle_satisfy`) and
      `Formula#pour_bottle?` instance_execs it, instead of `define_method` at run time (macOS `:clt_installed`).
    - Brew patch 0016: `Context#with_context`'s block is `T.proc.returns(T.untyped)`, not `void`: the method returns
      the block's value, which CurlDownloadStrategy#expand_deferred_environment_args uses.
    - Proof: `TestTraceRun` (a second run given `argv` loads traced_argv), `object_test.rb` (`ObjFetcher`,
      `ObjUnar`), `method_test.rb` (`test_several_double_splats`); all eight targets.txt lines equal MRI's through
      the traced build, and on macOS `brew info` wget, jq, git (downloading its manifest itself), python@3.13 and
      `--json=v2 wget`.

298. **ruby/spec regressions from the brew rounds** (#100; amends 199, 253; 2026-10-17).
    The brew work left three ruby/spec core directories unbuildable (core 5,004 → 4,669 passing). All three are
    ordinary-Ruby gaps, not brew constructs:
    - An ivar first typed by `@me = self` inside a generic body (a struct class with subclasses, or an anonymous
      class's metaclass) was declared `*Self`, a type parameter outside any generic. It is the owner's class type
      (its interface), as `Self` is everywhere else outside the body. Latent before #100 (the class-body form was a
      compile error, so specs skipped it).
    - `::Module.prepend M` (any receiver'd `X.prepend` / `X.singleton_class.prepend`) inside a class or module body
      is collected like the top-level form (decision 199), not left as a run-time call.
    - Decision 253 made an overridden private method dispatch; `initialize` is overridden almost everywhere but is
      never an interface slot, so a call through an interface-typed receiver (`SystemCallError.instance_method(
      :initialize)`) named `SystemCallErrorI.Initialize`. Such a call is the free func, as before 253.
    - Process: ruby/spec core (`go run ./cmd/rubyspec core`) joins the per-batch checks of #100, so a FATAL
      directory shows up in the round that causes it.
    - Proof: `object_test.rb` `SelfIvarTest`; core/class, core/exception, core/kernel run again.

299. **ruby/spec numbers, ranges and times: MRI's result class from the argument** (#102; amends 112, 140; 2026-10-17).
    - `Float#round`/`floor`/`ceil` with a literal digits ≤ 0 (`x.round(-2)`, `x.floor(0)`) is an Integer, as MRI's:
      the compiler picks `__round_int`/`__floor_int`/`__ceil_int`, a literal overload like BigDecimal's (decision
      112). A computed digits ≤ 0 still raises (the call is typed Float). Digits outside a C int raise MRI's
      `RangeError` (`integer 2147483648 too big to convert to 'int'`).
    - `Range#step(Float)` yields Floats whatever the bounds' class, and without a block it is an
      `ArithmeticSequence[Float]` (`(1..2).step(0.5).to_a` is `[1.0, 1.5, 2.0]`), where decision 140 made it a type
      error. An iterator call now takes a twin for its argument's class (`__step_float`) as other calls do, and a
      blockless call takes `__<name>_enum_<class>` when one exists. Float stepping counts the exclusive end as MRI's
      `ruby_float_step_size`; a numeric range with a negative Integer step walks backward (Ruby 3.4).
    - `Range#bsearch` over Floats searches their bits as ordered int64s (MRI's `double_as_int64`); a beginless
      Integer range probes downward. Find-any mode (a block answering a number) is not supported: the block is
      typed `bool`.
    - `Time.at(sec, subsec, unit, in:)` takes MRI's arguments (the zone was the second positional); `Time.now` and
      `Time.new` take `in:`; a fixed-offset Time's `zone` is nil (`String?`, as MRI's RBS). Float seconds keep
      their exact value floored to a nanosecond (`Time.at(100) + -1.3` has usec 699999).
    - Proof: `range_test.rb` (`RangeFloatStepTest`, `RangeIncludeSuccTest`), `number_test.rb`
      (`NumberFloatDigitsTest`), `stdlib_test.rb` (`StdlibTimeSubsecTest`).

300. **ruby/spec bugs that reach into codegen: a Hash subclass's `default`, `$~` in `grep`, Struct is Enumerable** (#102; amends 165, 171; 2026-10-17).
    - A `class X < Hash` that defines `default(key)` and not `[]` gets a generated
      `def [](key) = key?(key) ? super : default(key)` (`addHashDefaultIdx`), as MRI's `rb_hash_aref` reaches an
      overridden `default` through the method table. Hash#[] itself still reads the stored default, so plain Hash
      pays nothing. The generated `[]` is `(untyped) -> untyped`: the override's answer need not be a `V`
      (ponytail: type it from `default`'s signature when that names `V`).
    - `xs.grep(re) { ... }` / `grep_v` on an Array, with a Regexp pattern, in a frame that reads `$~` (decision
      171) compiles to `rbGrepLM`, which sets the frame's last match before each test's block, nil for a failed
      test, as MRI's `grep_iter_i`. Without a block, or on a non-Array receiver, `$~` is untouched (MRI's
      blockless grep does not set it).
    - Each `Struct.new` class includes `Enumerable[untyped]` over its values (its generated `each`). Not the
      `Struct` base: there the generated `to_h` (`Hash[Symbol, untyped]`) would fail the override check against
      `Enumerable#to_h`, so `Struct.include?(Enumerable)` is still false. Cost: 116_cuba_web +44 functions (+0.5%,
      rack's structs join untyped `map`/`each_*` dispatchers); 05_word_count unchanged.
    - Proof: `hash_test.rb` `HashDefaultOverrideTest`, `regexp_extended_test.rb` `RegexpGrepLastMatchTest`,
      `object_test.rb` `ObjectStructEnumerableTest`.

301. **`brew install` path: gem-code shapes `info` never reached** (#100; amends 205, 296; 2026-10-17).
    - A failed `try` attempt, and a discarded `probe`, undo what they noted about locals (declaration pass, block,
      hoist flag). A lenient retry had found the local "already declared" and assigned (`sym = ...`) without
      declaring it (`forbidden = forbidden.split.each_with_object({}) { ... sym = ... }`).
    - `alias base_respond_to? respond_to?` in gem code forwards to the intrinsic (`respond_to?` has no `Method`);
      `singleton_class.send(:undef_method, :x)` / `.undef_method` / `.remove_method` in gem code is a no-op (boot
      recorded the one definition that survives), and a receiverless `define_singleton_method` site boot saw is a
      no-op as `define_method` is (decision 198): bindata's self-replacing `inherited` hook compiles.
    - `(a, = expr)` as a condition is `expr`'s value; the Go it produced (`_ = `) did not parse.
    - An `each(&block)` whose block is `|*args|` (a gem forwarding to an untyped collection) gets an iterator adapter
      too: several yielded values reach the iterator as one Array (`rbPackYield`).
    - A gem's `x = super(*mods)` of a void method (`Module#include`) runs it and binds nil. A block parameter rebound
      to a non-Proc value (`task = ScheduledTask.execute(&task)`) becomes a new Go variable. A `NamedArgs < Array`
      (embedded `Array[any]`) passed where `Array[String]` is declared converts at run time (`rbAs`).
    - `$LOAD_PATH`, `$:` and `$-I` read what boot left in it (`Manifest.load_path`); without a boot they stay
      unsupported (decision 61).
    - Prelude: `File::Stat#ctime/#writable?/#readable?/#executable?/#uid/#gid/#grpowned?`, `File.grpowned?`,
      `Pathname#grpowned?`, `Process.groups`, `IO#getch` (line-buffered, no raw mode), `Errno::EBUSY`.
    - Brew patch 0017 gives the compiler plain shapes where Ruby is dynamic (`self.class.new`, `send(*args, **kw)`,
      `flat_map` returning an element, an untyped lambda passed as a typed proc, `T.self_type` through the
      `freeze` chain).
    - Linux `brew install` also needs bindata/elftools/patchelf (relocation reads ELF files): bindata's
      `define_singleton_method(:new)` closing over locals boot cannot record (`endian_classes`) is still poisoned.
    - Later in the same push (macOS `brew install wget`, `overrides/brew/macgen`/`macrun`): a private class hook
      (`private def inherited`) is called by the runtime, not refused; an `inherited` hook's
      `subclass.class_eval { @x = local }` runs where the hook stands (`seedsFromLocals`), not as the class's body;
      `module_eval(String)` in gem code raises NotImplementedError (a ScriptError the loader rescues: third-party
      tap formulae are unreadable in a compiled brew, API formulae are fine); an undefined method on a gem class not
      all loaded is a run-time NoMethodError inside methods; `T::Class[Mod]` of a module is any class object; a
      Delegator member of a union forwards an iterator call; Kernel#then/yield_self/tap fall back on any value;
      `def_delegators` through an untyped accessor forwards untyped; run-time `rbAs` unwraps a Delegator.
      Prelude: `FileUtils.copy_file`, `File.chown`/`Pathname#chown`, `Errno::EROFS`.
      Brew patches 0018-0023 (option lists, seatbelt template, merge_repeats kwargs, runtime dependency record
      type, `from_extension`, sandbox inheritance File).
    - Proof: `testdata/boot` (`rebind`, `rebind_block`, `Registry`, `safe_responds?`, `$LOAD_PATH`), `control_test.rb`
      (`test_destructure_as_condition`), `enumerator_test.rb` (`ForwardedTest`), `stdlib_test.rb`
      (`test_stat_access_and_groups`).

302. **Block-taking Array and Hash names on an untyped receiver go through the view; literal blocks auto-splat**
    (amends 236, 209, 243; 2026-10-17).
    - **Why:** profiling `brew info wget` (693k Go lines) put 54% of its 366k compiled functions in generic
      instantiations. Decision 236 had moved the blockless whole-container names to one body, but the block-taking
      ones (`select`, `inject`, `sort_by`, `each_with_object`, `all?`, ...) stayed per-shape `DynX` wrappers, and the
      iteration names (`each`, `cycle`, `each_slice`, `first`, `sum`, ...) stayed `_DynGen0` cases. Each body names an
      `Enumerable_X[*Array[E], E]`, so Go compiled the wrapper and its chain once per element type: 393 Array shapes
      (245 tuple or interface, which share no GC shape) and 88 Hash shapes. Making them free funcs would not help, as
      decisions 86/211 already did that for the methods themselves; the per-shape cost is the dynamic layer.
    - **What:** `seqNames` and `hashViewNames` map each name to the view helper that answers it. `rbViewBlockCall`
      takes 34 Array names, `rbViewPredicate` `all?`/`any?`/`none?`/`one?` (block, `===` pattern, or truthiness),
      `rbViewSum` `sum` (`Integer#+` for bignum safety, `+` dispatch otherwise); `rbHashViewBlockCall` 24 Hash names,
      `rbHashViewPredicate` the four predicates. The predicates and `sum` are separate functions so only programs that
      call them dynamically keep the `===` and `+` dispatchers. Hash gets one more one-line accessor, `_pair_at`
      (a protocol name), so the view yields the same `Tuple2[K, V]` the wrappers did. Blocks get raw elements, as the
      wrappers passed them; a block's result is unboxed before it is tested (an `Integer?` element's nil pointer was
      truthy). `rbViewCall` now pops the block too: `sort`/`max`/`min` compare with it, `count`/`index`/`rindex` test
      with it, `uniq` keys by it (the block used to count as an argument and raise ArgumentError).
    - **Behaviour fixed on the way:** the wrappers tested block results with `rbAs[Boolean]` (a truthy non-Boolean
      raised TypeError) and blockless `none?`/`one?`/`any?` raised LocalJumpError; `each` and friends returned the last
      block value instead of the receiver. All now follow MRI.
    - **Auto-splat:** a literal block of two or more parameters called with untyped values (`genDynBlockCall`,
      `dynBlockValue`, `bindFromRest` over `untyped`) splats a lone Array or tuple argument (`rbAutoSplat`), as MRI does
      for a proc: `h.each { |k, v| }` through a computed send bound the pair to `k` and nil to `v`.
    - **Not changed:** blocks on an untyped receiver in a literal call still compile through decision 224's Hash/Array
      switch, not the dispatchers; the view serves computed sends, `public_send`, forwarded blocks and every other
      dispatcher route. Not covered by the view: the pinned `<=>`/`to_json`, gem-defined Hash methods
      (ActiveSupport's `_deep_transform_*`), and blockless Enumerator forms (still LocalJumpError).
    - **Measured** (`brew info wget`, same machine, `-c=4 -dwarf=false`): Array half alone, compiled functions
      366k → 279k, archive 885 → 660 MB, build 144 → 107 s wall; output equal to MRI for `info wget`/`jq`/`git` and
      `--json=v2 wget`. Small programs flat (`TestCompileCost`: word_count 1,339 → 1,339, 64_observable
      29,432 → 28,977, 116_cuba_web 28,631 → 28,641). Runtime: the view replaces typed closures with `any` calls only
      on paths that were already untyped (the wrappers boxed every block argument and result), so no typed path pays.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`lib/demo/views.rb`: every name through `public_send` on Integer, `String?`, tuple
      and `Integer?` Arrays and on Hashes, mutation, errors, frozen). *See also 304* (the element-level names join the view).

303. **A gem method Go rejects is stubbed in the Go text, not by compiling again** (amends 205; 2026-10-18). The traced build used to poison each gem method `go/types` rejected and run the whole compile again (prelude parse, link, every inference round, emit, prune, format), then type-check again: one extra pass whenever any method was rejected, which brew always has.
    - **What:** `stubRejected` type-checks the output once, replaces each rejected gem func's body with the same `panic(NewNotImplementedError(...))` the compiler emits, keeps its signature, and deletes each import only stubbed bodies used (the check's `types.Info.Uses` says which funcs use each package, so no second check is needed). The warning per method is unchanged.
    - **Why it is safe:** the signature stays, so no caller's Go changes and nothing new can fail (a recompile could cascade: the poisoned method's inferred return became `untyped`). Callers keep the method's narrower inferred type, which only matters on a path that already raises.
    - **Differences from a recompile:** what only the stubbed body reached stays in the output (brew info: 54 lines fewer in all, so in practice none); a stubbed iterator raises when called rather than when iterated (every iterator call is a `for range`, so the same place). `RB2GO_GOPOISON_DUMP=path` writes `path.0`, the Go before stubbing.
    - **Measured** (`brew info wget`, quiet 10-core macOS): gen 56.8 → 33.9 s with a second type-check, 31.5 s with one; peak RSS 4.0 → 1.9 GB; same 25 stubs; `info wget`, `info jq`, `info git`, `info --json=v2 wget` equal MRI. Untraced compiles never ran the loop: small programs unchanged.
    - **Proof:** `TestStubGoFuncs` (internal/compiler/gopoison_test.go): a rejected method and free func are stubbed, an import only they used is dropped, one kept code also uses stays, the caller is kept, and the result type-checks.
    - **The other passes:** a traced compile runs `compileWith` five times (first try, three inference rounds, final), each from a fresh model. Redoing setup (prelude, user collect, link) cost ~0.95 s a pass; `realPath` is now memoized for the process (resolved paths only), which takes it to ~0.77 s. Keeping the model across passes would save the rest (~3 s of 31) but needs a snapshot of everything the rounds mutate (`m.Ret`, ivars, poison): not done. `RB2GO_TIMING=1` now also ticks `trace`, `boot`, each `round`, `compile` and `gocheck`. Where gen goes now: rounds 7 s, format 9 s, Go check 6 s, setup 3 s, final infer+emit 2.5 s, MRI trace+boot 2.2 s.

304. **Element-level Array names on an untyped receiver go through the view too** (amends 236, 302; 2026-10-18). After 302, a generic Array's `_DynGen0` still held one case per remaining dynamic name for every element type, each calling that name's free func instantiated per type: brew info wget's ~370 Array shapes × ~47 names. Dropping every Array/Hash `_DynGen0` case (a measurement hack, not a fix) takes the build from 259k compiled funcs to 226k.
    - **What:** `rbViewCore` (prelude/go/view.go) answers `length`, `size`, `empty?`, `frozen?`, `to_a`, `entries`, `deconstruct`, `itself`, `dup`, `clear`, `[]`, `slice`, `at`, `fetch`, `fetch_values`, `dig`, `last`, `[]=`, `<<`, `push`, `append`, `unshift`, `prepend`, `pop`, `shift`, `shuffle`, `shuffle!`, `sample`; `rbViewGrep` answers `grep`/`grep_v` (its own function, as `rbViewPredicate`, so `===`'s dispatcher stays only where used). Each mirrors the prelude body's arity, conversions and messages; arguments are converted to the element type before anything is written (`rbViewChecked`), as a typed call converts them.
    - **Accessors:** `_trunc` became `_resize(n)`, which also grows with E's zero value (decision 7's padding for `[]=` past the end), and `_drop(n)` reslices from the front, so `shift` stays O(1). `rbFrozen` joins the interface (every Array has it).
    - **Behaviour fixed on the way:** `fetch(i) { |i| ... }` and `grep(p) { |x| ... }` called dynamically ignored their block; `fetch(i, nil)` raised IndexError instead of returning nil; `slice(i)`/`slice(i, n)` took only a Range.
    - **Wording:** a conversion's TypeError names "element" instead of "E" (decision 20: rb2go words it apart anyway).
    - **Not changed:** names whose body is already a Go method on `Array[E]` (`==`, `eql?`, `hash`, `inspect`, `to_s`, `freeze`) keep their `_DynGen0` case, which instantiates nothing new; `pack` and `shelljoin` (lib-gated helpers) stay too. Names gem code adds to Array/Hash (Homebrew's `present?`, `blank?`, `compact_blank`, `second`..`fifth`, `deep_dup`, `deep_*_keys`) are the gem's Ruby bodies, so the view must not answer them; they are the next ~10k funcs. The Hash side's entry-level names (`[]`, `fetch`, `key?`, `delete`, `[]=`, `length`, `dig`, `dup`) were tried and reverted: a Hash `_DynGen0` case mostly calls a free func typed code instantiates anyway, while each view accessor costs a forwarder and a free func per instantiation, so brew info gained only 681 funcs (242,037 → 241,356) and 116_cuba_web, which calls all of them dynamically, lost 190 (28,233 → 28,423), even with one narrow helper per accessor. `testdata/boot`'s `hash_core` keeps them covered on the per-instantiation path.
    - **Measured** (brew info wget): compiled funcs 259,271 → 242,037, package archive 1.25 GB → 936 MB; compile of main.go, interleaved pairs on the same loaded machine: 122/140 s → 81/103 s. `info wget`, `info jq`, `info git`, `info --json=v2 wget` equal MRI. TestCompileCost: 05_word_count 1,339 → 1,339, 64_observable 28,978 → 27,086, 116_cuba_web 28,641 → 28,233.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` (`Demo::Views.core`, `core_errors`: every name through `public_send` on Integer and `String?` arrays, writes, splices, frozen and index errors, seeded `random:`).

305. **Symbol procs into gem blocks, and pruned aliases** (amends 169, 223, 244; 2026-10-18). The unmodified `pairing_heap` gem (example 120) needed three things.
    - **An unreached inherited alias is pruned with its target.** `alias_method :extract_min, :dequeue` in a subclass, where `dequeue` is the superclass's own alias, waits in `inheritedAliases` until `linkAliases` (decision 169's pruner never saw it). Unreached, the pruner dropped `dequeue` but kept the pending alias, which then could not find it. `prune` now drops a pending alias by the rule it applies to methods.
    - **`super(&:sym)` / `super(&proc)`** pass the block as the same `&expr` on a call would (`genClosure`). Decision 223 allowed only a literal block or the method's own `&block`.
    - **`:sym.to_proc` is compiled at the call site** where a Proc is expected: the lambda `&:sym` would build, typed by the expected Proc. Into a gem's untyped Proc or block (`^(*untyped) -> untyped`, decision 244: `@order = block || :<=.to_proc`, `super(&:<=)`), it is a variadic Go func that sends the name to its first argument through the dynamic dispatcher, with the rest as arguments; no arguments raise ArgumentError "no receiver given", as MRI does. No `Symbol#to_proc` method exists at run time; a non-literal symbol's `to_proc` is still unsupported.
    - **Not done** (*see also decision 306*, which now reads it): the gem ships `sig/pairing_heap.rbs`, which rb2go does not read (only `sig/gems/<gem>/` and a `sig/` beside the program). Its `class PairingHeap[Elem, Priority]` would also need generic struct classes (decision 44 says they may not exist), so the gem's internals stay untyped, and a Dijkstra + heapsort workload runs slower than MRI (0.98 s vs 0.71 s). *(See also decisions 308-309: generic struct classes and generic superclasses now exist.)*
    - **Proof:** example 120 (`examples/120_pairing_heap`), `object_test.rb` `ObjectSuperSymbolBlockTest`.
    *See also decision 307:* the pairing_heap internals now type (rbDyn 56 → 5; benchmark 0.49 → 0.17 s).

306. **A gem's own shipped `sig/` is read, leniently** (amends 160, 305; 2026-10-18). Decision 160 read only `sig/gems/<gem>/` (vendored) and a `sig/` beside the program; a gem that ships RBS (pairing_heap, ffi, rbs, prism, webrick) had its types ignored.
    - **Where:** for each gem lib dir whose base is `lib` (`boot.FindGems`' `full_require_paths`, or a traced build's `-I` dirs), `../sig` is walked for `*.rbs`, skipping `_`-prefixed dirs as RBS's FileFinder does. Precedence, first seen wins per member (`addSig`): the program's `sig/` > vendored `sig/gems/<gem>/` > the gem's own, so a vendored file can patch a shipped sig.
    - **Lenient:** a shipped file `rbs.ParseFile` rejects is skipped whole with a gem warning (shown under `RB2GO_GEM_WARNINGS=1`, decision 205), never a compile error; the program's and vendored sigs stay strict.
    - **Generics erased (shim):** a shipped `class C[Elem, Priority]` that no earlier sig or core class gives params would make C a generic struct class, which is not compiled yet (#104; `model.go` errors). Its params are dropped, and each member whose type names one of them (a method in any overload, block included; an attr, ivar or constant) is dropped too, so inference types it exactly as with no sig; members with no type variable (`size: Integer`, `empty?: bool`) stay. A gem warning names the class (`eraseGemGenerics`, `// ponytail:` until #104 lands). Reading the variables as `untyped` instead was tried first and rejected: it overrode inference (`elem` String → any, new dynamic `<=`/`>=` on the priority), against minimize-untyped. *(See also decision 312: the shim now applies only to a class whose superclass is a core `@go_type`; generic struct classes keep their params.)*
    - **RBS grammar:** `alias new old` in a class decl (`MethodAliases`): `old`'s sig also types `new` when `new` has none, following alias chains in the same decl. Header type params match brackets by depth (`[E, P < Comparable[P]] < Heap[E, P]` kept its superclass only by accident before: it was dropped). Also read now: members continued across lines (an open bracket, a leading or trailing `|`), `%a{...}` annotations (dropped), `def x: ...` and `| ...` arms (nothing to add), a method type with no `()` (`-> T`, `{ ... } -> T`), method type-param bounds (`[P < Pointer]`, dropped), a keyword parameter's variable name (`name: T name`), and class aliases (`class A = B`, which open no body).
