Of the 220 `.rbs` files under the installed gems, 54 failed to parse before, 12 after: intersection types `A & B` (prism, rbs: 7), single-quoted string literals (rbs: 3), a backquoted parameter name and a named proc parameter (1 each). *(See also decision 315: those four forms now parse, 0 of 220 rejected.)*
    - **Measured** (example 120, pairing_heap 3.1.2's sig, whose unparenthesised `-> [A] | [nil, nil]` folds into one union return and whose `Comparable[Priority]` bound is dropped): output equals MRI; `rbDyn` references 84 → 84, the gem's dynamic-call warnings identical to no sig; generated funcs 597 → 597. What the sig still types is what has no type variable: `MinPriorityQueue#initialize: () -> void` drops the block parameter inference had given it. Almost all of the shipped sig names Elem/Priority, so its value waits on #104. TestCompileCost unchanged (05_word_count 1,339, 64_observable 27,086, 116_cuba_web 28,233 compiled funcs).
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot/sigdemo` (a second gem dir in `TestBootAutoload`: its `sig/sigdemo.rbs` types `Meter#total` as Integer and `push` through `alias push add`; `sig/broken.rbs` only warns), `TestSigsShipped`, `internal/rbs` `TestParseFileNestedBoundAndMethodAlias`, example 120.

307. **Linked structures type statically: nil-initialized links, stored `&block`s, includer-only module calls** (amends 15, 146, 148, 183, 243, 305; 2026-10-18). The unmodified `pairing_heap` gem (example 120) compiled with 56 `rbDyn` calls in its own functions: every Node link (`@subheaps`, `@parent`, `@prev_sibling`, `@next_sibling`, set nil in `initialize` and a Node later through `attr_accessor` writers), the heap's `@root` and `@nodes = {}`, the comparator `@order = block || :<=.to_proc`, and `MergePairs#merge_pairs`, a module method shared by two heap classes with different Node classes, were untyped. Each fix is general (any linked list or tree has the shape) and applies to user code too.
    - **Attr writers had one inference key.** `attr_accessor :elem, :priority, :parent` declares its writers at one position, each with a parameter named `v`, and decision 146's key is position + name: every writer of one call joined into one type (`Integer | String | Node`), which a gem then widened to untyped (244) and user code failed on (`possibly-nil LNode? where Integer is expected`). The writer's parameter is now named for the attribute (`collect_methods.go` `addModuleAttr`).
    - **A round's untyped branch is not yet learned.** `@root = @root ? meld(@root, n) : n`: in a round meld's parameters are untyped, so its result is, and the join with `n`'s Node was untyped, so `@root` was, so nothing ever passed meld a Node. In an inference round (`c.round`), a branch value holding untyped drops out of an `if`/`case`/ternary's join when a typed branch remains (`lift`), as an untyped use already counts for nothing in `joinUses`. Rounds' output is discarded; the final compile joins as before, and by then meld returns a Node.
    - **Discovery passes nil where a typed parameter is expected.** The same line, in ivar discovery, reads `@root` (no write typed it yet) as nil (decision 148) and passes it to meld's now-typed `a`: "nil where Node is expected" lost the statement, so the write that types `@root`, so meld's only typed use, and the rounds oscillated. In discovery, nil coerces to the zero value instead (`expr_coerce.go`); discovery's output is discarded and the emitting pass still reports a real nil.
    - **A gem's lookup conversions do not veto a container ivar** (amends 15). `@nodes = {}` typed `Hash[String, Node]` from `@nodes[elem] = node`, then was dropped because the typing added conversions: `@nodes.delete(elem)` with `elem` untyped (`delete(elem)` is kept only by its name), and writer calls on the Node it returns. In gem code only conversions where something is stored veto: an argument of an element-adding Hash/Array call (`<<`, `push`, `[]=`, `store`, `concat`, ..., `storeNames`) or an ivar assignment. A demo gem's `@log << x` with x untyped still keeps `@log` untyped (else `Array[Symbol]` would reject the Integer at run time). User code keeps every conversion as a veto. A gem's lookup on a typed Hash or Array whose key is untyped or a union (Go `any`) converts it with a check (`rbConv[K]`) and takes MRI's miss when it does not convert, instead of raising TypeError: `nil` for `delete`/`dig`/`Array#index`, `false` for `key?`/`has_key?`/`include?`/`member?`, the Hash's `default` for `[]`, the default argument for `fetch(k, d)` (`lenientLookup`, `lookupMiss`). Not covered: `fetch(k)` (still TypeError where MRI raises KeyError), a lookup with a block, and a default-proc Hash's `[]` (its static default, not the proc's value). See also 314: `fetch(k)`, `fetch(k) { }` and the default-proc case.
    - **A stored `&block` takes its calls' argument types** (amends 183). An unannotated def's `&block` was `{ (*untyped) -> untyped }`, so `@order[priority, node.priority]` boxed both Integers and `:<=.to_proc` sent `<=` through the dispatcher. The block's Proc type now carries a key (`blockSrcAt`: the `&block` parameter's position), and each call of that Proc (`.call`, `[]`, `.()`; also through the ivar it was stored in: `subst` keeps a TFunc's `Src`) records each argument by position and the argument count. When every call passed the same count (1 to 8, no splat), the next compile's block is `{ (T0, ..., Tn) -> untyped }` (`typeStoredBlock`, from `resolveMethod`): a caller's block literal is typed, and decision 305's `:<=.to_proc` compiles to `func(a, b Integer) any { a <= b }`. The result stays untyped (ponytail: infer it from the blocks passed). Counts that disagree keep the variadic block, since a Proc takes any.
    - **Dyn wrappers pass an optional typed block as itself** (amends 243). A wrapper for a typed optional block boxed its adapter as a Proc, and passing that Proc wrapped it in a closure that is never nil, so `@hook = block` stored a non-nil func when the dynamic call gave no block (`DemoPool.new.report`: "undefined method for nil"). The adapter func is passed as the block itself, nil when none was given (`dynBlockArg`, `genClosure`); where the call is rewritten to another block shape (optparse's `on_tail` to `__on_tail_flag`'s `(bool)`), it is adapted as the Proc it was before (else brew lost `OptionParser#DynOnTail` and every `brew info` failed).
    - **A module method calling what only its includers define is copied into each includer** (`copyIncluderTemplates`, after `applyPrepends`). `MergePairs#merge_pairs` calls `meld`, which each heap defines for its own Node: one shared free func had to take untyped heaps (the two Node classes do not join, 244) and call `meld` dynamically. An unannotated method of a non-prelude module, included only by struct classes and not extended, whose body calls on self a name the module, Object and the top level do not answer and every includer does, without a block, is copied into each includer that does not define it (`Owner` the includer, so its parameters infer per class), and removed from the module. Not copied: a method naming an ivar (the module's own state, decision 147), calling `super`, or passing a block to the includer-only call (concurrent-ruby's `synchronize { ... }` may compile as an iterator, which loses the value the dynamic call answered; seen in `TestBootAutoload`'s `DemoDeref#value_with`). MRI-visible difference: `instance_method(:merge_pairs).owner` would name the class (ponytail). *(See also decision 313: each generic instance is an includer of its own, and this is the one per-includer copy mechanism.)*
    - **Also:** `x.nil?` in a condition generated its receiver twice when the receiver's type was neither optional nor untyped (a union from a type switch): the first, unused, value broke `go build` ("declared and not used") and ran a side-effecting receiver twice. The receiver is typed by a probe first (`genCondCall`). `include M` of a module with no type parameters no longer warns that it is untyped.
    - **Measured** (example 120, decision 306's shipped sig in place): `rbDyn` in `PairingHeap*` functions 56 → 5 (`NodeVisitor.visit_node`'s `stack.pop` elements, whose `if node.subheaps; stack << node.subheaps` does not narrow a call, and `merge(other)`, which no call reaches), whole program 79 → 11, dynamic-call warnings 67 → 8, generated funcs 597 → 556. `benchmarks/pairing_heap.rb` (Dijkstra with decrease-key over a 150×150 grid, then a 100,000-element heapsort; `RUNS=9 scripts/benchmark pairing_heap`, load ~8): MRI 0.70 s, rb2go 0.49 → 0.17 s (1.4x → 4.1x). Its `x = heap.pop #: Integer` became `x && x >= prev`: `pop` now returns `Integer?`, which an annotation does not take unchecked. TestCompileCost unchanged (05_word_count 1,339, 64_observable 27,086, 116_cuba_web 28,233 compiled funcs).
    - **Brew** (`overrides/brew/work/homebrew/bin/brew info wget`, macOS, traced with `info jq`, `info git`, `info --json=v2 wget` too, main 6a06774 vs this branch, behind the suite lock, load ~5-8): all four outputs equal MRI on both; compiled funcs 242,037 → 241,956 (archive 555.6 → 555.4 MB); gen 32.6 → 32.5 s, gen RSS 2.31 → 2.03 GB; traced build 122 → 113 s; main.go's go build 66.4 → 65.7 s, 11.4 → 11.0 GB RSS.
    - **Left:** a `T?` of a struct class is `*TI`, so each link write boxes the interface (`Ref(left)`), an allocation the untyped `any` did not need; call-result narrowing (`if node.subheaps; ... node.subheaps`); the stored block's result type.
    - **Proof:** `TestBootAutoload`'s `DemoKeyed` (a gem `Hash[String, Integer]` and `Array[String]` probed with 1, :ab and "ab": every miss as MRI's), `TestNilLinksTyped` (rb2go_test.go: a nil-linked heap with a stored comparator and a two-includer merge module compiles with no dynamic call; it fails before this decision), `infer_test.rb` `LinkTest` (the same shapes against MRI: two includers with different node classes, a block and the `:<=` default), `TestBootAutoload` (DemoPool's optional block through a Dyn wrapper, DemoFallbackExec's untyped `@log << x`, DemoDeref's block-passing mixin), example 120.
308. **Generic struct classes are monomorphized** (#104 phase 1; amends 44, 10, 210, 212; 2026-10-18). A class with `# @rbs generic T` (or a sig's `class Box[T]`) whose objects are Go structs used to be the link error "generic struct classes are not supported"; only `@go_type` classes could be generic.
    - **Options weighed.** Go generics (`type Box[T comparable] struct`, as decision 165's Hash subclass) turn off decisions 210 and 212 for the class (a generic owner's bodies stay generic, its calls go through interface methods) and leave every call on a `T` value dynamic or constrained; erasure (one `Box` over `any`) makes every read a conversion and every `T` call dynamic. Monomorphization emits one ordinary struct class per closed instantiation the program names, so direct calls, type-switch dispatch and class-ID dispatch on untyped values all apply unchanged, and Go never sees a type parameter. The cost is one struct per instantiation, paid only by programs that use the class.
    - **Template and instances.** The class as declared is a *template*: it is collected and linked like any class (its signatures resolve with `TVar`s) but never emitted, and its bodies are never generated (`isTemplate`; discovery, return inference, rounds and emit skip it). `instOf(template, args)` (`generic.go`) is an *instance*: a clone of the template's collected methods, ivars, includes and annotations, made before `nameGo`/`link`, whose signatures and ivar annotations resolve the template's parameter names to its arguments (`resolveNameType`). Every pass then treats it as an ordinary class. A type `Box[Integer]` anywhere (annotation, sig, a substituted signature) resolves to the instance (`classType`), so a call on it resolves on the instance's own methods, never by substituting into the template.
    - **Naming.** The instance's Go name is `Box_G<fnv32a of its RBS key>` (`Box[Integer]`), with the key as a comment on its struct: deterministic across compiles (decision 88's build cache), and `_G` plus hex is not a name decision 3's camel-casing produces from a Ruby name (a clash with a user class literally so named gains `_` in `nameGo`, as any clash does). `TClass.String()` of an instance is its key, so messages say `Box[Integer]`.
    - **Ruby identity.** Instances share the template's metaclass (`Box.class`, `.name`, `x.class == Box`) and Go class variable. Each instance keeps its own class ID, so decision 209's class-ID dispatch on an untyped receiver tells `Box[Integer]` from `Box[String]` apart; `rbAncestry` lists the template for each (so `is_a?`/`kind_of?`/`===` at run time answer as MRI), and `instance_of?` maps an ID to its template through a generated `rbRubyClassID` switch. A family marker, `Box_Any` (an interface every instance implements with an empty `_Box_Any()` method), is what an untyped `is_a?(Box)`, `Box === x` and `when Box` assert; such an arm keeps the value untyped, since it may be any instance.
    - **`Box.new(args)`.** With an expected type that is an instance (`b = Box.new(nil) #: Box[Integer?]`, a parameter typed `Box[untyped]`), that instance; otherwise initialize's parameters bind the template's parameters from the arguments (probed with `genArgs`, as a generic method's call binds its own). An unbound parameter is the compile error "cannot infer type parameter T of Box.new" (phase 3 infers it from later use).
    - **Invariance.** Instances are distinct classes: a `Box[Integer]` passed where a `Box[untyped]` is expected is a compile error, never a copy. `Box[untyped]` is the one erased instance, built only where the program names it.
    - **Discovery is a restart.** Instances are created before link, so a compile that names one it lacks records its key (`opts.instances`), types it as the template meanwhile (the template's signatures substituted, as for `Array[E]`) and, at the end or at any error, returns `errNeedInstances`; `compile` runs again with it (at most 32 times). One pass finds every sibling instance; only nesting (`Stack[E]#top_box` naming `Box[E]`) costs another. An inference round that names one continues to the next round, which has it.
    - **Polymorphic recursion** (`Box[T]#wrap` returning `Box[Array[T]]`) would need instances without end: an instance whose type arguments nest more than 8 deep is the compile error "instantiates Box without end (polymorphic recursion)".
    - **Not yet:** generic inheritance (phase 2), `X.new` with no binding argument (phase 3), class methods on a generic class (still an error), `klass.new` on a generic class object held untyped (no class-object `new`: NoMethodError; ponytail), and an unannotated parameter of a template's method (its inferred uses join across instances).
    - **Measured** (TestCompileCost, compiled funcs): 05_word_count 1,339 → 1,339, 64_observable 27,086 → 27,087 (`rbRubyClassID`), 116_cuba_web 28,233 → 28,233.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/test/generic_class_test.rb` (`Box[T]`, `Stack[E]`, `Bag[E]` including `Enumerable #[E]`, `Pair[K, V]`, each at two or more instantiations; `.class`, `is_a?`, `instance_of?`, `inspect`, `instance_variable_get`, `send` and `map(&:get)` on untyped values, `case/when`, `===`); `testdata/errors/generics.txtar` (unbound parameter, wrong arity, invariance, argument mismatch, polymorphic recursion). `objects.txtar`'s `generic_struct_class` rejection is gone.
309. **A generic superclass takes type arguments** (#104 phase 2; amends 308, 305, 8; 2026-10-18). rbs-inline's `class MinQ < Heap #[E, P]` (the `#[...]` on the class line, read as `include X #[...]` is) or a sig's `class MinQ[E, P] < Heap[E, P]` (`ClassDecl.Super`) names the superclass's arguments (`superArgs`).
    - **Templates.** A generic class whose superclass is a struct class (Object, a template, or a plain struct class) is a template too (`markTemplates`, before link: `isTemplate` no longer requires no superclass). A template's own `Super` is the parent template, typed generically and never emitted.
    - **Instances embed the parent instance.** An instance resolves `superArgs` in its own scope (so `Flipped[A, B] < Heap #[B, A]` remaps, and arguments may be any closed type), and its superclass is that parent instance (`superInst`, made with it in `makeInstances`): `MinQ[String, Integer]` embeds `Heap[String, Integer]` as any struct subclass embeds its parent, so `super`, inherited calls, overrides (decision 8's adapters), `is_a?(Heap)` and the parent's interface all work unchanged. Two subclass instances over the same arguments share one parent instance, which then dispatches over both (decision 212). A non-generic child (`class IntHeap < Heap #[Integer, Integer]`) is an ordinary class whose superclass is `Heap[Integer, Integer]`.
    - **Class objects.** `metaFor` of any instance is its template's metaclass, so `MinQ.superclass` is `Heap` and `IntHeap.ancestors` is `[IntHeap, Heap, Object, ...]`, as MRI answers.
    - **Inherited aliases** (decision 305's `alias_method :extract_min, :dequeue` over a superclass's alias): a template's pending alias is copied to each of its instances, where the target is a plain instance method and copies as any; the template's own is skipped. Decision 305's error for an alias of a generic owner's method stays only for `@go_type` owners.
    - **Proof:** `generic_class_test.rb` (`MinQ[E, P] < Heap #[E, P]` with `super` and an alias of an inherited alias, `Flipped[A, B] < Heap #[B, A]`, `IntHeap < Heap #[Integer, Integer]`; `superclass`, `ancestors`, `instance_of?`, `===` on an untyped value).
310. **An open generic `X.new` is typed by later calls** (#104 phase 3; amends 308, 182; 2026-10-18). `q = MinQ.new` binds nothing (initialize takes no `E` or `P`), which decision 308 rejected. Like decision 182's empty `Hash.new`, it is now an open literal when assigned to an unannotated local (`openTemplate`: an `X.new` of a template whose arguments' parameters do not mention every type parameter).
    - **Evidence.** Pass 1 types the local as the template at `untyped` arguments (no instance is made: genNew returns a placeholder of that type), and each later call on it (`noteTemplateArgs`) unifies its positional arguments with the method's parameters: `q.push("a", 1)` against `push(E, P)` records `E = String`, `P = Integer`. Pass 2 builds the instance of the joined evidence (`refineTemplate`), so `X.new` takes it as its expected type. Typed evidence joins as a parameter's uses do (decision 146): `h.push(:x, 1.5); h.push(7, 0.5)` is `Heap[Integer | Symbol, Float]`; untyped evidence counts for nothing.
    - **No evidence** keeps decision 308's "cannot infer type parameter E of Heap.new" in user code. In gem code the parameter is `untyped` (the erased instance) with a gem warning, as a gem's empty container is (decision 192).
    - **Not yet:** an open `X.new` assigned to an ivar (decision 182's `refineIvars` path) or passed straight to a call; keyword and block arguments as evidence.
    - **Proof:** `generic_class_test.rb` `test_open_new_typed_by_later_calls`; `generics.txtar` `new_unbound` (no evidence).
311. **A helper class nested in a generic class takes its parameters** (#104 phase 4; amends 308, 146; 2026-10-18). A linked-list or heap node (`Chain::Link`, pairing_heap's `PairingHeap::Node`) is usually an unannotated class whose ivars hold the outer class's `T`: one shared class would join every instance's uses (`Integer | String`, decision 146) or go untyped.
    - **Which classes.** A plain struct class declared directly in a template's body (its constant is `Outer::Name`), with no superclass, no type parameters, no `def self.`, no sig and no method with a `#:` signature (`markHelpers`), becomes a template over the outer template's parameter names (`helperOf`). Inference decides its types per instance, so the parameters need not appear in any signature; an annotated helper is the author's, and keeps its declared types.
    - **Where it is named.** `Link` (a constant in `Link.new`, or a type in an annotation such as `# @rbs @head: Link?`) inside the code of an instance of the outer template, or of the helper itself, along the class's superclasses (`helperArgs`, so a subclass instance's inherited bodies find it too), is the helper's instance at that instance's arguments: `Chain[Integer]`'s `push` builds `Chain::Link[Integer]`. In the template's own (never compiled) code it is the helper at its type variables.
    - **Inference per instance.** A pending parameter's key already carries its owner's Go name (decision 253), so `Link[Integer]#initialize(val, rest)` and `Link[String]#initialize` infer apart. Two fixes on the way: an inferred type naming an instance carries over to the next compile by instance key (`rehome`, which went by Ruby name and found the template), and a template's own pending parameters, which no call reaches, are untyped without decision 183's warning.
    - **Also fixed:** a class body's `# @rbs @x: T` scan (`collectIvarDecls`) took the annotations of every nested class too (an annotation before a nested `def` is keyed to that line), so a module around `Chain` failed to resolve `Chain`'s `@head: Link?`. A nested class's lines but its first are now left to it; Date's `@jd` keys to its nested `class Error` line and stays Date's.
    - **Not done:** a helper reached from outside the outer class's code (`Chain::Link.new(1)` in main) cannot bind its parameters and is decision 308's "cannot infer" error; only direct nesting counts (a helper's own helpers stay plain).
    - **Proof:** `generic_class_test.rb` `test_nested_helper_takes_outer_params` (`Chain[Integer]` and `Chain[String]` over an unannotated `Link` with `attr_accessor`s, inferred `initialize`, and an ivar annotated with the helper's bare name).
312. **Type-parameter bounds, and gem sigs' generic classes** (#104 phase 5; amends 306, 160, 308, 310; 2026-10-18).
    - **Bounds.** `# @rbs generic P < Comparable` and a sig's `class C[P < Comparable[P]]` (`ClassDecl.Bounds`, read by `typeBounds` in `internal/rbs`: the bound's class name, its arguments dropped) bound a template's parameter. Every instance is checked in link, once supers and includes are linked (`checkInstanceBounds`; before link `isSubclassOf` has no ancestors yet): an argument whose class is not a subclass of the bound (modules count through includes, a union through each member, nil and untyped pass) is the compile error "Best[Object]: Object is not a Comparable, the bound of Best's P", at the place the instance was first named (`instAt`, kept across restarts). Gem code is not checked: MRI never checks an RBS bound, and a gem's sig may bound what its callers pass loosely.
    - **Gem sigs.** Decision 306's shim (`eraseGemGenerics`) dropped a shipped sig's class type params and every member naming them. A sig-generic struct class is now a template like any (decision 160 already gave it the sig's `TypeParams`), so the shim is kept only for a class whose superclass is a core `@go_type` (a generic Hash subclass is generic over `V` alone, decision 165). Class methods on a generic gem class compile, their sigs seeing the type params as untyped (user code keeps the error).
    - **pairing_heap** (example 120, with the gem's shipped `sig/pairing_heap.rbs` loaded): `MinPriorityQueue.new` is open (decision 310) and typed `[String, Integer]` by the later `push(source, 0)`; `MinPriorityQueue[String, Integer]` and `MaxPriorityQueue[String, Integer]` share one `PairingHeap[String, Integer]` parent instance, `SimplePairingHeap.new { |l, r| ... }` (a block is no evidence; the open check now admits it) is typed by `words.push(w)` alone, and `PairingHeap::Node` and `SimplePairingHeap::Node` are helpers (decision 311) instantiated with them.
    - **Two fixes it needed.** An omitted argument whose default is an earlier parameter (`def push(elem, priority = elem)`) is evidence for that parameter's type too (`noteTemplateArgs`), so `push(w)` binds `Priority = String`. In gem code, a default that does not fit its parameter in one instance (`priority = elem` in `PairingHeap[String, Integer]`, where Elem is not Priority) is converted at run time (`rbAs`) when it runs, instead of poisoning the method (decision 205).
    - **The example changed** where the sig changed its types: `pop_with_priority` is `[Elem, Priority] | [nil, nil]`, so `top = queue.pop_with_priority #: [String, Integer]` is decision 150's "narrow it first" error; it is now `node, d = queue.pop_with_priority` plus `next unless node && d`, MRI's output unchanged.
    - **Measured** (example 120): `rbDyn` references 84 → 76, generated funcs 597 → 583; output equals MRI. What stays dynamic is `merge_pairs`' parameter (decision 313) and the `nil`/`{}`-initialized `@root` and `@nodes` (decision 307's nil-initialized ivars).
    - **Proof:** example 120; `generic_class_test.rb` `test_bounded_parameter`; `generics.txtar` `bound`; `internal/rbs` `TestParseFileNestedBoundAndMethodAlias` (bounds).
313. **A module method a generic instance needs typed its own way is copied per includer** (#104 phase 6; amends 307, 308; 2026-10-18). A module body is one free function generic over `Self` (decision 9), and its unannotated parameters join every includer's arguments (decision 146), so generic includers mixed their instances' types: `Twice#twice(x)` called from `Doubler[Integer]` and `Doubler[String]` became `x: String` and failed at the Integer call.
    - **One mechanism: decision 307's.** `copyIncluderTemplates` (decision 307) copies a module method that calls on self what only its includers define into each includer. Instances are ordinary classes in `classList` by link, each including the module (its includes are cloned from the template's), so each instance gets its own copy, whose pending keys carry the instance's Go name and so infer apart. A separate clone-at-instantiation pass (`cloneModuleMethods`, built in parallel with 307) covered the same proofs and was dropped at the merge: 307's narrower rule (an includer-only self call) is what makes a per-includer type necessary, and it also removes the module's own copy, so no stray untyped-parameter warning is left behind.
    - **Merge fix.** An inference round that named an instance it lacked (decision 308's restart) still recorded its argument types, the template standing in for the instance: a `Node` with no instance (`PairingHeap::PairingHeap::NodeI`, undeclared) then typed 307's nil-initialized `@root` and `meld`'s parameters in the final compile, which failed `go build`. Such a round's uses are now discarded (`updateInference` is skipped when `instMissing`); the round after the restart records them on the instance.
    - **Measured** (example 120 with the shipped sig, combined with 307): output equals MRI; `rbDyn` references 11 (307 alone: 11), generated funcs 556 (556), gem dynamic-call warnings 5 (8). `RUNS=9 scripts/benchmark pairing_heap`: MRI 0.671 s, rb2go 0.159 s (4.2x; 307 alone 0.17 s). Generic instances add no dynamic call over 307's typing: pairing_heap's main program uses one element/priority shape per heap class, which 307 already typed by joining.
    - **Brew** (`overrides/brew/work/homebrew/bin/brew info wget`, traced with `info jq`, `info git`, `info --json=v2 wget`, macOS, main 9410ae5 vs this branch, behind the suite lock): all four equal MRI on both; compiled funcs 241,956 → 241,957 (`rbRubyClassID`), archive 555.4 MB both; RB2GO_TIMING phase ticks summed over the traced build's compiles 54.4 → 54.9 s, traced build+run 112 → 113 s; no generic-instance restarts (`RB2GO_TIMING` reports them: brew declares no generic struct class). TestCompileCost: 05_word_count 1,339, 64_observable 27,086 → 27,087, 116_cuba_web 28,239 (main's own number today) → 28,239.
    - **Proof:** `generic_class_test.rb` `test_module_method_per_instance`; example 120; `benchmarks/pairing_heap.rb` (its `top = queue.pop_with_priority #: [Integer, Integer]` is decision 312's union now: `cell, d = queue.pop_with_priority` plus `next unless cell && d`).
314. **A gem's mistyped-key `fetch` misses as MRI's, and a default proc refuses it** (amends 307; 2026-10-18). Decision 307 left three lookups raising rb2go's `TypeError` where a gem's untyped key of another class reaches a typed Hash. Now (`lenientLookup`):
    - `fetch(k)`: `KeyError` with MRI's message (`key not found: <inspect>`), `key` and `receiver`, via `KeyError.__for`.
    - `fetch(k) { |x| ... }`: the block is compiled a second time with `x` untyped and called with the key, as MRI yields it; a hit calls the typed block.
    - `h[k]` where `h` has a default proc: the proc is typed `(Hash[K, V], K)`, so it cannot be handed the key, and MRI would store a key the Hash cannot hold. It raises `TypeError` naming the key class and the file:line instead of returning the static default (nil), which hid the miss. A Hash without a proc still returns its default. Only this branch differs from MRI; the fixture cannot check it against MRI.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` `DemoKeyed#probe_fetches` (Integer, Symbol and String keys into `Hash[String, Integer]`). Decision 307's `on_tail` adaptation now has a check too: `DemoTailCli` (an untyped parser's `on_tail { |v| }`, rewritten to `__on_tail_flag`'s `(bool)`); with `funcBlockArg` passing the func as itself the build fails.
315. **Intersection types, single-quoted strings, backquoted and proc parameter names in RBS** (amends 306, 12, 150; 2026-10-18). Decision 306 left 12 of the 220 installed gems' `sig/` files unparsable, so each was skipped whole: prism 1.7-1.9's `node.rbs` and `parse_result.rbs` (`type node = Node & _Node`), rbs 4.2's `rdoc_annotater.rbs` (`Array[Object & _WithRDocComment]`), `collection/config.rbs`, `config/lockfile.rbs`, `sources.rbs` (`{ 'type' => 'git', ..., }`), `type_alias_dependency.rbs` (``(Types::t `type`, ...)``) and webrick 1.9.2's `httprequest.rbs` (`^(String body_chunk) -> void`).
    - **Grammar** (ruby/rbs `docs/syntax.md`, `src/util/rbs_unescape.c`): `A & B` is `rbs.Intersection`, binding tighter than `|` and looser than `?` (`A | B & C?` is `A | (B & (C?))`); it prints with parentheses where nested in a union, intersection or optional, as a union does. `'...'` is a string literal whose only escapes are `\'` and `\\`; the tokenizer rewrites it as the double-quoted token of the same text, so literal types (decision 12) and record keys take it unchanged. A backquoted name (`` `type` ``, any text but a backquote) is a parameter name and prints backquoted unless it is a plain identifier. A proc type's parameter may carry a name, which is documentation and dropped (`rbs.Proc` holds types only). A record's trailing comma already parsed.
    - **Compiler mapping of `A & B`** (`resolveIntersection`). A value of the intersection is a value of every operand, so any one operand types it soundly; Go has no type that is a struct and an interface at once, and an RBS interface (`_Node`, a name whose last segment starts with `_`) names no class in rb2go (one alone resolves as an unknown name: untyped in gem code, an error in user code). So: the first operand that resolves to a class wins (`Node & _Node` is `Node`, `Object & _WithRDocComment` is `Object`); else the first typed one (a module: `Comparable & _Each` is `Comparable`); interfaces are skipped while any other operand remains; when every operand is an interface the first resolves as one alone does. Rejected: untyped for every intersection (against minimize-untyped: prism's `node` is a real class), and a Go interface built from the interface's methods (the closed world never declares RBS interfaces as types; one would be the first such machinery for a gem-only form). The interface's extra methods are not checked on the class: a call the class lacks is what decision 205 already handles in gem code.
    - **Census** (`rbs.ParseFile` over `/opt/homebrew/lib/ruby/gems/4.0.0/gems/*/sig/**/*.rbs`): 220 files, 12 unparsable before, 0 after.
    - **Measured:** TestCompileCost unchanged (05_word_count 1,339, 64_observable 27,087, 116_cuba_web 28,239 compiled funcs).
    - **Proof:** `internal/rbs` `TestParseTypeShapes` (intersections, precedence, single quotes and their escapes, a record's trailing comma, a named proc parameter), `TestParseMethodTypeParams` (backquoted names), `TestParseTypeErrors`, fuzz seeds; `internal/compiler` `TestResolveIntersection`; `testdata/boot/sigdemo/sig/sigdemo.rbs` (`Meter & _Reading`, a `'single-quoted'` record with a `'meter'` literal, ``Meter & _Reading `other` ``, a named proc parameter, in type aliases and method sigs), whose types `TestBootAutoload` checks in main.go and whose output equals MRI.

316. **A computed `send`/`respond_to?` on a receiver of known class switches over that class's names only** (amends 32, 235; 2026-10-19). `rbSendByName` and `rbRespondsByName` switch over every method name in the program, and `genSend` boxed the receiver to `any` even when its static type was a struct class: one `x.send(m)` on a typed `x` kept a dispatcher for every name and a Dyn wrapper on every class (`64_observable`, 40 lines: 741 dispatchers + 2,487 wrappers, 30% of its functions, from one `send`). Now a receiver typed as a struct class `C` (or `Self` in `C`'s body, or a union of struct classes) compiles to `rbSendOn_C(recv, name, how, args...)` / `rbRespondsOn_C(recv, name, priv)` (`noteSendOn`, `emitSendOn` in `dynamic.go`): a switch over what `C` and its subclasses answer (`sendOnNames`: their method sets, filtered as `allMethodNames` is), each case the name's ordinary dispatcher, then the same `method_missing` / `respond_to_missing?` fallbacks and `NoMethodError`. Only those names are noted (`noteDyn`/`noteRespond`), so the every-name set (`dynAll`) is not raised by such a site; the dispatcher's `how` keeps `public_send` off private methods. The receiver stays `any` in the Go signature: the static type narrows the names, and a subclass held behind the parent type still reaches its own methods through the dispatcher. Not scoped (the every-name switch stays): an untyped, `T?` (nil answers only Kernel's names) or universal receiver, and a union holding one of those. *Amended the same day:* a class object (`Formula.public_send(m)`: the metaclass and its subclasses' metaclasses), a module or `Self` in a module body (the module and everything that includes it, `moduleIncluders`) and a `@go_type` class (`"abc".send(m)`, `Locale | String`) scope too; they were 20 of brew's 22 every-name sites. The warning for a site that keeps the every-name switch now says `on <type>` and, under `RB2GO_UNTYPED_WHY`, the root.
    - **Why:** the user's 2026-10-19 research (memory `untyped-sources-2026-10-19`): across the 121 examples, 90% of dispatchers and 53% of `rbAs` casts come from two computed sends; brew has 145 computed sends on receivers of known class against ~10 untyped ones (decision 235). Decision 235 measured that scoping by receiver "does not help" because one untyped site flips `dynAll`, and that still holds: this is the half that makes typing those remaining sites pay off, and what a prelude param inferred from its callers (the Observable `observer` follow-up) lands on.
    - **Measured:** the two example sends are on `Array[untyped]` elements and an `untyped` prelude parameter, so they are unchanged by design; TestCompileCost 05_word_count 1,339, 64_observable 27,087, 116_cuba_web 28,239 → 28,245 compiled functions (rack's typed computed sends now carry their own switch).
    - **Measured on brew** (`info wget`, macOS gen, 2026-10-19): 22 every-name sites → 2 (`install_steps.rb` `context_value`/`context_config_value`: `@context` declared `Object`, `config` returned `T.nilable(Object)`), 14 → 23 scoped switches. Counts unchanged (63,572 → 64,091 functions, 3,867 → 3,991 dispatchers, 12,105 → 12,172 wrappers, 7,842 → 7,860 casts) because those two sites, and `SimpleDelegator#respond_to_missing?` (`__getobj__.respond_to?(name)` on the prelude's untyped object, reached from every `rbResponds*` fallback), still keep `rbSendByName`/`rbRespondsByName`, which decision 235 narrows but keeps whole. The payoff needs all three gone: brew patch 0024 types `context` as `T.any(Formula, Cask::Cask)` and `config` as `Cask::Config?`; decision 319 takes the delegator's `respond_to?` off the dispatcher path. With both: 63,572 → 59,859 functions, 3,867 → 2,565 dispatchers, 12,105 → 10,342 wrappers, 7,842 → 6,366 casts, no `rbSendByName` call left.
    - **Proof:** `dynamic_test.rb` `SendOnTypedReceiverTest` (private via `send`, `public_send` refused, `NoMethodError` for an unknown name, a subclass's own method and `method_missing` behind the parent type, `respond_to?` with and without `true`, a union receiver); `TestErrors/dynamic` keeps the computed-send warning.

317. **A module's instance variables are discovered from its own methods; a prelude parameter may take its type from the program's calls** (amends 147, 183; 2026-10-19). Decision 147 made an undeclared module ivar a compile error because module methods were not dry-run for discovery. They are now (`discoverIvars` takes non-generic modules; `readsUnassigned` and `ivar` treat a module in discovery as a class: a read before any write is nil and the write that follows declares the ivar `T?`), so `# @rbs @x: T` in a module body is optional, as in a class; a module ivar only ever read still needs the annotation (`objects.txtar` `module_ivar_untyped`). With it, a prelude def can leave a parameter to decision 146 inference (the per-parameter `# @rbs` form with that parameter omitted): `Observable#add_observer(observer, fn = :update)` types `observer` as the join of what the program's calls pass (`Display | Recorder` in example 64), the `{ observer => fn }` literal types `@observer_peers`, and `notify_observers`' `observer.send(fn, *args)` is a decision 316 switch over those classes' names. Its `%x{ rbSendByName(...) }` body is gone. A prelude parameter no call types is untyped with decision 183's warning, as a user def's is, so this form belongs only where every caller is user code.
    - **Why:** the 2026-10-19 research (memory `untyped-sources-2026-10-19`): one `untyped` prelude parameter put 741 dispatchers and 2,487 Dyn wrappers into a 40-line program. Typing from use is what the closed world is for (decision 146); the prelude was the one place it did not reach. Rejected: a new marker type in prelude sigs (the unannotated form already means "from use"), and keeping `untyped` with a per-call cast (the Hash's key type is the observer's).
    - **Measured:** example 64 (`gen`): functions 10,693 → 3,063, `rbDyn` dispatchers 741 → 61, `rbAs` casts 1,325 → 488; TestCompileCost 64_observable 27,087 → 7,209 compiled functions, 5.8 → 1.4 s wall, 1,128 → 350 MB RSS (05_word_count and 116_cuba_web unchanged).
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `ModuleIvarTest#test_discovered` (an undeclared counter, an Array built on first use, a nil-until-written read), example 64 against MRI, `objects.txtar` `module_ivar_untyped` (read-only stays an error).

318. **`RB2GO_UNTYPED_WHY` traces through block parameters, multiple assignment and `||=`** (amends 283; 2026-10-19). The tracer named any untyped local that was not a plain `x = v` "local set by a non-plain write", which the 2026-10-19 research read as a compiler gap (17 of cuba's roots). Traced through, every one was a gem parameter no typed call reaches, an untyped ivar (`@app`, a Rack app by duck type), or `inject`'s memo from such a value: `blockParamWhy` names the block's call (an untyped receiver's root, `inject`'s untyped memo, or the yielding method), `noteAnyTargets` a multiple assignment's untyped value, and a first `x ||= v` its value. Debug-only (`fctx.blockWhy` is set only under the flag). The lever that label suggested, flow typing of such writes, has no cases to fix; what remains in gem programs is gem signatures (decision 160, `sig/gems`) and trace typing (memory `minimize-untyped`).

319. **A computed `respond_to?` on an untyped value emits every `rbResponds*` marker check, not every dispatcher** (amends 32, 235; 2026-10-19). `rbRespondsByName` (a computed name on an untyped receiver) was tied to `rbSendByName` by one flag, `dynEvery`: reaching either emitted every noted dispatcher, every Dyn wrapper on every class and every forwarder up front. Answering `respond_to?` needs none of that: `rbResponds<Name>` is a class-ID switch (`rbHas_Responds<Name>`) plus the `respond_to_missing?` fallback. The prelude's `SimpleDelegator#respond_to_missing?` (`__getobj__.respond_to?(name)`, untyped by decision 118) is reached from every `rbResponds*` fallback, so any program with a delegator and one `respond_to?` dispatcher paid for every dispatcher. Now `respondEvery` (set when `rbRespondsByName` is reached, or by the eager retry together with `dynEvery`) emits every `rbResponds*`, and dispatchers stay lazy (`dynNeeded`). `tableInputs` carries the flag so a late flip recompiles eagerly, as `dynEvery`'s does.
    - **Why:** the brew measurement under decision 316: after patch 0024 typed the last two computed sends on untyped receivers, the delegator's `respond_to?` alone kept 3,867 dispatchers and 12,105 wrappers. Of brew's five SimpleDelegator subclasses only two have a typed object (decision 284), so a per-subclass `respond_to_missing?` would not have cleared it.
    - **Measured:** brew `info wget` (macOS gen, with patch 0024): 63,572 → 59,859 functions, dispatchers 3,867 → 2,565, Dyn wrappers 12,105 → 10,342, `rbAs` 7,842 → 6,366; `rbSendByName` call sites 117 → 0, `rbRespondsByName` 6 → 2 (the delegator's and `install_steps`' `respond_to?` guard on the typed union, which the scoped form answers). What remains is dynamic calls on genuinely untyped receivers, each noting its own name.
    - **Proof:** `dynamic_test.rb` `test_computed_respond_to_on_untyped` (public, inherited, missing, `respond_to_missing?`, private with and without `true`); TestCompileCost 05_word_count 1,339 and 64_observable 7,209 unchanged, 116_cuba_web 28,245 → 28,286 compiled functions (its computed `respond_to?` now answers over every `rbResponds*`).

320. **A delegation through a nilable accessor is typed** (amends 99, 118; 2026-10-19). `def_delegators :@dsl, ...` with `@dsl` typed `DSL?` (Homebrew's `Cask::Cask`: `@dsl = T.let(DSL.new(self), T.nilable(DSL))`, 29 delegated names from `DSL_METHODS` that boot read, decision 204) was "cannot delegate to @dsl of type DSL?" in user code and, in gem code, an untyped forwarder: every `cask.version`, `url`, `depends_on`, `staged_path` call and everything built on their results dispatched at run time (130 of brew's 2,113 dynamic-call roots). The forwarder now reads the accessor into `target`, raises `NoMethodError` ("undefined method 'x' for nil") when it is nil, as MRI's forwarder does calling the method on nil, and makes the typed call (`delegation.nilable`, `nilGuard`); the same for the keyword `(...)` form.
    - The accessor's annotation may sit in any method, not only `initialize` (`delegateTarget`: Cask's `@dsl` is set in `refresh`); the first annotated write wins.
    - Typing those delegators exposed `SoftwareSpec#owner=`: `@name = owner.name` with `owner: Formula | Cask::Cask` is `Array[String] | String` into `@name: String?`, which was a decision 205 stub and crashed `info wget` (MRI stores whatever comes; a Cask's `name` is its names Array). In gem code a union value that does not fit the ivar now converts where it lands, as an untyped one does (decision 225): the fitting member is stored, another raises `TypeError` at run time, where the untyped path raised the same. User code keeps the compile error.
    - **Measured:** brew `info wget`: dynamic-call warnings 2,128 → 2,002, stubs 141 → 140, functions 59,859 → 59,851; output equals MRI. The 40 DSL roots left are delegated names whose `DSL` method has no return type.
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `ForwardableTest#test_nilable_accessor`; `testdata/boot` `DemoOwnerSpec` (Sorbet `T.let` nilable accessor set outside `initialize`, `NoMethodError` on nil, the union owner's `name` into `@name`).

321. **A gem method's `U | String` return is `String` at a call without the block that binds `U`; a `type_member`'s upper bound types its reads** (amends 150, 172, 196; 2026-10-19). Sorbet's block-or-value idiom, `.returns(T.any(T.type_parameter(:U), String))` with `U` bound only by an optional block (Homebrew's `Utils.popen_read`, 78 of brew's dynamic-call roots), resolved to untyped as a whole: decision 150 admits no type variable in a union, and decision 172 makes that untyped in gem code. The declaration stays untyped (its body and Go shape are unchanged; a first attempt that kept the variable in the union leaked `U` into the union conversion helper, a free function), but the method remembers the members (`Method.retUnion`, `varUnion`), and a call that passes no block drops the members only the block could bind (`dropVars` over the block signature's free variables) and converts the untyped result where it lands, as decision 225 does: `popen_read(cmd).chomp` is a `String` call. A call with a block keeps the declared untyped result. `type_member { { upper: T } }` resolves to `T` as `fixed:` does (Homebrew's `Cacheable`: `cache.key?` is a typed Hash call; the elements stay untyped by its bound).
    - **Measured:** brew `info wget`: dynamic-call warnings 2,002 → 1,795 (`popen_read` 78 → 0, `Cacheable#cache` 86 → 0), stubs 140 → 139, functions 59,850; output equals MRI.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` `DemoRead` (with and without the block) and `DemoCacheable`/`DemoCached`.

322. **An annotated class-body ivar write declares the class object's ivar** (amends 99's `# @rbs self.@x: T`; 2026-10-19). `@refs = {} #: Hash[Symbol, Integer]` (rbs-inline) or `@refs = T.let({}, T::Hash[Symbol, TypeReference])` (Sorbet, Homebrew's `CacheStore`) directly in a class body was a load-time statement whose ivar only `# @rbs self.@refs: T` could type: user code got "has no known type", gem code an untyped class-object ivar and dynamic calls on everything read from it (`cache_store.rb`: 10 roots). `collectBody` now records such a write as the `self.@x` declaration (`classBodyIvar`); the statement still runs at load time as before. An unannotated write stays a plain statement.
    - **Measured:** brew `info wget`: dynamic-call warnings 1,795 → 1,769 (ivar roots 66 → 50), functions 59,850 → 59,836; output equals MRI.
    - **Proof:** `object_test.rb` `BodyRefsTest`; `testdata/boot` `DemoRefs` (Sorbet form).

323. **Compiled `brew install wget` on macOS equals MRI's** (#100; amends 172, 193, 205, 301; 2026-10-19). `overrides/brew/macgen build` then `macrun`: through a symlink `/opt/homebrew/bin/brew-rb2go` (bin/brew takes the prefix from the link's directory and the repository from its target, so the patched copy pours into the real Cellar, whose bottles are pinned to `/opt/homebrew`), stdout, stderr and the exit status each equal MRI's for the same state; only the interleaving of the two streams differs (MRI's download threads). Gem-code shapes on the way: *See also decision 325* (this run never parsed a Mach-O file).
    - an `attr_writer`-only ivar is `T?` (it holds nil until the writer runs: `@sandbox_inheritance = T.let(nil, ...)`);
    - a gem's non-empty Hash literal local widens with later `h[k] = v` writes, as decision 193 does for Arrays (`widenHashSeed`);
    - an empty literal branch joins the other branch's container type in gem code (`untypedContainerJoin`);
    - a super bridge into a parent of another shape passes the parent's missing optional parameters as zero values and drops a value the override's type cannot hold (`reshapedBridge`, ObserverPathnameExtension#mkpath over Pathname#mkpath(mode:));
    - `|_, (token, _)|` binds nothing for the blanks (Go's `_ := x` did not compile);
    - a block call on a value held as Go `any` (a Sorbet `T::Enumerable[X]`) goes through the view, and a concrete value converts at run time where a method type parameter is wanted.
    - Prelude: `Process.spawn` reads a trailing options Hash, Integer keys as descriptors (`ExtraFiles`), and redirects a `File`; `Pathname#find` runs its block inside `catch(:prune)` so `Find.prune` leaves the block (it is a closure, not a loop body); `File.fnmatch` (MRI's `fnmatch_helper`: PATHNAME, DOTMATCH, CASEFOLD, NOESCAPE, EXTGLOB) and `File::FNM_*`; `File.absolute_path`, `File.ctime`, `File.world_writable?`; `FileUtils` `rm_r`, `mkdir`, `rmdir`, `mkpath`, symbolic `chmod`/`chmod_R`, `chown`/`chown_R`, `ln`, `install`, `compare_file`/`identical?`/`cmp`, `uptodate?`, `remove_entry`, `pwd`; `Pathname#chown`, `fnmatch?`, `ctime`, `each_child`, `descend`, `world_writable?`, glob with a block; `Tempfile.new(name, dir)`/`Tempfile.open`, `binmode`; `Etc.getgrnam`/`getgrgid`; `Thread#report_on_exception`; `Array#sort!` with a block; `JSON.pretty_generate(obj, nil)`; `Errno::ELOOP`.
    - Brew patches 0025-0028: exec3's options as spawn's trailing Hash, `RUBY_PATH` from `HOMEBREW_RUBY_PATH` (the compiled program is not a Ruby), and Pathname subclasses for `ObserverPathnameExtension`, `InstallRenamed` and `MachOShim` instead of per-object `extend`.
    - Third-party tap formulae installed from source (`.brew/<name>.rb`) cannot be read by a compiled brew (decision 301's string eval): `Upgrade.dependants` skips them as unreadable. The Linux container still stops at ELF relocation (bindata, decision 301).
    - Proof: `stdlib_test.rb` (`test_fnmatch`, `test_pathname_find_prune`, `test_pathname_walk_helpers`, `test_tempfile_open_in_dir`, `test_report_on_exception_false`), `fileutils_test.rb` (`test_install_path_methods`), `process_test.rb` (`test_spawn_trailing_options_with_fd`), `control_test.rb` (`test_blank_params_in_nested_tuple`), `array_test.rb` (`test_sort_bang_with_block`), `json_test.rb`, `testdata/boot` (`spawn_opts`, `observed_mkpath`).

324. **Run-time Ruby that bindata, elftools and patchelf need, compiled ahead of time** (#100; amends 35, 136, 172, 198, 204, 230, 301; 2026-10-19). *See also decision 326* (Fiddle calls work; the in-method LoadError is gone). Linux `brew install` relocates bottles with patchelf, which reads and writes ELF through bindata's DSL. The gems run unmodified except one gem patch (below); every construct is compiled at build time, with no run-time method table or eval (decisions 198, 204). *See also decision 325*.
    - **Per-object singleton methods.** A gem's receiverless `define_singleton_method(name_expr) { |params| body }` with a computed name (bindata's `Struct#define_field_accessors_for(name, index)`) compiles the block once as `__rb2go_sm_N(captures..., params)`; the call records `[N, name.to_s, captures...]` on the object (`@__rb2go_sm`, a new Array per record so a `clone` shares nothing it later changes), and the class gets `method_missing`/`respond_to_missing?` that find the latest record by name. A literal name on any receiver (`parser.define_singleton_method(:parse_and_append_field)`, bindata's `:big_and_little`) adds the same record and a check at the top of each class's own def of that name. Class objects keep decision 198's boot defs.
    - **Per-object `extend M`.** A gem instance method's `extend M` sets `@__rb2go_ext_K`; each def of M is copied into the class whose def an object would otherwise run, its `super` calling a renamed copy of that original, and the original def starts with the flag check (bindata's `Choice` `CopyOnChangePlugin`, `BasePrimitive`'s value plugins).
    - **instance_eval of a dynamic block.** A class-body DSL block passed to `method_missing`, to a dynamic call, or to a gem method's untyped `&block` when it does not compile on its lexical self, takes its self at call time: `instance_eval(&blk)`/`instance_exec` of an untyped block pass `rbSelfArg{obj}` first (bindata's `struct :e_ident do ... end`).
    - **String evals that define classes** (`BinData::Int.define_class`): the call site switches on the built source over what boot recorded (`genEvalSwitch`); a site whose sources only define methods stays a no-op. Their classes exist only once the eval ran (`rbEvalConst`, `const_missing` hook).
    - **64 bits.** In gem code a run-time `Integer#<<` past 64 bits wraps (Go's shift, mod 2**64), `unpack` of an unsigned value past 2**63 keeps its 64 bits, and a boot eval's source has each decimal literal past int64 rewritten mod 2**64 (`wrapBigLiterals`), so `"val &= #{(1 << nbits) - 1}"` equals what boot recorded and means Go's 64-bit arithmetic. Literal-only arithmetic (`(1 << 64) - 1`) folds exactly and saturates to the Integer range (`clamp(0, max)` stays valid). **Differs from MRI:** these values print as their int64 bits, not Bignums (decision 35).
    - **Binary Strings.** A String that is not valid UTF-8 is ASCII-8BIT (decision 136), so its `length`, `[]`, `[i, n]`, `[range]`, `ljust`/`rjust` count bytes; they counted runes, turning invalid bytes into U+FFFD (patchelf's section data).
    - **Strings stay values** (decision 136). A gem's `s[range] = v` / `s[i, n] = v` on a local rebinds it, as `<<` does; an untyped local is checked at run time and a non-String takes `[]=` as before. An edit through an alias (patchelf's `new_dynstr = replace_section(...)`, also held in `@replaced_sections`) is lost, so `overrides/brew/gem-patches/patchelf-1.7.0.patch` writes the edited String back (two lines; no change under MRI); `in-container.sh` applies `gem-patches/<gem>-<version>.patch` to the vendored gem.
    - **Typing.** An untyped argument to an overridden gem method or a gem `initialize` makes the parameter untyped (`paramUse.widen`; `obj.assign(value)` on a `Base`, `RawIO.new(io)` of a File); a gem `super`'s arguments type none of the target's parameters. A gem module's ivar its includer's class assigns is read and written by name (elftools' `Dynamic` reading `Segment`'s `@offset_from_vma`). `instance_variable_set("@tl_#{sym}")` declares one untyped ivar per literal its self-callers pass. A gem's `@x ||= v` on a non-nil-typed object ivar checks for nil. `klass.new(*args, **kw)` over subclasses whose `new` take other keywords dispatches at run time. Gem sigs `sig/gems/bindata`, `sig/gems/patchelf`.
    - **Smaller shapes.** Untyped `each` with 3+ block parameters splats; a blockless untyped `each` is an Enumerator; untyped `zip`; `send(*args, &blk)`; `refine X do ... end` reopens X and `using` is a no-op (patchelf's StringIO#fill); a gem module's own `loop do ... end`; `to_enum(__method__, ...)` in a private method; Integer arithmetic with an object answering `coerce`, and implicit `to_int`/`to_str` of an object through its `method_missing` (`rbImplicitConv`); a dynamic Hash lookup with a key of another class misses; a `method_missing` that `class_eval`s a String def to memoize is left to answer each time; `Range` converts between instantiations. Prelude: `StringIO#binmode`, `File#pos=`, `Object#clone` (frozen kept), `Module#autoload?` (nil), `ObjectSpace.define_finalizer` (records nothing; ponytail: run at exit), `Integer#step` without a limit (`__step_0`, which an iterator call with no arguments now picks, decision 12), `Array.new(n)`, `Errno::ESPIPE`.
    - Go-rejection stubs (decision 205) are written after the prune, so a gem compile keeps `NewNotImplementedError`.
    - **Proof:** `testdata/boot` `DemoObjDyn` (computed and literal singleton methods, `extend` with `super`, a class-body DSL block instance_eval'd, computed ivar names, `||=`, 64-bit masks, String edits and binary slicing, `coerce`/`to_int` through `method_missing`, `refine`).
    - **Linux `brew install wget` equals MRI's** in the container (`TARGETS=/w/install-targets.txt ./gen && ./build && TARGETS=/w/install-targets.txt ./check`): the same 79 lines and exit 0, apart from two differences. MRI's "Landlock ABI 10 or later is required…" warning is "Sandbox unavailable: processing downloaded files without sandboxing!" here, and the ✔︎ lines of the parallel downloads come in another order. `check` now keeps both outputs (`work/mri-*.txt`, `work/rb-*.txt`) for an order-free comparison.
    - **No Landlock sandbox.** brew probes Landlock through Fiddle's `syscall(2)` and runs extraction and relocation in a forked child that applies it; without cgo nothing can be called through Fiddle, and a Go program cannot fork and keep running Go code. A `require "fiddle"` inside a method raises LoadError, which brew takes as `:missing_fiddle` and runs unsandboxed (Fiddle's constants still load for top-level users). Fiddle over purego (#32) plus a fork-free sandbox child is the follow-up.
    - **What the sandbox child had run now runs in the brew process**, so a traced compile also takes each loaded gem file's literal `require` of the same gem's file inside a `def` (patchelf's `require 'patchelf/alt_saver'` in Patcher#save), and everything such an added file requires (`addGemOwnRequires`). Engine-specific load-time requires (concurrent-ruby's TruffleRuby backend) stay out.
    - **Gem patches:** `bindata-3.0.0.patch` counts read bytes with `bytesize` (MRI's `read(n)` is ASCII-8BIT; a short binary read that happens to be valid UTF-8 counted characters here and raised "data truncated"). Brew patch 0029: the download queue printed a download that finished between its two passes twice when not on a TTY; MRI's GVL hid the race, goroutines in parallel did not.
    - **More shapes:** a block's `return false` in a gem method carries the method's Boolean, not Go's untyped `false` (`Utils::Output.claim_warning`); a binary Regexp (`/n`, now kept in its options and `inspect`) matches a String that is not valid UTF-8 byte by byte, Latin-1 decoded so `/\xFD7zXZ\x00/n` matches the byte 0xFD (`UnpackStrategy::Xz.can_extract?`), offsets mapped back; any other Regexp still raises MRI's ArgumentError on it; `"#{self}"` whose `to_s` dispatches at run time is a String; `::Regexp.last_match(n)`; `Regexp.escape(Symbol)`; `FileUtils.mv`/`rm` options; `File.readlines(path, chomp:)`; `n.times do ... end` as a value is an Integer; Integer folding applies only to the arithmetic call itself (case's `when 0` handed its `===` the literal and lost the subject); an `if` whose branches are a typed and an untyped Array literal takes the typed one only when it is more specific (`cond ? [x.soname, x.needed] : [nil, []]`); a gem's `[]=` on an untyped local is a String edit only for an Integer or Range index and a String value; `Array#include?` of a value of another class compares `elem == obj` (`[SHT_SYMTAB, SHT_DYNSYM].include?(shdr.sh_type)` of a bindata Uint32, which answers through its own `==`), and `rbEq` honours a gem's `==` returning untyped; a gem module's ivar is read by name only when every including class assigns it.
    - **Differs from MRI:** patchelf sorts sections and program headers with `sort!` on offsets that tie (`.bss`/`.comment`, `PT_TLS`); MRI's unstable quicksort and Go's sort order ties differently, so a relocated file is a valid ELF (the same program headers, dynamic section and symbols, each symbol's section index consistent with its own table) but not always byte-identical to MRI's: 20 of the 151 ELF files in wget's 12 bottles are.
    - `RB2GO_GO_STACK=1` (via `HOMEBREW_RB2GO_GO_STACK` under brew) adds the Go stack to a Go runtime panic turned into a Ruby exception.

325. **macOS `brew install wget` recheck after 324: Mach-O is read for real** (#100; amends 12, 198, 323, 324; 2026-10-20). *See also decision 326* (patch 0030's fallback). Rerunning `macgen build`/`macrun` after decision 324 printed "Failed to read Mach-O binary" twice. The cause was older than 324: decision 323's run never parsed a Mach-O file. ruby-macho failed at once with an error brew swallows, so linkage checking saw no dylibs. Output still matched MRI because wget's arm64 bottles already carry `/opt/homebrew` install names, so relocation has nothing to change. Getting the parse to work exposed three gaps; with them fixed, stdout, stderr and exit status each equal MRI's again, and the installed wget runs, links and passes `codesign -v`.
    - **A local bound once to a literal names a computed ivar.** ruby-macho's field readers are `define_method(name) { instance_variable_defined?("@#{name}") || instance_variable_set("@#{name}", ...); instance_variable_get("@#{name}") }`. Decision 198 bakes the captured `name` into the def as `name = :cpusubtype`, but no ivar `@cpusubtype` was declared, so the set raised NameError. `declareComputedIvars` (324) now also takes a local the body writes exactly once with a Symbol or String literal (`literalLocals`).
    - **A class-picked overload converts generic arguments.** An untyped call's twin chosen by its first argument's class (decision 12; `dynClassOverloads`, `dynArityOverloads`) tested `args[0].(*Range[Integer])`. A gem's `start..stop` of untyped bounds is a `*Range[any]` and missed the test. A generic instantiation is now tested with `rbConv` (`dynClassTest`), so Integer bounds convert as they would for a typed parameter.
    - **`String#slice(range)` on an untyped receiver.** The dynamic `slice` had no Range form. Its overload search (`__slice_` prefix) instead found `__slice_rest`, decision 233's helper for what `slice!` leaves behind, and returned the remainder (`"abcdef".slice(3..4)` gave `"abcf"`). The helper is renamed `__rest_of_slice`, and `__slice_range` is the real overload. Helper names must not start with a public method's `__<name>_` prefix.
    - **No Fiddle function calls (brew patch 0030).** On macOS a system dylib exists only in dyld's shared cache, so `LinkageChecker#dylib_found_in_shared_cache?` calls `_dyld_shared_cache_contains_path` through Fiddle, which raises `Fiddle::DLError` in a compiled program (324). The patch rescues it and treats `/usr/lib/` and `/System/Library/` paths as cached. That gives the same answer for every bottle that links only system libraries from those directories. The fallback is the same kind as 324's Landlock one: #32 (Fiddle over purego) would remove both.

326. **Fiddle over purego** (#32; amends 59, 242, 296, 324, 325; 2026-10-20). *See also decision 327* (sqlite3 on top of it). Fiddle, Ruby's way to call C shared libraries without a C extension, works without cgo. `github.com/ebitengine/purego` (v0.11.2, Apache-2.0, no dependencies) does libffi's job in Go and assembly. It is the second runtime module after regexp2, wired the same way (242): every generated `go.mod` requires it (`rb2go.GoMod`/`GoSum`, `TestGoModMatchesRepo`) and Go loads it only when `main.go` imports it, which happens only when the pruner keeps a Fiddle helper (`prelude/go/fiddle.go`). The generated module's `go` directive is now `1.25.0`, purego's minimum. *See also decision 82*: no `reflect` is involved.
    - **Calls without reflect.** Fiddle's types are run-time values, and Homebrew passes them through parameters (`NativeLibrary#function(name, argument_types, return_type)`), so `Function.new` cannot be typed from literals. Instead every C function is bound to one Go signature, `func(8 × uintptr, 8 × float64) uintptr` (or `float64` for a float/double return). On the System V (amd64) and AAPCS64 (arm64, Apple's too) ABIs, integer and floating arguments fill their own register files independently, so this superset passes any register-passed signature: the callee reads the registers it declares and ignores the rest. A float sits in the low half of its register. Narrow results (char, short, int, bool) are truncated and sign- or zero-extended in Go, since the ABI leaves the upper bits unspecified. More than 8 integer or 8 floating arguments (stack-passed, laid out differently per platform) and `TYPE_VARIADIC` (C varargs, which purego cannot make) raise `NotImplementedError`. `Fiddle.last_error` is errno, read on the calling OS thread (`runtime.LockOSThread`) right after the call.
    - **Ruby on top.** `prelude/fiddle.rb` is the C half: `Handle` (dlopen; `nil` is `RTLD_DEFAULT`), `Function`, `Pointer` (libc `malloc`/`free`, byte and range `[]`/`[]=`, `to_s`/`to_str`, `ptr`/`ref`, arithmetic, `free=`/`call_free`), `Closure`/`Closure::BlockCaller` (purego callbacks, the same superset signature; at most 2000 per process, never freed, no float return), `CStructEntity`, `CStruct`, `CUnion`, the `TYPE_*`/`SIZEOF_*`/`ALIGN_*` constants, `Fiddle.malloc`/`realloc`/`free`/`dlopen` (with MRI's Linux linker-script fallback). `prelude/fiddle_import.rb` (`fiddle/import`, `fiddle/cparser`) ports MRI's `CParser` and `Importer` (`dlload`, `typealias`, `sizeof`, `extern`, `bind`, `[]`, `import_symbol`, `import_function`, `bind_function`) and `CompositeHandler`.
    - **extern/bind are compiled.** MRI's `extern "int abs(int)"` `module_eval`s a method. A literal signature in a module that extends `Fiddle::Importer` is read at compile time (`internal/compiler/fiddle.go`, cparser's patterns, typealiases seen so far) and a `def self.abs(a0)` is synthesized that calls the registered `Function`, typed by the C return type (`Integer`, `Float`, `bool`, `Fiddle::Pointer`, `nil`); the `extern` call still runs at load to look the symbol up. A non-literal signature, a variadic one, and an unknown type are compile errors. Only the module method is made (MRI's `module_function` also makes a private instance method).
    - **struct/union are compiled.** `Name = struct ["int id", "char tag[4]", ...]` in an Importer module declares `class Name < Fiddle::CStruct` (`CUnion` for `union`) with the LP64 layout CStructBuilder computes (offsets, `size`, `alignment`, `members`, `types`, `offsetof`, `malloc`) and typed accessors per member: integers `Integer` (a `bool` member too, as MRI reads it through pack), floats `Float`, pointers `Fiddle::Pointer`, fixed arrays `Array[...]` (a fresh Array, as MRI's for signed element types). `[]`/`[]=` by name, `to_h`, `each`, `each_pair`, `to_ptr` (a `CStructEntity`), `to_i`. Nested structs and non-literal member lists are compile errors. **Differs from MRI:** `CUnion` is a subclass of `CStruct` here (MRI's is not).
    - **Strings passed as pointers** are copied into C memory, since rb2go Strings are immutable values (decision 136): C's writes to the copy do not reach the String, and `Pointer[str]` is a copy. The copies are freed after the call unless the function returns `void*`, which may point into one (`strchr`); those leak (ponytail).
    - **Overrides need a signature.** A `Fiddle::Closure` subclass's `call` overrides `(*untyped) -> untyped`; written without a `#:` its result is not known when the override is checked, so it is a compile error until annotated.
    - **No more LoadError.** Decision 324's in-method `require "fiddle"` raised LoadError so Homebrew took Landlock as `:missing_fiddle`; that special case is gone, and `Fiddle.dlopen` no longer raises (296).
    - **Linux `dlopen(nil)` also loads libm** (`RTLD_GLOBAL`): MRI's process links it, a binary without cgo links only libc, so `pow` was missing.
    - **`respond_to?(:fork)` is false** (`notImplemented` in `expr_untyped.go`), as MRI answers for a method it defines with `rb_f_notimplement` on a platform without it; `fork` itself still raises NotImplementedError. **Differs from MRI** on Linux and macOS, where MRI forks. `Errno::<NAME>::Errno` constants exist now, with `Errno::ENOSYS` and `Errno::EOPNOTSUPP`.
    - **Homebrew backfill.** macOS `brew install wget` without brew patch 0030 (the shared-cache fallback, deleted): `LinkageChecker#dylib_found_in_shared_cache?` calls `_dyld_shared_cache_contains_path` through `NativeLibrary#function`, and stdout, stderr and exit status each equal MRI's. On Linux, Fiddle made Landlock's probe work, so compiled brew took the sandboxed path and failed in `Utils.safe_fork` (fork is not implemented). New brew patch 0030 reports `:missing_fork` when `Process.respond_to?(:fork)` is false (a no-op under MRI), so the output is as in decision 324: the same lines as MRI's except "Sandbox unavailable" for MRI's Landlock ABI warning, and the parallel-download order differences are gone in this run. A fork-free Landlock child stays the follow-up: the restricted work is Ruby, and a Go program cannot fork and keep running it. Example 121 and `fiddle_test.rb`, cross-built for linux/arm64 with `CGO_ENABLED=0`, equal MRI and pass in the container.
    - **Proof:** example 121 (`examples/121_fiddle`: Importer externs, a struct, `Function.new`, a `qsort` callback, a Pointer C string, DLError), `testdata/test/fiddle_test.rb` (mixed int/double/float arguments, narrow returns, errno, Pointer bytes and arithmetic, struct and union layout and members through `memcpy`, a Closure subclass and a BlockCaller called from C and from `Function`), `testdata/errors/fiddle.txtar`; sig `sig/fiddle.rbs`.
327. **sqlite3 over Fiddle** (#32; amends 150, 155; 2026-10-20). `require "sqlite3"` loads `prelude/sqlite3.rb`, a port of the sqlite3-ruby gem's API (2.10.1), and never looks the gem up (the lib table in `libs.go`, decision 155). The gem is a C extension, so it cannot be compiled from source; this is the prelude-copy exception to preferring real gems. Its Ruby layer (`Database`, `Statement`, `ResultSet`/`HashResultSet`, the `Pragmas` accessors, `Exception#message` with the SQL and a caret, `Blob`, `String#to_blob`) is ported; its C extension is `SQLite3::C`: one `Fiddle::Function` per `sqlite3_*` entry point (decision 326), bound at require time from `libsqlite3.dylib` on macOS or `libsqlite3.so.0` elsewhere, with typed wrappers (`C.int`, `C.float`, `C.str`, `C.ptr`) over `call`'s untyped result. The compiler's `extern` synthesis skips prelude files, hence `Function.new` directly. SQL text and bound Strings go through Fiddle's temporary C copies with `SQLITE_TRANSIENT` (−1), so sqlite copies them again before the call returns; `sqlite3_prepare_v2`'s tail pointer is turned into `Statement#remainder` by its offset into the copy. Column and value reads are typed by `sqlite3_column_type`: INTEGER → Integer (int64), FLOAT → Float, TEXT and BLOB → the bytes by `column_bytes` (so embedded NULs survive), NULL → nil.
    - **Rows are `Array[untyped]`**, as `JSON.parse`'s result is untyped: `execute` returns `Array[untyped]` whose elements are Arrays, or Hashes under `results_as_hash`, so a typed caller indexes rows dynamically. An `Array[untyped] | Hash[String, untyped]` element type was tried and rejected: `row[0]` on it is a compile error for the Hash arm (decision 150's argument check), the common case. `Statement#step` and `#each` are typed `Array[untyped]`, `ResultSet#next_hash` is `Hash[String, untyped]?`.
    - **Binding.** `execute(sql, bind_vars)` takes one value, an Array (positional `?`), or a Hash (`:name`, with or without the colon, String or Symbol keys); Integer → `bind_int64`, Float → `bind_double`, String → `bind_text`, `Blob` → `bind_blob`, nil → `bind_null`, anything else `RuntimeError: can't prepare Class`, as the gem.
    - **Blob is a marker class, not a String subclass. Differs from MRI.** A `class Blob < String` compiled, but a `[name, Blob.new(data)]` literal is `Array[String]` (a subclass is absorbed by its superclass, decision 150), and a boxed String subclass unwraps to a plain String there, so the blob bound as text. As its own class the literal is `Array[String | Blob]` and the marker survives. It answers `to_s`, `to_str`, `bytesize`, `length`, `bytes`, `==` (against a Blob or a String), `hash`, `inspect`. Columns read back as plain Strings, as the gem's do. A blob String's `encoding` reports UTF-8 when its bytes happen to be valid UTF-8 (decision 136, #83); the tests compare bytes.
    - **Functions.** `create_function(name, arity) { |fn, a, b, ...| fn.result = ... }` and `define_function(name) { |*args| ... }` register a purego callback (`Closure::BlockCaller`, never freed) through `sqlite3_create_function` (eight arguments, the `_v2` form's ninth would exceed decision 326's register-passed limit). A block cannot be typed `(FunctionProxy, *untyped)` (a fixed parameter before a splat yields one value plus an Array), so `create_function`'s block is typed with `fn` plus six `untyped` arguments, padded with nil; a block declares fewer (ponytail: raise past six). A Ruby exception inside the callback cannot unwind through C frames: it is parked on the Database, `sqlite3_result_error` is set, and the `step` that ran the function raises the parked exception, so `assert_raises(RuntimeError)` sees the block's own error, as under the gem.
    - **Errors.** The result code's primary code picks the class (`SQLException`, `ConstraintException`, `ReadOnlyException`, `CantOpenException`, ...); `code`, `sql` (prepare errors only) and `sql_offset` (`sqlite3_error_offset`, when the library has it) are set as the gem sets them, and `message` appends the SQL with a caret under the offset, byte for byte MRI's text. Pragmas live on `Database` itself rather than in an included module: a module body's calls on `self` resolve dynamically and warned, and `table_info`'s `prepare` result had to be typed.
    - **Differs from MRI, besides Blob:** `prepare`, `query` and `execute` with a block return the Statement, ResultSet or an empty Array rather than the block's value (one typed return per method); `Database#encoding` is from `PRAGMA encoding`; the system SQLite is used, not the gem's bundled one, so `SQLITE_VERSION` and `libversion` differ from the gem's (3.51 vs 3.54 on this macOS); Absent, so a call is a compile-time undefined-method error: `create_aggregate`, `define_aggregator`, `authorizer`, `busy_handler`, `trace`, `collation`, `load_extension`, `serialize`/`deserialize`, `execute_batch2`, `Backup`, UTF-16 database names, the `strict:`/`extensions:` options. `Dir.tmpdir` was added to `tmpdir.rb` for the example.
    - **Proof:** example 122 (`examples/122_sqlite3`: binds by position, value and name, typed rows, blobs, `execute2`, `results_as_hash`, a reused prepared statement, a rolled-back transaction, `create_function`, `table_info`, a syntax error's message, a file database opened read-only), `testdata/test/sqlite3_test.rb` (142 assertions against the real gem: rows and types, every bind shape and rejection, `execute2`/`get_first_*`, `results_as_hash`, Statement and ResultSet stepping, error classes/codes/messages, transactions and `execute_batch`, functions including a raising one, pragmas and schema, a file database read-only/read-write); sig `sig/sqlite3.rbs` (the gem ships none).
